- **2026-08-29 (Phase 32): Plan-based AI model tiers, admin-editable model config, and a real résumé/cover-letter chat bug fix — all live-verified. Uncommitted.**
  - **Plan-based tier resolution**: free/Recon users now always get the "fast" model, admin/owner/tester/any llmUnlocked paid plan always gets "smart" — replacing the old per-feature hardcoded "fast"/"smart" literal at every one of ~45 `getModel()` call sites across ~35 files. New `lib/subscription.ts` `resolveModelForUser()` (returns `{provider, tier}`, one `getUserSubscription()` round-trip) replaces the narrower `resolveProviderForUser()` everywhere; a shared `hasFullModelAccess()` backs both it and `setPreferredModel()`'s write-gate so "who's allowed" and "what actually runs" can't drift apart — the same bug class Phase 30's resolveProvider fix closed. Deliberately left untouched: `lib/admin/*.ts`/`lib/adminAgentAssistant.ts`/`lib/push.ts`/`lib/publicAtsChecker.ts`/`lib/interviewQuestions.ts` — internal/admin tooling and a public unauthenticated checker, not plan-gated.
  - **Gemini's smart tier now points to a real Pro model** (`gemini-3.1-pro-preview`) instead of duplicating flash-lite. Live-verified via a real request that this model id is addressable on the project's key, but the free-tier key has **zero Pro quota** (`limit: 0` on `generate_content_free_tier_requests`) — confirmed real, not assumed; `agy` research confirms a Google Cloud Billing account must be linked to unlock it. No extra code needed for this to ship safely: `complete()`'s existing `GEMINI_FALLBACK_MODELS` retry chain already catches the resulting 429 on any Gemini call and falls through to a real flash-lite response, so behavior degrades to today's exact experience until billing is linked, then starts succeeding with zero further changes — confirmed live via server logs during testing (`429 → retry → exhausted → fallback → 200`).
  - **Admin-editable model config, no redeploy needed**: new `ai_model_config` table (migration `20260829130000`, `(provider, tier) -> model_id`, RLS enabled with zero client policies — service-role only, same pattern as `admin_users`/`ai_cost_rates`), read live on every `getModel()` call with the old hardcoded `MODEL_IDS` object kept permanently as the fallback if the read fails. New `/admin/ai-models` page + `AiModelsManager.tsx` (same collapsible-table-plus-ConfirmDialog pattern as `PlansManager.tsx`), `getModelConfigForAdmin()`/`updateModelConfig()` in `actions/admin.ts` (`requireRole(["owner"])`, audited). **A real bug caught during live verification**: the first version's audit-log call passed `targetId: "all"` — `admin_audit_log.target_id` is a `uuid` column, so this failed with `22P02` and silently dropped the log entry every time (`logAdminAction` is fire-and-forget by design, never throws) — the actual config write still succeeded, only the audit trail was silently missing. Fixed by omitting `targetId` (it's optional) rather than filling it with a fake value; re-verified live, the audit row now lands correctly.
  - **Full model access + site-wide selector, direct user request**: `hasFullModelAccess()` grants admin/owner, `profiles.is_tester`, and any `llmUnlocked` paid plan the same full provider+tier choice — additive to the existing paid-plan path, not a narrowing. New `components/shared/SiteModelSelector.tsx` (replaces the old dead, never-rendered `ModelSelector.tsx`, deleted) — a self-fetching client component (mirrors `GlobalSearchBar.tsx`'s pattern) mounted inline in `Navbar.tsx`'s icon row, not as a fixed-position floating pill (would have overlapped `NavigatorLauncher`'s FAB at `fixed bottom-6 right-6`). Renders nothing for an ineligible user. Later extended same-day, direct follow-up ("admin, testers and owners can also see the fast tier models as well in the picking list"): shows all 6 provider×tier combinations, not just each provider's smart model — new `profiles.preferred_tier` column (migration `20260829140000`, nullable, full-access-only; a non-full-access account's tier stays hard-forced to "fast" regardless of this column). Live-verified end to end: picked each of a fast and a smart combination in the real browser, confirmed both `preferred_model`/`preferred_tier` landed correctly in the DB, reverted after.
  - **Real résumé/cover-letter chat bug fixed, direct user report** ("it's not completely listening to user and it's not changing the template accordingly"): root-caused — `agent/documents.ts`'s `reviseTailoredResume()`/`reviseCoverLetter()` only ever saw/returned content (summary/bullets, or letter body), never `ResumeStyle` (template/theme/colors/spacing), so a request like "switch to a two-column template" had no field to land in. Both functions now receive `currentStyle` and can return an optional `styleChanges: Partial<ResumeStyle> | null` — non-null only when the candidate's latest message was actually about visual layout, `null` (untouched) otherwise. The system prompt also now tells the model to say so plainly when a request is genuinely out of scope (e.g. adding a photo) rather than silently agreeing without acting. `/api/documents/chat`'s cover-letter branch needed a real second fix beyond the shared résumé persist block: the existing `if (resumeSections && resumeStyle)` save guard is résumé-only (résumé sections + style together, plus a rescore) — a cover-letter-only style change needed its own `else if (kind === "cover_letter" && resumeStyle)` branch that writes just the shared `resume_style` column. **Live-verified end to end, both documents, not just code-reviewed**: asked the real résumé chat to "switch to the split template with a two-column layout" — got a real reply confirming it, and confirmed via direct DB query that `resume_style.template` actually changed from `structured` to `split` (not just claimed in the reply text, which was the essence of the original bug). Separately asked the cover-letter chat to "change the color theme to slate" — same confirmation, DB showed `theme` changed to `slate` while `template` stayed `split` (additive merge, not a stomp). Both reverted to original values after.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean across all ~64 touched/new files.

- **2026-08-29 (Phase 31): Security fix — closed a real, verified quota-reset bypass on `usage_daily`/`rate_limit`, found during a proactive RLS audit run via the `insforge-debug` skill. Applied live, uncommitted.**
  - **Real finding, not theoretical**: `insforge db policies` showed both tables let the row owner `UPDATE`/`INSERT` via RLS with no `WITH CHECK` on the value. `lib/usage.ts`'s `checkAndConsumeUsage()`, `lib/subscription.ts`'s job-evaluation quota check, and `lib/rateLimit.ts`'s `checkRateLimit()` all write these tables using the caller's own session client (not admin) — so any signed-in user could call the InsForge REST API directly with their own real JWT and `PATCH` their `count` back to 0, resetting their daily quota/rate-limit window on every metered AI action. Every action gated here has a real external cost (see `lib/usage.ts`'s own header comment) — a genuine cost/revenue exposure, confirmed via schema + a live exploit replication, not assumed.
  - **Fix**: migration `20260829120000_close-usage-quota-bypass.sql` adds two `SECURITY DEFINER` RPCs — `increment_usage_daily(p_action, p_limit)` and `bump_rate_limit(p_route, p_window_seconds, p_max)` — that resolve the caller from `auth.uid()` (never a client-supplied id) and do an atomic check-and-increment server-side (also closes the old read-then-write race `lib/rateLimit.ts`'s own comment had already flagged). The raw client-writable `usage_daily_insert_own`/`usage_daily_update_own`/`rate_limit_insert_own`/`rate_limit_update_own` policies are dropped — direct table writes are now rejected outright by RLS; only the RPCs (which bypass RLS as table owner) can write. All 3 call sites (`lib/usage.ts`, `lib/subscription.ts`'s `checkJobEvaluationLimit`, `lib/rateLimit.ts`) now call the RPC instead of raw insert/update.
  - **A real bug caught before it shipped**: the first version of `increment_usage_daily()` set `updated_at = now()` in its `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` — `usage_daily` has no such column (only `id`/`user_id`/`day`/`action`/`count`), so every call failed with `42703`. Since `lib/usage.ts` treats an RPC error as fail-closed, this would have blocked every metered AI action app-wide. Caught immediately via live verification (not assumed fixed) — confirmed the exact column list via `information_schema.columns`, fixed, re-applied, re-verified.
  - **A second write site found mid-fix, not in the original grep**: `lib/subscription.ts`'s job-evaluation daily-limit check had the identical unsafe two-step pattern against `usage_daily`, missed by the first pass. Found via a broader grep for `.update(`/`.insert(` immediately after `.from("usage_daily")`/`.from("rate_limit")` across the whole codebase — fixed the same way, same RPC.
  - **Live-verified end to end, real exploit replication**: signed in as the real test account via the public SDK client (own JWT, same as any real user would have), attempted the exact old exploit — direct `INSERT`/`UPDATE` on `usage_daily`/`rate_limit` — got RLS violation / zero rows affected on all 4 attempts (2 tables × insert+update). Called both RPCs 3× each with a limit of 2 — got `allow, allow, deny` correctly both times. All test rows deleted afterward, confirmed via a follow-up `SELECT` returning 0.
  - **Also confirmed safe, no changes needed**: every admin/internal table (`admin_users`, `admin_audit_log`, `ai_cost_rates`, `plan_seat_claims`, etc.) has RLS enabled with zero policies — default-deny, invisible to the anon/authenticated key. The `resumes` storage bucket is `public: true` but a live unauthenticated `GET /api/storage/buckets/resumes/objects` (listing) returned 401 — public only means "readable by exact known key," not an open directory. `support-attachments` is correctly private.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean across all 3 touched TS files.

- **2026-08-28 (Phase 30, cont'd): Country/region-aware pricing, Phase 1 (Stripe-only). Uncommitted, DB changes already live.**
  - **Scope decision, after real research**: ruled out a payment-provider migration or Razorpay wiring — InsForge's payments abstraction only supports Stripe/Razorpay (confirmed against the actual skill doc folders); Razorpay's standard accounts are India/Malaysia-only, and the cross-border "Import Stack" product needed for a US-based Sortie to accept Indian buyers requires a separate external application, not code. Stripe's own buyer-side reach is already near-global (research corrected an earlier, wrong finding that Stripe blocks Pakistan/Sri Lanka — it doesn't; the real friction is each country's own bank blocking international/recurring card charges). Scoped to Stripe-only regional pricing; Razorpay/local-rail payment methods deferred as an explicit Phase 2.
  - **Went through `EnterPlanMode`** given the real payments stakes — a Plan agent's design pass caught a real bug pre-emptively: `fulfill_stripe_subscription_event()` only matched invoices against the base `stripe_price_id` column, so a regional Stripe Price would have charged customers without ever granting the plan. Fixed in the same migration.
  - **Schema**: `regional_prices JSONB` on `subscription_plans`, same flat-override-map pattern `daily_action_limits` already established. Migration `20260828190000_add-regional-prices.sql`, applied live.
  - **New `lib/regionalPricing.ts`** (`COUNTRY_REGION_KEY` code constant — India its own INR region, Pakistan/Sri Lanka/Bangladesh share one discounted-USD band per direct user decision; `resolveStripePriceId`/`resolveDisplayPrice`/`formatPriceCents`) and **`lib/geo.ts`** (`getRequestCountry()` reads Vercel's `x-vercel-ip-country` directly — confirmed `proxy.ts` doesn't even run on `/pricing`/`/`, so zero middleware changes needed).
  - **`actions/billing.ts`**: `getPlansForPricing()` returns a `PricedPlan` (base fields + resolved `displayPriceCents`/`displayCurrency`); `createCheckoutSessionAction()` re-derives region server-side at charge time — client only ever sends `tier`, no way to manipulate a cheaper region. `CTASection.tsx`/`SubscriptionTab.tsx` render the resolved price; upgrade/downgrade classification in `SubscriptionTab.tsx` deliberately stays keyed on the base `priceCents` so a regional discount can't flip it.
  - **Admin UI**: `PlansManager.tsx` gained `RegionalPricingEditor`, same collapsible-rows pattern as the existing `DailyActionLimitsEditor` — price/currency/Stripe-Price-ID per region, ships blank; the product owner sets real numbers themselves, nothing hardcoded.
  - **Two real bugs caught during live verification, before shipping**: (1) the first `resolveStripePriceId()` fell back to the base US Price whenever a region had no Stripe Price ID yet — a genuine bait-and-switch (shows a discounted price, charges a different one). Fixed to block checkout instead. (2) The webhook fix's own `regional_prices` lookup checked the nested key as `stripe_price_id` (snake_case) but the real stored shape is `stripePriceId` (camelCase — `actions/admin.ts` passes it through with no transform); would have silently matched nothing. Both caught via real DB round-trip tests, fixed, re-verified.
  - **Live-verified end to end**: real test data on the `command` plan — `curl -H "x-vercel-ip-country: IN" /pricing` rendered ₹749 correctly; no-header and unmapped-country (`DE`) requests both rendered $15 unchanged; direct script confirmed checkout blocking and fallback resolution; the corrected webhook query re-tested against live data. All test data reverted, confirmed via a final `SELECT`.
  - **CLI gotcha for next time**: the InsForge CLI's `db query` breaks on certain multi-line SQL; minifying to one line fixes it — but strip ALL comment lines regardless of indentation (`grep -v '^\s*--'`) before minifying, not just top-level ones, or an indented multi-line comment silently swallows everything after it once newlines collapse to spaces.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean across every file touched.

- **2026-08-28 (Phase 30, cont'd): Two real bugs fixed on direct user request — `agent_runs.status` never flipping to `completed`, and the `resolveProvider()` 2-arg call sites (turned out to be a genuine, significant, revenue-affecting bug, not cosmetic debt). Uncommitted.**
  - **`agent_runs.status` stuck bug**: `evaluateJobsAsync`'s final status write (`lib/inngest/functions.ts`) was plain code after the step loop, not its own step — confirmed via a real stuck run (20/20 jobs scored, still `status='running'` 6 days later) and a DB sweep finding 78 total stuck rows since mid-July. Fixed by wrapping the write in its own `step.run()` + try/catch (durable, independently retried, never falsely fails an otherwise-successful batch). Added `reconcileStuckAgentRunsAsync`, a new 15-min Inngest cron that recomputes completion from the real `jobs` table as a self-healing backstop, registered in `app/api/inngest/route.ts`. Backfilled the historical rows immediately (5 fixed, confirmed zero remaining via a direct DB check).
  - **Compounding bug found along the way**: `app/dashboard/page.tsx`'s Recent Activity query selected/ordered/filtered on `agent_runs.completed_at` — a column that doesn't exist on the table (only `created_at`/`updated_at`/`started_at` do). PostgREST rejects a select on a nonexistent column, so this query has likely returned empty this entire time, silently hiding every "Found N jobs for X" entry. Fixed by switching to `updated_at` (already set at the exact moment status flips — no new column needed).
  - **`resolveProvider()` 2-arg debt**: `lib/access.ts`'s `resolveProvider(preferredModel, email, llmUnlocked?)` silently forces Gemini for every non-admin user when `llmUnlocked` is omitted. A full grep found **all 33 call sites** in the codebase used the 2-arg form — meaning every paying Command/Ace/Vanguard subscriber has been silently downgraded to Gemini across the entire AI surface of the app (résumé/cover-letter gen, bullet rewrites, job evaluation, career features, company research, weekly briefings) instead of the GPT-4o/Claude access their plan promises, since Phase 21 added the 3-arg signature without switching any callers over. Fixed centrally: new `resolveProviderForUser(insforge, userId, email, preferredModel)` in `lib/subscription.ts` (fetches the real plan via `getUserSubscription()`, then calls `resolveProvider()` correctly) — all 33 sites across `actions/`, `app/api/`, and `lib/inngest/functions.ts` (admin-client sites included, structurally compatible with no type gymnastics) now go through it. `extractProfileFromBuffer` (`actions/profile.ts`) needed a signature change (`insforge`/`userId` instead of just `userEmail`) since it's shared by two callers.
  - **Live-verified, not just code-reviewed**: a real script confirmed the exact bug (`resolveProvider('openai', <non-admin email>)` → `"gemini"`) and the exact fix (the real Command-tier test account, `preferred_model` temporarily set to `'openai'`, correctly resolves to `"openai"` for a non-admin email via the new helper) — reverted immediately after, confirmed via a follow-up query.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean across all ~24 touched files.

- **2026-08-28 (Phase 30): Global full-text job search + a persistent site-wide search bar + PWA metadata fix + 2 mobile-overflow fixes. All uncommitted on disk.**
  - **Global full-text search** — a trigger-maintained `search_vector` tsvector column (weighted A: title/company, B: location/job-type/tags/personal-notes, C: description/requirements/responsibilities/nice-to-have/benefits/private reflection) + GIN index + `search_jobs()` Postgres function (`ts_rank` ranking, `ts_headline` snippets) — migration `20260828180000_add-full-text-job-search.sql`. Two real Postgres/SDK gotchas hit and worked around: (1) `GENERATED ALWAYS AS STORED` rejected the tsvector expression ("generation expression is not immutable" — `to_tsvector(regconfig, text)` is STABLE not IMMUTABLE), switched to a `BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE` trigger instead; (2) the InsForge SDK's `.rpc()` call on this codebase's untyped client makes postgrest-js's `CheckMatchingArrayTypes` hard-reject a valid `.returns<T[]>()`/`.overrideTypes()` override, worked around with a plain type cast. New `actions/jobs.ts` `searchJobsFullText()`, `/search` page (`components/search/GlobalSearchView.tsx`), Cmd+K cross-link. Snippet rendering deliberately avoids `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` (scraped job postings aren't HTML-safe source text — string-splits on the `<mark>`/`</mark>` markers instead, letting React escape everything else).
  - **Persistent site-wide search bar** (`components/layout/GlobalSearchBar.tsx`) — went through several direct live corrections: rejected inside the Navbar pill, rejected as a per-page hero, landed on one shared component rendered as a SIBLING of `<header>` in `Navbar.tsx` (not a child) — a real bug caught via live screenshot: placing it inside the sticky header made it inherit `position: sticky` and overlap page content while scrolling. Fixed, confirmed live via a real `getBoundingClientRect()` scroll test.
  - **Verification**: both features live-verified with real DB data — a standalone authenticated script (signs in as the real test account via the public SDK client, calls `search_jobs` directly) AND a real browser page load (fresh dev-server instance, `location.href` navigation to work around the Browser pane's recurring `document.hidden` issue) both show real ranked results with real highlighted snippets. The literal type-a-keystroke interaction isn't click-verified yet.
  - **PWA**: `build-plan.md`'s "🆕" marker was stale — manifest/icons/global SW registration were already built. Added the missing `appleWebApp` metadata (iOS ignores the web manifest for install; only reads apple-specific meta tags).
  - **Mobile-overflow audit** — real 375px-viewport `getBoundingClientRect()` sweep across Dashboard/Missions/Career/Interview/Settings/Resume/Profile/Search. Found and fixed one real bug: `app/missions/loading.tsx`'s skeleton had a fixed `w-96` (384px) placeholder overflowing at mobile width; applied the same defensive `max-w-full` fix to `app/career/loading.tsx`'s `w-80` one (not yet overflowing, same fragile pattern). Everything else checked clean. Not yet audited: find-jobs, job-detail, admin, touch-target sizing.
  - **Live bug diagnosed, not a code bug**: a 73-result search stuck at "Scoring…" for ~5 minutes — root cause was Inngest Cloud processing delay/backlog (confirmed by replicating the evaluator's exact DB-write shape and testing Gemini connectivity directly, both fine; the backlog then visibly cleared, 23/99 scored within a couple minutes). Real secondary finding worth its own fix later: several `agent_runs` today never flip their own `status` to `completed` even after all their jobs finish scoring. Also: local `npm run dev` genuinely needs a local Inngest dev server running (`.env` has `INNGEST_DEV=1`) for evaluations to process during local testing — not needed for Preview/prod, which use Inngest Cloud.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean across every file touched.

- **2026-08-28 (Phase 28, cont'd): Subscription-tier scaling for all ~30 daily usage limits + email_lookup moved to a real monthly cap + new "Ace" tier + polished quota/upgrade modal wired app-wide. NOT YET COMMITTED — direct user instruction to hold off on commit/push until next session, after the one remaining manual step (see below).**
  - **The gap this closes**: `checkAndConsumeUsage`'s ~30 daily-limited actions (search, document_generation, bullet_rewrite, etc.) previously used the SAME flat number for every user regardless of plan — paying for Command never actually raised any of them. Only `job_evaluations_daily_limit` and the two `checkUsageLimit` features (insider_connections, company_research) already scaled per tier.
  - **Real pricing research, not guessed**: `agy` researched competitor pricing (JobRight/Teal/Huntr/Simplify/Rezi/Kickresume/Careerflow/LazyApply — clusters $24-$40/mo for an "unlimited AI" top tier) and proposed a cost-profile-cohorting scaling model. That model was independently reviewed and revised — the real disagreement: `agy`'s model gave the lifetime Vanguard tier ($149 one-time) the MOST generous numbers purely because it's priciest, which is backwards for a one-time payment with no recurring revenue to fund unbounded usage (a $149 lifetime payment amortized over 24-36 months of real activity has a LOWER sustainable monthly COGS budget than Command's $15/mo recurring, not higher — this is the same failure mode that's bankrupted real AppSumo-style lifetime-deal SaaS products). Final numbers used the reviewed, more conservative table, with Vanguard staying at its existing values (explicitly deferred by the user — "limited time, will change as time goes").
  - **New "Ace" tier** ($29/mo, real recurring revenue) — named for the Sortie/aviation vocabulary's own term for a pilot who's proven elite status across many sorties, consistent with Recon/Command/Vanguard. Genuinely generous/unlimited limits on the actions that can safely afford it.
  - **`email_lookup` moved out of the daily system entirely** into the same monthly `checkUsageLimit`/`PremiumFeature` mechanism insider_connections/company_research already use — its real ~$0.10/call cost and low natural frequency fit a monthly cap far better than a daily-reset one. Recon's real exposure was also tightened in the same pass (was unbounded — 4/day with no monthly ceiling — now a real 10/month cap).
  - **Schema**: `daily_action_limits` JSONB per-plan override map (absent key = falls back to `lib/usage.ts`'s `DAILY_LIMITS` constant, explicit `null` = unlimited) + `email_lookup_monthly_limit` column. Migration `20260828160000_add-ace-tier-and-daily-action-limits.sql`, applied via the now-familiar `db query` workaround.
  - **Full admin control** (direct user request — "full ability to control all the features and limits," confirmed live that plan edits already reflect on `/pricing` and `/settings`): `PlansManager.tsx` extended with an email-lookup-monthly field and a collapsible per-action daily-limits editor (29 rows, blank = default / number = override / "unlimited" = no cap). **Confirm-before-save dialog added** (direct user request) — a plan edit takes effect live for every subscriber instantly with no undo, so "Save" now requires confirming first, reusing the existing `ConfirmDialog`.
  - **Per-user override UI fixed** (direct user request — a real, confirmed gap): `UserDetailView.tsx`'s 4 override toggles (insider connections/company research/unlimited evaluations/GPT-4o) previously showed bare On/Off with zero indication of what the user's actual PLAN already grants — a Vanguard user's toggles all read "Off" even though the plan itself already includes real access, misleadingly implying the admin needed to flip them. Each toggle now shows its plan's real default value inline.
  - **Polished quota/upgrade modal wired app-wide** — the original ask that started this whole effort. `LimitReachedModal` generalized from 2 states to 3 (`upgrade_required` / `monthly_cap_reached` / `daily_cap_reached`) with a new `canUpgrade` flag, computed server-side by actually comparing every real plan's limit for that specific action (not assumed) — this matters now that Ace exists above Command, since a "monthly cap reached" no longer automatically means "already on the best tier" the way it used to when Command was the ceiling. Wired into: job search (`FindJobsForm.tsx` — `scrapeAndEvaluateJobs` changed to return a structured result instead of throwing for quota failures specifically, since Next.js Server Actions can mask thrown-error custom properties in production), job evaluation (`RequestScoringButton.tsx`), email lookups (`EmailLookupButton.tsx`), and Insider Connections (`InsiderConnectionsButton.tsx`, retrofitted with the same `canUpgrade` logic it never had before).
  - **Ace's Stripe price created and wired in, same session** — direct user correction: initially left `stripe_price_id` NULL assuming it needed real Stripe account access, but confirmed via `npx @insforge/cli payments stripe status` this project only has a `test`-mode Stripe connection configured (`live` shows `unconfigured`), so creating a test-mode product/price is a safe, fully reversible sandbox action, not a real-money one. Created via `insforge-cli payments stripe products create`/`prices create` (matching the exact Command/Vanguard convention — one product per tier, named after the plan) → `prod_V9nauw7Caxhw1G` / `price_1U9TzRQIPDGVmA61G7MbcOlc` ($29/mo recurring, USD) → written into `subscription_plans.stripe_price_id` for the `ace` row → `payments stripe sync` confirmed all 3 paid tiers now mirrored. `/pricing`'s "Not yet available" state for Ace should now resolve to a real "Upgrade to Ace" button on next load.

- **2026-08-28 (Phase 28, cont'd): TheirStack fallback provider wired into job search; SerpApi pagination cap raised 3→10 pages (real bug, direct user report).**
  - **Pagination bug**: a broad "software developer"/Canada search returned only ~30 results — `lib/jobScraper.ts`'s `fetchSerpApiPages()` was hardcoded to 3 pages, and Google Jobs returns exactly 10 results/page. Researched via `agy` CLI: Google Jobs itself has a hard ceiling around 100-200 total results per query regardless of page count (a consumer aggregator, not a comprehensive DB — LinkedIn/Indeed get real volume from their own indexed DB + direct ATS feeds, a different architecture entirely). Raised the cap to 10 pages (100 results, close to that real ceiling) — direct user confirmation on the real cost tradeoff (up to ~3.3x more SerpApi credits per broad search; narrower searches still exit early via the existing `next_page_token` check).
  - **TheirStack fallback**: `build-plan.md` documented TheirStack (structured job API, 100+ countries) and Serper.dev as candidate fallback/replacement providers for SerpApi — neither was ever built. Researched both via WebFetch against their real docs before writing any code: **Serper.dev has no job-search capability at all** (confirmed via two independent sources — its supported types are search/news/places/images/videos/shopping/scholar/patents, no "jobs" type), so it was dropped entirely rather than wired in as something that would silently return wrong results. **TheirStack does have a real Job Search API** (`POST https://api.theirstack.com/v1/jobs/search`, Bearer auth) — built as `theirstackProvider` in `lib/jobScraper.ts`, wired into `searchJobs()` as an overflow-only fallback: only tried when `isQuotaExhaustedError` fires AND every one of SerpApi's 3 keys is already exhausted (never on any other failure class, never spending TheirStack credits speculatively). `THEIRSTACK_API_KEY` env var, optional — falls through to the original SerpApi error if unset, no crash.
  - **Update, same session**: user signed up and provided a real `THEIRSTACK_API_KEY` (added directly to `.env`, confirmed gitignored, never echoed back in chat). Live-verified with one minimal real call (`limit: 1`, so at most 1 credit spent) — real `200` response, real job data, response envelope (`{metadata, data: [...]}`) and every field name (`job_title`/`company`/`short_location`/`source_url`/`date_posted`/`salary_string`/etc.) matched `theirstackProvider`'s parsing exactly, no code changes needed. The fallback chain is now genuinely production-ready, not just written-to-spec.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean.

- **2026-08-28 (Phase 28, cont'd): Root-caused and fixed the storage-download bug flagged in the previous entry — real fix, not a workaround.**
  - **Root cause, confirmed with a minimal reproduction, not guessed**: every server-side `fetch()` to InsForge's storage CDN (`cdn.insforge.dev`, CloudFront-backed) — `insforge.storage.from(...).download()`, used by the "View" button, the résumé-slot PDF route, and this session's new version-manager restore — failed with `ECONNRESET` during the TLS read. A raw `node --eval` repro isolated it: `fetch("https://cdn.insforge.dev/")` fails with `ECONNRESET`; the same URL with `dns.setDefaultResultOrder("ipv4first")` set first returns a clean `403` (CloudFront's normal response for an unsigned root request). This dev environment's IPv6 route to CloudFront's edge resets mid-connection; Node's default DNS order tries the IPv6 addresses first (`cdn.insforge.dev` resolves to both IPv6 and IPv4). Confirmed scoped to this one domain, not a general network problem — the same script hit `google.com` (200) and InsForge's main API domain (`umhshbx9.us-east.insforge.app`, a real 404 response, not a connection error) successfully with default DNS order.
  - **Fix**: `instrumentation.ts`'s `register()` hook (already existed, for Sentry config) now also calls `dns.setDefaultResultOrder("ipv4first")` once at server boot, scoped to the `nodejs` runtime (the `edge` runtime has no `node:dns` module and never hits this code path). This is a real, permanent fix — not a per-call workaround — since it's a process-wide Node.js DNS setting that every subsequent `fetch()`/`insforge.storage.download()` call in the server process inherits automatically.
  - **Not yet live-verified end-to-end in the running app**: `instrumentation.ts` only runs once at server *boot*, so this fix needs a dev server restart to take effect — and the dev server on port 3001 this session, belongs to a different Claude Code session, restarting it wasn't done unprompted. The underlying mechanism itself IS directly verified (the raw repro script, run both with and without the fix, against the exact same URL). **Ask the user to restart `npm run dev`** (or confirm it's fine to restart on their behalf) next session if this hasn't already been done, then re-test the "View" button and the version-manager Restore action for real confirmation.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean.

- **2026-08-28 (Phase 28, cont'd): Résumé/cover-letter version manager built — blocked from full end-to-end verification by a real, pre-existing, previously-undiscovered storage-download bug (not introduced by this feature).**
  - **Direct user report**: "we don't have version manager for AI generated resumes and cover letter for the same job." Confirmed real: `lib/documentPersistence.ts`'s `persistGeneratedDocument()` removed+overwrote the SAME fixed storage path and the SAME `applications` row on every regenerate — every prior AI draft was destroyed with no way back.
  - **Shipped**: new `document_versions` table (owner-only RLS, standard select/insert/delete policy set, migration `20260828140000_add-document-versions.sql`, applied via the now-familiar `db query` workaround for the untracked-migrations gap). `archiveCurrentDocument()` (exported from `lib/documentPersistence.ts`) archives whatever's currently live — a real permanent copy of the PDF plus a full content/sections/style snapshot — right before it gets overwritten, called from both a fresh generate and a restore. `listDocumentVersions()`/`restoreDocumentVersion()` actions (`actions/documents.ts`), a new `DocumentVersionHistory.tsx` panel (History button + list + View + Restore), wired into `DocumentGenerator.tsx`'s compact card AND both `ResumeWorkspace.tsx`/`CoverLetterWorkspace.tsx` full editor workspaces (same "don't just add it to the compact card, the workspace is where people actually look" lesson from the DOCX-button gap found earlier this session). New `/api/documents/download-version` route to view an archived PDF. Model name deliberately **not** shown in the UI (direct user correction — that's backend-only data).
  - **Real bug found while verifying, not caused by this feature**: `restoreDocumentVersion`'s `insforge.storage.from("resumes").download()` call returns a generic `{statusCode:500, error:"STORAGE_ERROR"}`. Root-caused via the SDK source (`node_modules/@insforge/sdk/dist/index.js`): `.download()` fetches the signed CloudFront URL from the InsForge gateway successfully (confirmed 200 in `insforge.logs`, "CloudFront signed URL generated successfully"), then does a **second, unlogged** `fetch()` directly against that signed CloudFront URL — that second fetch is what's failing. **Confirmed pre-existing, not something this session introduced**: the already-existing, untouched "View" button (`/api/documents/download`) fails with the exact same `{"error":"Failed to download document"}` on this same job's résumé. `npx @insforge/cli storage download` (bypassing the app entirely) also failed, with a raw Node/libuv network crash (`Assertion failed: !(handle->flags & UV_HANDLE_CLOSING)`) — points at a network-path problem reaching CloudFront specifically, not an app bug. **Not yet root-caused further or fixed** — worth its own dedicated investigation (possibly this dev environment's outbound network path to AWS CloudFront specifically); the DOCX download route works fine because it never calls `.download()` at all (rebuilds fresh from `resume_sections`).
  - **What's actually verified working**: the table/RLS/list UI (`document_versions` rows list correctly, History panel opens, shows real archived entries with correct relative timestamps) — tested by manually inserting a test row via `db query` and confirming it renders and the Restore button is reachable. **Not verified working end-to-end**: the actual file-copy mechanics (archive-on-generate, restore-to-live) — both call the same broken `.download()` path, so they're blocked until that's fixed. Code is correct (follows the identical pattern already used successfully elsewhere in this codebase before this bug was ever hit), the blocker is external.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean throughout.

Includes a "Report a bad link" button that calls the existing `createSupportTicket()` (`actions/support.ts`, category `bug`) with the job/link pre-filled — reuses Phase 26's feedback system rather than building a parallel one. Live-verified: real ticket created, then cleaned up (test data).
    3. **`functions/apify-job-search.ts`** — new Deno edge function (deployed via `functions deploy`, excluded from the main `tsconfig.json` since it's a separate runtime), a final fallback tier in `lib/reresolveApplyLink.ts` after the free ATS guess and the paid SerpApi search both fail. Runs a real Google search via Apify's free `apify/rag-web-browser` Actor (already connected at the account level from an earlier phase — `webscraper apify login` succeeded without needing fresh OAuth) using the token-bridge pattern from `insforge-cli`'s Apify skill (`GET /api/webscraper/apify/token`, never a stored personal key). **Deliberately stricter acceptance than the other tiers**: only takes a result classifying as `ats`/`employer`, never `aggregator` — live-tested proof that a general web search can return true false positives (unrelated companies' pages that just matched the search text), unlike Google Jobs' own apply_options which are guaranteed job-relevant. Real bug caught before shipping: `searchResult.url` in the Actor's own response is a `google.com/goto?url=...` redirect-tracking link, not the real destination — `metadata.url`/`metadata.canonicalUrl` are the real ones, confirmed via a live test call before writing the fix.
    4. Full chain live-verified on a real job (Amazon, previously mis-linked to `bebee.com`): viewing its detail page correctly self-healed it to `ca.indeed.com` via the tier-2 SerpApi step — confirming the cascade (ATS guess → SerpApi search → Apify search → give up) fires correctly in the real app, not just in isolation.
  - **Same-day, two more real bugs found live while testing the above**:
    1. **Evaluation-trigger robustness** — a real search (22 jobs, saved successfully) came back as a full 500 to the user because triggering AI evaluation via `inngest.send()` threw uncaught (the local Inngest dev server wasn't running — a separate process this app has always needed, not something today's work broke). Fixed: `evaluateWithinQuota()` (`lib/actions/scraper.actions.ts`) now wraps that call in try/catch — a failed evaluation-trigger degrades to "saved, not yet scored" instead of failing jobs that already saved correctly. Pre-existing gap, unrelated to today's ATS/apply-link work; just never surfaced before since Inngest happened to always be running in prior sessions. Also started the local Inngest dev server for this session so scoring actually runs.
    2. **Company logos, real root cause found** — user-reported "logos not showing properly." First hypothesis (an actual render bug) turned out to be a dev-only Fast Refresh/HMR artifact — `CompanyLogo.tsx`'s local error-recovery `useState` was getting reset by this session's own many live code edits before it could converge, confirmed by the exact same jobs rendering correctly on a clean reload once edits stopped. The REAL, separate issue the user then pushed back on (too many companies showing the generic icon at all) was genuine: `CompanyLogo.tsx`'s naive fallback guesses a domain from the company NAME string (`"Royal Bank of Canada"` → `royalbankofcanada.com`, which doesn't exist — the real domain is `rbc.com`). Fixed with `lib/applyLinkTrust.ts`'s new `extractLikelyLogoDomain()`: reuses the job's own trust-classified apply URL (this session's earlier work) as a real, verified domain signal instead of a name-derived guess. Deliberately looser than the apply-link picker itself — accepts `"employer"` AND `"unverified"` tiers (only excludes `ats`/`aggregator`/`low_quality`, which would return the wrong company's or vendor's logo), since a wrong domain here just 404s harmlessly through the existing `/api/logo` proxy and falls through, unlike an apply link where showing the wrong destination actively misleads. Wired into the 6 real call sites (`JobResultCard`, `JobIdentityRail`, `KanbanCard`, `InboxTable`, `JobDetailDrawer`, `RecentlyViewed`) via a new optional `applyUrl` prop; two narrowed `Pick<Job,...>` types (`RecentlyViewedJob`, `KanbanJob`) and one `.select()` query updated to carry the field through. Live-verified: RBC's real logo (`rbc.com`, 32px real image) now loads where it previously 404'd on the naive guess.
    3. **"Back to Jobs" reverting to an older search, real root cause found** — direct user report. `app/find-jobs/page.tsx`'s "last run" query (added specifically to fix exactly this symptom in an earlier phase) excluded `agent_runs.status = 'running'` on the theory that only a "completed" run represents a real, finished search. That's backwards: `scrapeAndEvaluateJobs()` upserts every job row synchronously before AI evaluation is even triggered, so `'running'` only ever means scoring hasn't finished — never that the jobs aren't real or saved. Since AI evaluation is async (Inngest) and commonly still running when a user opens a job's detail page and presses Back, the exclusion was hiding the search they just ran and silently falling back to an older completed one — the exact regression the feature exists to prevent. Fixed by dropping the status filter entirely (order by `updated_at DESC`, full stop) — unscored jobs already render correctly via `FindJobsForm`'s own polling ("Scoring…"), so there was no real reason to filter by status here. Live-verified via a surgical test (flipped a real run back to `'running'`, confirmed the page still correctly showed it as the last search, reverted after) — a fresh end-to-end click-through test was blocked by the test account's own real 5/day search quota, already exhausted by this session's own testing.

Fixed with the standard pattern: `flex h-full flex-col` on each card (stretches to the grid row's real height) + everything above the button wrapped in a `flex-1` div (absorbs the leftover space) + the button keeps its real `mt-8`. Considered a bare `mt-auto` on the button first — rejected because it would resolve to a real 0 on whichever card is tallest, losing the guaranteed minimum gap there specifically. Created one (`jobpilot-experiment-preview-sortie3.vercel.app`, aliased to the latest preview deployment via `vercel alias set`) and added it to `insforge.toml`, applied via `config apply`. Future preview deploys need that same alias command re-run to stay current — test/share the stable alias URL, not the raw per-deploy hash URL, which stays unwhitelisted. Not fully click-verified end-to-end (Vercel's own separate Deployment Protection/SSO gate sits in front of the whole preview and needs the user's own Vercel login — disclosed, not worked around).

Remaining, deliberately not started: an admin-scoped command palette (always scoped as an optional stretch item, not required).

- **2026-08-26 (Phase 26, cont'd): Admin panel — Delete user (+ billing/other ticket categories, Phase 2 groundwork). All uncommitted.**
  - **Delete user, direct request ("as an admin and owner under manage users")** — `/admin/users/[userId]` gained a "Delete this user" danger-zone card, matching the exact "type DELETE to confirm" pattern the user's own self-service Settings → Delete my account already used (not a lighter-weight admin shortcut). Owner+admin gate (`support_readonly` blocked server-side), self-deletion explicitly refused with a message pointing at the real Settings flow instead.
  - **Real shared-logic extraction, not a second copy**: `actions/account.ts`'s `deleteAccount()` had a hard-won, twice-live-verified row-delete sequence (storage cleanup, explicit deletes for tables with no cascade from `auth.users`, one implicit-transaction raw-SQL call so nothing partial survives a failure). Extracted to `lib/accountDeletion.ts`'s `deleteAllUserData(targetUserId)`, switched to the service-role client throughout (the self-service caller's own RLS-scoped reads only ever returned their own rows anyway; the new admin caller needs to read a *different* user's rows, which a regular client can't). Both `deleteAccount()` and the new `actions/admin.ts`'s `deleteUserAsAdmin()` now call the one function.
  - **Real bug caught before it shipped, not after**: `admin_audit_log.target_user_id` is a hard FK to `auth.users(id)` (`ON DELETE SET NULL`, confirmed live via `pg_constraint`). Logging the audit entry *after* deleting the user — the natural-looking order — would have hit that FK on a now-nonexistent id; `logAdminAction()` swallows its own errors by design, so the failure would never have surfaced, it would have just silently dropped the one audit record that matters most in the whole file. Fixed by logging before the delete; verified live that Postgres's own `ON DELETE SET NULL` still nulls `target_user_id` afterward as expected, but the `before: {email}` snapshot taken at log time preserves the real identity regardless.
  - **Live-verified end to end** against a real disposable test account (`sortie-deletion-test-*@example.com`, created in an earlier session's own deletion-testing) — the confirm button stayed disabled until "DELETE" was typed, then confirmed both `profiles` and `auth.users` rows gone, the audit log row present with the right email, and a real redirect to `/admin/users` with the account no longer listed.

  - **Ticket categories**: added `billing` and `other` (2 small migrations) to the Phase 1 feedback system per direct follow-up requests — `TicketCategory` now bug/feature_request/change_request/feedback/billing/other/support, all three admin+user-facing components updated to match.

- **2026-08-23 (Phase 22): Vanguard — $149 one-time lifetime tier, 250-seat cap, direct user request.**
  - `subscription_plans` gains `max_seats`/`seats_claimed` (any plan can be scarcity-capped now, not hardcoded to Vanguard) and a new `lifetime` `billing_period`. A real Stripe Product/Price created via the CLI (`price_1U7T5XQIPDGVmA612XZuyXHN`, one-time, no `--interval`), seeded with Command's exact limits (7 insider connections/mo, 25 company research/mo, unlimited evaluations, LLM unlocked) so the existing plan-driven circuit breakers in `lib/subscription.ts` needed zero new logic.
  - **Race-safe seat claim**: `claim_plan_seat()` (new `plan_seat_claims` append-only ledger + an atomic `UPDATE subscription_plans SET seats_claimed = seats_claimed + 1 WHERE seats_claimed < max_seats` — Postgres's own row-level lock is the entire mechanism) runs at Stripe *fulfillment* (`fulfill_stripe_one_time_purchase()`, a new trigger on `payments.webhook_events` for `checkout.session.completed` in `mode=payment`, separate from the existing subscription-fulfillment trigger), not at checkout-start — that's the only point overselling can actually happen. `actions/billing.ts`'s pre-checkout seat check is a fast best-effort UX guard on top, not the real guarantee.
  - `createCheckoutSessionAction` now branches `mode: "payment"` vs `"subscription"` off `plan.billingPeriod === "lifetime"`, and stamps `metadata: { plan_tier }` on the session so the one-time fulfillment trigger can resolve which plan was bought (a Checkout Session webhook payload has no line-item/price array the way an invoice does).
  - UI: `CTASection.tsx` (pricing) and `SubscriptionTab.tsx` (Settings) both generalized to render every real paid plan instead of just one, with a scarcity progress bar + "Sold out" lockdown for capped plans, and a distinct `buy_once` action (never the price-comparison upgrade/downgrade logic, which is meaningless for a one-time purchase). `PlansManager.tsx` (admin) gained a Max Seats field + read-only claimed count.
  - New daily Inngest cron (`resetLifetimePlanUsagePeriodsAsync`) rolls each lifetime-plan holder's own `api_usage_metrics` period forward on their own purchase anniversary — a one-time payment has no recurring Stripe invoice to do that automatically the way Command's subscription renewal does.
  - **Live-verified end to end**: real 3-card pricing page render (scarcity bar at "0/250"), real Settings flow as a Command-tier test account (correctly offered "Buy Vanguard" as a distinct one-time action, not an upgrade/downgrade), a real Stripe test-mode Checkout Session created and confirmed showing "Vanguard $149.00 — Lifetime access, limited to 250 seats" (payment not completed — that would consume a real seat and wasn't asked for), and the admin billing form. `tsc`/`eslint`/`npm run build` all clean.

  - **Layer 1**: `lib/pipelineStrategy.ts`'s `computePipelineSnapshot()` and `app/dashboard/page.tsx`'s `funnelCounts` were counting jobs the Action Center itself already flags as likely-gone (`lib/dashboardInsights.ts`'s own confirmed/likely `getListingSignal` threshold) as real pipeline backlog. Both now exclude them, matching what the Action Center already does.
  - **Layer 2, structural** (research recommendation from an earlier session, verbatim: split into a pre-pipeline Inbox feeding a Pipeline that only starts at an explicit shortlist stage): `jobs.application_status` gains two new values — `inbox` (the new DB default; pre-pipeline, excluded from the Kanban's `STAGE_ORDER` entirely) and `shortlisted` (the real first Kanban stage — "I want to pursue this," replacing what `draft` used to loosely mean). **Direct user decision**: all 1,315 existing `draft` jobs backfilled straight into `inbox` (`migrations/20260823140000_add-inbox-shortlisted-stages.sql` — had to widen the CHECK constraint to the union of old+new values first, backfill, then tighten it, since a plain add-and-drop-in-one-step fails Postgres's own immediate constraint validation against existing rows).
  - New `components/missions/InboxTable.tsx` — a genuinely lighter dense table (not `JobResultCard`-per-row like List view), its own local search, per-row and bulk Shortlist/Archive, wired into `MissionsView.tsx` as a third tab (Board/List/Inbox) with a live count badge. `KanbanBoard.tsx`'s `groupByStatus()` got a defensive skip-if-unknown-status guard so a stray Inbox job can never crash it, on top of `MissionsView.tsx` splitting `pipelineJobs`/`inboxJobs` before either view ever sees them.
  - New daily Inngest cron (`archiveStaleInboxJobsAsync`) auto-hides (`is_hidden = true`, the same lever the existing bulk-archive action already uses) any Inbox job still untouched 14 days after being found — real trackers researched (Huntr/Teal/JobRight) all do this rather than letting an inbox pile up forever.
  - Every other real touchpoint updated to the two-stage split: `lib/outcomeInsights.ts`/`lib/careerTimeline.ts`/`app/career/page.tsx`/`app/api/career/export/route.ts` (a job hasn't been "genuinely applied to" while either pre-application status), `components/job-details/JobActionBar.tsx` (status dropdown now offers Shortlisted as the note-prompt-free transition, matching what `draft` used to be), `DocumentGenerator.tsx`'s cost-warning gate, `KanbanCard.tsx`'s staleness badge.
  - **Live-verified end to end on real production data** (129 real Inbox jobs on the test account): shortlisted one real job from the Inbox table, watched the Inbox count drop and the job disappear, confirmed it landed in the Board's Shortlisted column, and confirmed the dashboard Pipeline Funnel/AI snapshot both updated to reflect it (down from a fake "Draft: 99" bottleneck to a real "Shortlisted: 1"). `tsc`/`eslint`/`npm run build` all clean.

- **2026-08-20 (Phase 20): "AI heavy dashboard" — item 2 of 2, the proactive weekly briefing.**
  - Deliberately NOT a live call — a weekly Inngest cron (`generateWeeklyBriefingsAsync`, Monday 9am, matching `sendFollowUpNudgesAsync`'s exact "find eligible users, one `step.run` per user" shape) pre-generates one short real summary per user and stores it on new `profiles.weekly_briefing`/`weekly_briefing_generated_at` columns (added via plain `db query` — no `auth.users` FK involved, so the migrations-tracking workaround wasn't needed per this project's own recorded gotcha). The dashboard just reads and displays the stored value — zero AI cost on page load, exactly the "feels automatic without a live call" goal from the earlier cost-tradeoff conversation with the user.
  - **Real, deliberate eligibility filter, not a blanket weekly call for every user**: only users with a job found, an application/interview event, or an upcoming deadline in the last/next 7 days get a briefing generated — a completely dormant account costs nothing.
  - `lib/weeklyBriefing.ts` (aggregation types + AI synthesis, same grounded-only-in-real-numbers system prompt discipline as every other AI widget in this app) + the cron function in `lib/inngest/functions.ts`, registered in `app/api/inngest/route.ts`. New `WeeklyBriefingCard.tsx` is pure display, no client interactivity needed.
  - **A real verification obstacle hit and worked around, not a bug in the feature**: manually invoking a cron-triggered Inngest function through the local dev server's UI (`localhost:8288`) proved unreliable in this environment — repeated synthetic click attempts (plain `.click()`, a full pointerdown/mousedown/pointerup/mouseup/click sequence) produced no visible effect and zero runs recorded. A standalone `tsx` script hit a separate wall (`@insforge/shared-schemas`'s package exports aren't resolvable outside Next.js's own bundler). Worked around by adding a temporary, clearly-labeled API route (`app/api/test-weekly-briefing-temp/route.ts`) that ran the exact real cron logic once inside the real Next.js runtime, hit via a plain `curl`, then deleted immediately after — confirmed via `git status` that it was never committed.
  - **Live-verified end to end with real production data**: real snapshot (`jobsFoundThisWeek: 1`, everything else accurately zero), a real AI-generated summary that exactly matched those numbers with zero invention, confirmed written to the real `profiles` row via a direct DB query, and confirmed rendering correctly on `/dashboard` ("YOUR WEEKLY BRIEFING — August 20, 2026 — You found 1 new job opportunity this week...").
  - `tsc --noEmit` clean, full `npm run build` clean (confirmed the temp test route left no trace in the build output).

- **2026-08-20 (Phase 20): Homepage performance pass — build-plan.md §S fast-follow, found 2 real pre-existing bugs while checking network requests.**
  - **Real bug #1**: `CommandPalette.tsx` had NO public-route gating at all — every command it offers points to an authenticated-only page (`quickSearchJobs` also requires a logged-in user), yet Cmd+K was fully active on the marketing homepage, opening a palette full of links that just bounce to `/login`.
  - **Real bug #2**: `NavigatorLauncher.tsx`'s own `isPublicRoute()` had gone stale — it never picked up `/methodology`, `/pricing`, or `/ats-checker` when those shipped earlier this session, so Navigator's FAB could still render (and fail on click, per that component's own header comment) on all three.
  - **The deeper performance issue underneath both**: `next/dynamic()`'s `import()` fires as soon as the wrapping component is instantiated — the existing internal "return null on a public route" checks only ran AFTER the JS chunk had already downloaded, not before. A network-request check on the live homepage (checking for a Lighthouse-equivalent audit, since no Lighthouse runner tool is available here) surfaced this: `NavigatorLauncher`/`CommandPalette`/`SettingsModal`/referral/service-worker chunks were all downloading on the public homepage even though most of them are entirely irrelevant pre-signup.
  - **Fixed**: new shared `lib/publicRoutes.ts`'s `isPublicMarketingRoute()` (one source of truth, replacing the stale local copy in `NavigatorLauncher.tsx`), used to gate both `NavigatorLauncherLoader.tsx` and `CommandPaletteLoader.tsx` at the loader level — a public-page visitor now never fetches either chunk at all. `CommandPalette.tsx` itself also gained the same internal bail-out `NavigatorLauncher.tsx` already had, for defense in depth.
  - **Deliberately left ungated**: `SettingsModalLoader`/`ReferralCaptureLoader`/`ReferralClaimerLoader`/`ServiceWorkerRegisterLoader` — Settings only renders visible UI behind a `?settings=1` query param a logged-out visitor would never organically have; referral capture is specifically meant to work pre-signup (a `?ref=` link can land on the homepage); PWA install/service-worker registration working pre-signup isn't clearly wrong either way and changing it is a real product-scope decision, not a quick performance fix.
  - **Live-verified, including a real false-alarm correctly not chased**: confirmed via `read_network_requests` that the two chunks no longer load on the public homepage; confirmed Cmd+K genuinely does nothing there. Testing the authenticated regression case (Cmd+K on `/dashboard`) initially looked broken in the same browser tab used for the public-page test — but that was, again, the browser tab's own stale Next.js Router Cache (same root cause documented in the conversion-tracking entry above), not a real regression: a fresh tab confirmed Cmd+K and the Navigator FAB both work correctly on `/dashboard`.
  - `tsc --noEmit` clean, full `npm run build` clean.

- **2026-08-20 (Phase 20): Conversion tracking on the new marketing CTAs — build-plan.md §S fast-follow.**
  - New `trackPostHogClientEvent()` in `lib/posthog-client.ts` (same no-op-if-unconfigured guard as the rest of that file) fires a `marketing_cta_clicked` event with a `location` property (`hero`/`pricing`/`nav_desktop`/`nav_mobile`) on every "Start for free" click across the homepage.
  - New `TrackedCtaLink.tsx` — a small Client Component boundary, since `Hero.tsx`/`CTASection.tsx` are Server Components and can't own an inline `onClick` themselves. `Navbar.tsx` is already a Client Component, so its two "Start for free" links got the tracker wired in directly instead.
  - **A real environment finding, worth remembering for this project going forward**: hit what looked like a 3rd recurrence of the "stale Turbopack graph" bug (`ReferenceError: Link is not defined` in `CTASection.tsx`, despite the file being confirmed correct on disk, `tsc --noEmit` clean, and a full `npm run build` clean) — but this time a full `.next` wipe + dev-server restart did NOT clear it. Opening a genuinely fresh browser tab (rather than reloading the existing one) cleared it immediately. Root cause: Next.js's client-side Router Cache lives in the BROWSER TAB's memory, not the dev server, and survives a full server restart since nothing forces the tab to discard it. **Going forward, try a fresh tab before escalating to a `.next` wipe** — it's a cheaper, more targeted fix, and may explain some of this session's earlier "stale graph" diagnoses too.
  - **Verified PostHog is genuinely configured in production** (`vercel env ls production` confirms real `NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_KEY`/`_HOST` values set) even though local `.env` has an empty key by design — so this isn't dead code, it will actually fire once deployed. Local verification confirmed the click wiring itself is error-free and navigation still works correctly (the actual PostHog capture is a local no-op by design, not independently observable without a configured local key or dashboard access).
  - `tsc --noEmit` clean, full `npm run build` clean.

Confirmed via the real server logs that this was a fixture problem, not a code problem, by retesting with a genuinely spec-compliant PDF (`pdf-parse`'s own test fixture, fetched into the browser and cleaned up after) — extraction succeeded cleanly (82,756 real characters populated the textarea).

- **2026-08-20 (Phase 19): Accessibility (WCAG 2.1 AA) audit — build-plan.md §H, via the design:accessibility-review skill.**
  - **Real finding #1, fixed everywhere**: NONE of this app's 11 custom dropdown/popover components (`MissionsFilterBar.tsx`'s `FilterPopover`, `JobResultCard.tsx`'s options menu, `Navbar.tsx`'s Jobs dropdown, `StyleTab.tsx`, `find-jobs/FilterBar.tsx`, `ProfileForm.tsx`'s date picker, `ResumeManager.tsx`'s row menu, `EditorTab.tsx`'s add-section menu, and all 3 panels in `JobActionBar.tsx`) closed on Escape — only click-outside or scroll closed them, a real WCAG 2.1.1 keyboard-operability gap (`NavigatorLauncher.tsx`/`AdminNavigatorLauncher.tsx` already had this right — used as the reference pattern). Added a parallel `keydown`/Escape listener next to each existing click-outside listener, same shape as the already-correct two. **Live-verified on 2 real instances through the actual UI** (Missions' Sort popover, JobActionBar's status menu) — confirmed each panel genuinely opens, then genuinely closes on a real dispatched Escape keydown, not assumed from code review alone.
  - **Real finding #2, flagged but deliberately NOT unilaterally fixed**: `--color-text-muted` (#8A8F89) computes to only ~3.3:1 contrast against white surfaces — below WCAG AA's 4.5:1 minimum for normal text (1.4.3), verified by hand via the real relative-luminance formula, not assumed. This token is used extensively app-wide for legible informational text (timestamps, secondary labels), not decorative/exempt content. **Not changed** — `text-muted`'s specific lightness is a deliberate, hand-tuned design-system choice (ui-tokens.md: "quiet, informational" specifically because it's a step lighter than `text-secondary`), and this project's own history shows color-token changes always get real visual verification before shipping, never as a silent side-fix bundled into an unrelated pass. A verified fix candidate is documented (darkening to roughly `#6B706B` would land at ~5.05:1, comfortable margin) but needs a real look-and-feel check before landing, not a unilateral swap.
  - `tsc --noEmit` clean on all 9 changed files.

- **2026-08-20 (Phase 19): Bulk actions on Missions (multi-select archive/tag) — build-plan.md §H.**
  - List-view-only (Kanban's drag-one-at-a-time interaction doesn't suit multi-select). `JobResultCard.tsx` gained optional `selectable`/`selected`/`onToggleSelect` props (same optional-prop pattern as `index`/`reappearanceSignal`) — a checkbox renders next to the company logo when selection mode is on, with `preventDefault`/`stopPropagation` so it doesn't trigger the card's own navigation `<Link>`.
  - New `actions/jobs.ts` actions: `bulkHideJobs` (plain `.in("id", jobIds)` update) and `bulkAddTag` (fetches each selected job's own existing tags first and appends+dedupes per job, since a blanket overwrite via the existing `updateJobTags` would wipe out tags a job already had — one fetch + concurrent per-job updates, since InsForge has no single-statement "append to array across heterogeneous rows" primitive).
  - **Live-verified end to end through the real UI**: entered select mode, selected 2 real jobs, confirmed the bulk-tag action wrote the tag to both jobs' real `tags` arrays; selected 2 different jobs, confirmed bulk-archive set `is_hidden=true` on both. All test tags/hidden-flags reverted after.
  - `tsc --noEmit` clean.

- **2026-08-20 (Phase 19): Dedup audit — build-plan.md §37, found and fixed two real, separate duplicate-creation bugs, deliberately did NOT attempt a retroactive merge of existing duplicates.**
  - **Bug 1 (manual paste + browser extension capture)**: `lib/externalJob.ts`'s `createExternalJob` (shared by `actions/jobs.ts`'s `addExternalJob` and `app/api/extension/capture-job/route.ts`) had zero dedup — every call generated a fresh random `external_id` and inserted unconditionally. A double click of the extension's "Save to Sortie" button, or re-pasting the same URL, silently created a genuine duplicate row every time. Fixed with a same-user-same-`url` pre-check that returns the existing job instead of inserting a new one.
  - **Bug 2, more severe (the primary SerpApi search path)**: a real production data check found one account with the exact same job URL duplicated up to **11 times**. Root cause: `lib/actions/scraper.actions.ts`'s existing `upsert(..., {onConflict: 'user_id,external_id'})` dedup only catches a repeat SerpApi `job_id` — but SerpApi/Google Jobs does NOT return a stable `job_id` for the same real listing across separate search runs (each carries a differently-signed `htidocid` token), confirmed by inspecting the real duplicate rows: several shared byte-identical `title`/`company`/`location` under completely different URLs. This is exactly the gap build-plan.md's original §37 spec anticipated ("same external_id OR same title+company+location fingerprint"). Fixed with a fingerprint pre-check: a job whose normalized title+company+location already exists for this user now refreshes the existing row's `run_id`/`dropped_from_search_at` instead of inserting a second row.
  - **Deliberately scoped to preventing NEW duplicates only** — did not attempt an automated retroactive merge of the duplicate rows already sitting in the data. Merging is a real data-loss risk (which row's `application_status`/tags/personal notes/interview history "wins" when two rows represent the same real job) that needs an actual product decision on merge semantics, not a unilateral cleanup script. Flagging this as still open: real duplicate rows exist in production data today and were not touched.
  - **Verification tier, disclosed honestly**: the manual-paste fix (Bug 1) was live-verified end to end through the real UI — submitted the same URL twice through `/jobs/external`, confirmed only one row existed after both, cleaned up. The search-path fix (Bug 2) was NOT live-tested with a fresh real search (would have spent a real, arguably redundant SerpApi call to prove logic already independently confirmed) — instead verified by confirming the fingerprint function actually collides on real already-duplicated production rows (`Sr Software Developer` / `Royal Bank of Canada` / `Toronto, ON` appeared 3+ times byte-identical) and a full clean `npm run build` on this exact code.
  - `tsc --noEmit` clean.

- **2026-08-20 (Phase 19): Profile page mobile-responsive fix — direct user request, mid-session.**
  - Real bug in the shared `components/ui/Tabs.tsx` (used by Profile, the job detail page's Overview/Company/Offer Tools, and both document workspaces): the tab-bar container was a plain `flex w-fit shrink-0` row with no wrap or scroll handling. `/profile`'s 4-tab set (Personal/Education/Work Experience/Preferences) sat right at the edge of a real 375px viewport with zero margin — a longer label render or slightly different font metrics would silently push the whole page into horizontal scroll instead of failing loudly. Fixed by making the tablist itself `overflow-x-auto` (contains any overflow to the pill bar via real native touch-scroll, never the page) with `w-full sm:w-fit` to keep desktop's original compact sizing.
  - Fixing the shared component fixes this for every page that uses `Tabs.tsx`, not just Profile.
  - **Live-verified at a real 375px viewport, not assumed**: confirmed a real overflow before the fix (tablist `scrollWidth` 445 vs 343 available, `overflow-x: visible`), confirmed zero page-level horizontal overflow after (`document.body.scrollWidth` exactly 375, `overflow-x: auto` correctly containing the tablist's own internal scroll). Re-checked all 4 tabs individually plus the "Edit Personal" modal at the same viewport — zero overflowing elements anywhere.
  - `tsc --noEmit` clean.

Confirmed via two independent signals instead of restarting a server that isn't this session's to restart: `tsc --noEmit` clean, and a full fresh `npm run build` compiling `/career` (and every other route) with zero errors on the exact current code. Trusted the clean independent build over the stale dev-server error.

Code-identical to `SkillGapTracker`'s already-proven data-fetch shape, and passed a clean independent production build, but this is a real, disclosed lower-confidence tier, not silently assumed equal to the other live-verified items in this batch.

- **2026-08-20 (Phase 19): job_decisions table — build-plan.md §Q3 fast-follow, the one piece deliberately deferred when §Q3 originally shipped.**
  - New `job_decisions` table (`user_id, job_id, decision enum('applied','skipped'), skip_reason, decided_at`, `UNIQUE(user_id, job_id)` so a job's later re-decided decision replaces the old one rather than duplicating). References `auth.users(id)`, so this one genuinely needed `db migrations` (not `db query`, which rejects auth-schema FKs) — hit the long-standing legacy-migrations-tracking blocker again, worked around it again (move every other `migrations/*.sql` file aside, apply, move back).
  - **Design decision made without asking, to resolve the exact overlap risk §Q3's original entry flagged**: rather than a new parallel "skip" UI, the existing Hide toggle IS the skip-decision capture point — `JobActionBar.tsx`'s Hide button now opens a small optional quick-pick reason panel (same portal/fixed-position pattern as the existing status-note prompt) only when hiding, never on un-hide. `setApplicationStatus` records `decision='applied'` the same way on a real transition to `applied`. Both writes are fire-and-forget/best-effort, same non-blocking pattern as every other side-write in `actions/jobs.ts`. Deliberately scoped to the job detail page's `JobActionBar` only, not `JobResultCard`'s list-view Hide — a modal-per-card interrupt on a high-traffic list view is a real UX cost the detail page doesn't have.
  - `lib/outcomeInsights.ts` gained `computeSkipReasons`/`computeAppliedVsSkipped`, surfaced as a new "Applied vs. skipped" block in `/career`'s existing `OutcomeInsights.tsx`, same 3-sample honesty gate as its other stats.
  - **A real gating bug found and fixed via live testing, not caught by `tsc`**: the whole `OutcomeInsights` section returns an empty state whenever the OLDER `hasEnoughData` (interview-rate stats) is false — the new applied-vs-skipped block, gated independently on its own real sample size, never got a chance to render even with enough decision data, since it sat inside the same early-return branch. Fixed by checking both data sources' gates before falling back to the empty state, and by hiding the AI-narrative button specifically when only decision data exists (it would always fail server-side otherwise, since `generateOutcomeNarrativeAction` still checks the older gate).
  - **Live-verified end to end**: hid a real job through the actual UI reason panel, confirmed the exact chosen reason landed in the DB; seeded 2 more decisions to cross the 3-sample threshold, confirmed `/career` rendered "1 applied · 2 skipped" with the correct reason counts and the AI button correctly absent; all 3 test decisions deleted and the one real UI-hidden job's `is_hidden` reverted after.
  - `tsc --noEmit` clean.

Folded the legitimate SEO value into the shareable-link page instead, which shows only the user's own AI-generated evaluation — genuinely original content, never the scraped description/salary/personal notes/application status.

Applied directly via `db query` (one statement per call, per this project's documented multi-statement gotcha) rather than `db migrations up --all`, which still hits the long-standing legacy-migrations-tracking blocker; the migration file on disk is documentation-only, same historical pattern as this project's other early schema changes.

Fixed with the same established pattern: defer to a `useEffect`-set state instead of a render-time read.
  - **A real environment artifact re-confirmed, not a new bug**: this session's Browser pane isn't compositing frames (a `screenshot` call fails with the exact error RESUME.md's Phase 18 "phantom bug" already documented), which silently no-ops `computer`-tool clicks with zero error — two revoke-button clicks via `computer` left zero trace in either the network log or the DB. Verified the real revoke path instead via a direct DOM `click()` dispatched through `javascript_tool` (debugging-only use, not implementation), which fired the real server action and cleared `share_token` in the DB. Don't mistake a silent `computer`-click no-op for a broken button in this environment — check for a fired network request first.
  - **Live-verified end to end**: generated a real token on a real Asana job, confirmed the public `/share/[token]` page renders correctly and unauthenticated (correct title/company/match%/grade/10-dimension breakdown, real `generateMetadata` title), then revoked and confirmed the same URL now 404s.

- **2026-08-19 (Phase 18): All 4 items in the "AI Driven and AI heavy" admin queue built, plus a new referral system — direct user request, session redirected mid-build to prioritize the admin-panel halves.**
  - **Programmatic SEO/GEO content engine** (item 1, highest ROI): `lib/admin/geoContent.ts` aggregates real, anonymous cross-user job-posting counts (role family via the existing `normalizeRoleFamily`, average match score, top skills) into a data-backed "Job Market Report" page, drafted by Gemini and queued into the existing `pages` table (`content_source = 'ai_geo'`, new column). `lib/inngest/functions.ts`'s `generateGeoContentAsync` runs on both a weekly cron and a manual "Generate GEO page now" button on `/admin/content` (`actions/adminContent.ts`'s `generateGeoPageNow`) — same underlying logic either way, never regenerates an already-covered topic. `ContentList.tsx` shows an "AI GEO" badge. Published pages now carry real `Article` JSON-LD (`app/blog/[slug]/page.tsx`) for AI-search extractability. **Live-verified end to end**: triggered a real generation, confirmed via direct DB query (36 real Software Developer postings aggregated correctly, a genuinely good, honest, data-grounded draft), published it, confirmed the JSON-LD renders on the live page — then reverted it back to `draft` after verification since a human should decide when it goes public for real.
  - **"Success Story" content repurposing pipeline** (item 2): new `social_drafts` table. `actions/jobs.ts`'s `setApplicationStatus` fires `success-story/consider` on a real `'offered'` transition; `lib/admin/socialDrafts.ts`'s `generateAndQueueSuccessStory` drafts a fully anonymized 3-part thread (never the real name/company/title) grounded only in real data (role family, days-tracked). New "Success story drafts" approval inbox on `/admin/marketing` (`SocialDraftsQueue.tsx`, `actions/adminSocialDrafts.ts`) with Approve/Reject/Mark-posted. **Live-verified**: fired the real event against a real tracked job, confirmed a correctly anonymized draft landed in the DB, approved it through the real admin UI, confirmed the status/audit-log write.
  - **Referral system** (item 3, new build): `profiles.referral_code`/`referred_by_code` + `referral_rewards` table. Lazily generated per-user code (`actions/referrals.ts`), captured client-side from a `?ref=` link via localStorage (`components/referrals/`) and claimed once after login — reward is a small permanent `custom_usage_multiplier` bump (+0.1, capped at 2.0), idempotent via a real `UNIQUE(referred_id)` constraint. New Settings → Referrals tab. Admin visibility: a "Referrals" overview (top referrers, total count) added to `/admin/marketing`. **Live-verified**: generated a real code, confirmed the link/stats render correctly.
  - **AI-personalized referral copy** (item 4, depends on item 3): `lib/referralCopy.ts` + `actions/referralCopy.ts`, new `referral_message` usage action (10/day, free-tier Gemini). Grounds the draft only in the user's real title, never invents Sortie stats; the real link is appended after generation, never AI-typed. **Live-verified**: generated a real LinkedIn draft, honest content, correct link appended.
  - **Signal-based outreach automation** (item 5) — the one item explicitly gated on a paid decision (Clay/Apollo) no agent can make. Shipped the free half for real: `lib/hiringSignal.ts` computes a genuine hiring-velocity signal (rising posting volume per company) from the app's own already-collected `jobs` data, surfaced as a new `OutreachSignal.tsx` card on the job detail page's Company tab (only renders on a real signal, never fabricated). Shipped the paid half as an inert scaffold, same "wired but dormant until a real key exists" pattern as Sentry/Resend: new `outreach_signal_settings` table + `/admin/marketing`'s "Outreach signal enrichment" card — provider picker persists, but the "configured" status is read live from `ENRICHMENT_API_KEY`'s presence, never a DB lie. **Live-verified**: provider selection persists correctly in the DB.
  - **A real bug found and fixed via live testing, not caught by `tsc`/`eslint`**: a `"use server"` file (`actions/referralCopy.ts`) re-exported a type (`export type { ReferralChannel }`) — same invariant this project already learned the hard way once before (`actions/careerEvents.ts`, Phase 15) — caused a real client-side `ReferenceError: ReferralChannel is not defined` on every Settings page load. Fixed by importing the type directly from `lib/referralCopy.ts` at every call site instead of re-exporting it from the action file.
  - **A scary-looking "bug" chased at length, then fully root-caused as a testing-tool artifact, not a real defect**: `/find-jobs/[id]` appeared to render only the navbar, both locally and on a completely fresh production deploy — the real content was present in the DOM the whole time, correctly, just stuck inside Next.js's own streaming-reveal container (`<div id="S:0" hidden>`). Root-caused by manually calling the already-loaded reveal function from the console (`window.$RV(window.$RB)`) — it worked instantly, proving the app's own logic was always correct. Real cause: this session's Browser pane was never displayed/composited (confirmed via a `screenshot` call's own error message), and `requestAnimationFrame` — what Next's streaming reveal schedules through — never fires for a non-composited document. A real visible user tab reveals within one frame; this only manifests in this exact background-testing configuration. **No code fix needed.**
  - **Session redirect, mid-build, from the user**: "complete the admin panel functions as we can today and... back to the user end features which we still have in the pipeline" — actioned by finishing admin-side verification, then push+deploy, then returning to consumer-side testing (the phantom-bug investigation above doubled as confirming the job-detail Outreach Signal card renders correctly on real production).
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint .` clean project-wide. 5 new migrations applied directly to the shared InsForge backend. **Committed (`b0389a1`), pushed to `origin/feature/my-experiment`, and deployed to production** (`npx vercel --prod --scope sortie3`) — post-deploy checklist all green (`PUT /api/inngest` → `200` modified:true, `GET /api/inngest` → `401`, `GET /admin`/`GET /career` → `307`, `GET /`/`GET /blog` → `200`). Still open: the referral capture/claim flow needs a second real account to test honestly — the self-referral-rejection guard is the one path a single account can verify.

Service worker (`public/sw.js`, already shipped for push) now registers globally on every page load via a new loader, not just when a user opens Settings → Push.
