  - **Toast notification system** (§H) — `ToastProvider`/`useToast` infra mounted at root layout, wired into `AddToCompareButton.tsx` as a real proof of concept rather than a risky mass retrofit of every existing inline message in the app.
  - **A real reactivity bug caught and fixed before it shipped**: `EmailDrafts.tsx`'s original deep-link effect read `window.location.search` with a mount-only `useEffect([])` — since `FollowUpNudge.tsx` lives on the SAME page and only changes the URL's query string (no remount), the deep-link would have silently failed to switch tabs when clicked from an already-loaded page. Fixed by switching to Next.js's `useSearchParams()` hook with `searchParams` in the effect's dependency array, so it reacts to the query-string-only navigation instead of only firing on initial mount. Caught via code review before deploy, then independently confirmed correct with a real click-through on production (clicked "Draft a follow-up," verified the Follow-up tab actually got selected and the URL param was cleaned up).
  - **Live-verified end to end on real production for all 6 pieces**: welcome tour shown/dismissed with the flag persisting; a real toast rendered and read via a timing-safe two-step check (naive checks kept missing it — the 3-second auto-dismiss window is shorter than this tool's round-trip latency); skill-gap tracker showed real counts (Python 18, Java 16, Spring Boot 11, React 8, Kubernetes 7 across 58 real evaluated postings) and a real synthesis citing those exact numbers; 90-day plan and follow-up nudge both confirmed against a real job temporarily flipped to the right status for testing, then fully reverted afterward (status + every test-generated AI field cleared).
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint .` clean project-wide. Committed (`ce929c6`), pushed, deployed, Inngest re-synced (`modified: true`, picked up the new cron), post-deploy checked.

  - **Real, live, end-to-end verification, further than the email round-trip could go**: this session's own dev-server browser had notification permission genuinely denied by sandbox policy (explained to the user directly — real end-user browsers show the native OS/browser permission prompt normally, this is specific to the automated test environment), but a real subscription with a genuine `fcm.googleapis.com` endpoint was captured earlier in the session and used as a real send target. Confirmed via the Inngest dev server's own API (`localhost:8288`, started fresh this session — a real, previously-undocumented environment step, needed because `INNGEST_DEV=1` means nothing serves that locally by default) that the event was received, the function ran to `Completed` status with no error, and — the strongest signal available without visually observing a notification — the subscription was NOT deleted as stale, which only happens on a genuine 404/410 dead-subscription response from Google's push service. Also directly verified: service worker registers and activates for real, the zero-subscriber case sends cleanly with no error.

Worth remembering for any future `step.run()` call in this file.

Live-verified with a real token (correctly flipped the flag, confirmed via `db query`, then reverted the test account back to `false`) and a bogus token (correctly `404`s, doesn't crash).

A draft-only edit lock (`status !== "draft"` disables every field) prevents editing a broadcast after it's been sent.

The Inngest chunk-send loop itself is code-reviewed/type-checked, not click-verified end-to-end, disclosed honestly rather than assumed equal-confidence to the rest.

- **2026-08-19 (Phase 17, continued): Admin console expansion item 4, Support (`/admin/support` inbox + a real user-facing submission point in Settings), shipped.**
  - New `support_tickets` + `support_ticket_messages` tables (migration `20260819090000_support-tickets-tables.sql`) — a real child table for messages, not agy's JSONB-array shortcut, matching this app's own `admin_notes`/`admin_audit_log` pattern. **The one table pair this session where RLS is real, not "zero policies, service-role only"**: a normal user creates/reads their own tickets through the regular cookie-authenticated client (`auth.uid() = user_id` policies), while `/admin/support` reads everything through the service-role client (bypasses RLS, same as the rest of `/admin`). `support_ticket_messages` carries a denormalized `user_id` (copied from the parent ticket) specifically so its own policy is a direct equality check, not a cross-table subquery against another RLS-enabled table — avoided the SECURITY DEFINER helper-function route entirely by design.
  - **One real design decision worth remembering**: reopening a ticket to `open` when the user replies is a system-maintained field, not user-authored content, so `actions/support.ts`'s `replyToMySupportTicket` writes it through the service-role client (`createAdminDbClient()`) *after* the RLS-protected read already proved the user owns that ticket — deliberately not a full "own-row UPDATE" RLS policy, which would have also let a user directly write `assigned_admin_id` (more surface than needed).
  - Two new action files: `actions/support.ts` (user-facing, `requireUser()`, no admin role check) and `actions/adminSupport.ts` (admin-facing, `requireAdmin()`/`requireRole()` — reply/status/assign gated owner+admin, `support_readonly` gets full read access matching its "read everywhere, zero writes" role definition). `lib/admin/support.ts` reuses the exact two-hop email-lookup pattern from `lib/admin/queries.ts`'s `getAdminNotes()` (no direct FK-embed to `profiles`/`admin_users` through this project's PostgREST layer).
  - User-facing UI lives as a new "Contact support" tab inside `SettingsPanel.tsx` (`components/settings/SupportTab.tsx`) — not a standalone page, matching how "Browser extension" already hosts a comparably self-contained UI in the same slot, and keeping the main nav from growing another top-level item. Admin side: `components/admin/{SupportInbox,SupportTicketDetail}.tsx`, `app/admin/support/{page,[id]/page}.tsx`.
  - **A real bug caught and fixed during live verification, not just planned**: the admin ticket-detail component originally hand-patched local state after a reply (`setTicket(prev => ({...prev, status: "pending"}))`), which correctly updated the status badge but left the "Assigned" label showing "Unassigned" even though the reply action had genuinely set `assigned_admin_id` server-side (confirmed via `db query` — the DB write was correct, only the client render was stale). Fixed by replacing every hand-patch with a real refetch of the full ticket detail after any write (reply/status-change/assign) — a reply can touch more than one field server-side, so partial optimistic patches will keep drifting from real state as more fields get added.
  - **Live-verified end to end, the full real lifecycle**: created a real ticket as the test user (confirmed via `db query` the RLS-protected insert actually worked), saw it appear in the admin inbox with the right resolved email, opened it and replied as admin (confirmed status flipped to "pending" and `assigned_admin_id` was set), saw the reply appear on the user side with status "Awaiting reply," replied as the user (confirmed status flipped back to "open" via the service-role write), confirmed that reply appears back on the admin side — then deleted the test ticket (`ON DELETE CASCADE` took the message with it) and confirmed both tables back to 0 rows.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean on every touched file.

- **2026-08-19 (Phase 17, continued): Support fast-follows, direct user request — real email round-trip (outbound + inbound, inert until a domain) plus manual ticket creation, delete, and reassign-to-any-admin.**
  - **Email round-trip, built now, activates later with zero code changes**: `resend@6.20.0` installed. `lib/email/resend.ts`'s `sendSupportReplyEmail()` fires (via `after()`, same pattern as `last_viewed_at`) whenever an admin replies, using a `support+{ticketId}@{SUPPORT_INBOUND_DOMAIN}` Reply-To so a plain email-client reply threads straight back into the right ticket. `app/api/webhooks/resend-inbound/route.ts` receives Resend's `email.received` webhook (Svix-signature-verified via `resend.webhooks.verify()`), fetches the real body via `resend.emails.receiving.get()` (webhooks only carry metadata, confirmed via Resend's own docs before writing any code — not guessed), matches the sender against `profiles.email`, and either appends to the ticket encoded in the reply-to address or opens a new one. All DB writes go through the service-role client (a webhook has no user session to run RLS as, trust comes entirely from the verified signature).
  - **Real, live-confirmed limitation, not assumed**: ran a direct test send against the real Resend API — confirmed pre-domain-verification, Resend restricts delivery to only the account owner's own verified email (`ridham.doshi.rd@gmail.com`), rejecting even the test Sortie account's own real email with a 403. Matches exactly what was disclosed to the user before building this. The reply action still saves correctly when the email fails (confirmed live) — the send is fire-and-forget, never blocks the real DB write.
  - **Activation checklist, once a domain is purchased** (documented in `lib/email/resend.ts`'s own comment, no re-deriving needed): verify the domain in Resend, enable Receiving, create an `email.received` webhook pointed at `/api/webhooks/resend-inbound`, set `SUPPORT_FROM_EMAIL`/`SUPPORT_INBOUND_DOMAIN`/`RESEND_WEBHOOK_SECRET`.
  - **Manual ticket creation** (`/admin/support/new`, direct user request) — an admin logs a real support contact that didn't come through the app (a call, a walk-up), picking an existing Sortie account by email (same "must already have an account" pattern as Team & Roles) — the opening note is authored as the admin's own note, not impersonating the user's voice. Auto-assigns to the creating admin.
  - **Delete ticket and reassign-to-any-admin** (direct user request, "different action options which needed there") — `deleteTicket()` (owner+admin, `ConfirmDialog`-gated) and `reassignTicket()` (a real `<select>` of the admin roster, not just the previous "assign to me" one-shot). `AdminTicketRow` gained a real `assignedAdminId` field so the reassign dropdown reflects true state instead of a fragile email-string match.
  - **Live-verified end to end**: manual creation (real ticket appeared, correctly auto-assigned, opening note correctly attributed), reassign-to-unassigned (confirmed via `db query`, UI reflected it on reload), delete (confirmed both `support_tickets` and cascaded `support_ticket_messages` rows gone). All test data cleaned up.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean on every touched file.

No admin UI for this — future redirects get added directly to the array.

Same RLS lockdown as every other admin table (enabled, zero policies, service-role client only) — the public route doesn't get its own anonymous-read policy; `lib/admin/content.ts`'s `getPublishedPageBySlug()`/`listPublishedPages()` instead read through the same service-role client with an explicit `status = 'published'` filter baked into the query (server-only code, never exposed to the browser) — simpler than a real RLS policy for one narrow always-filtered path, and just as safe.

  - **Live-verified end to end, not just type-checked**: created a real test page, generated a real AI draft live (Gemini call actually fired, markdown came back well-formed, rendered correctly through the new preview component — headings/paragraphs/list all confirmed), saved (confirmed via direct `db query`), confirmed the public `/blog/{slug}` route correctly 404s while still a draft, published it, re-confirmed the same URL now renders live with the right title/date/body and the blog index (`/blog`) lists it, confirmed the editor's button correctly flips to "Unpublish" on reload (state persisted, not just client-side optimism), then deleted and confirmed `pages` is back to 0 rows.

AI cost 30d, est. total burn), an add-expense form + table with Remove, and a per-action rate table with inline provider/rate editing (owner-only) and a live Save button gated on an actual dirty-check.

Added a real test expense ($26/mo) and confirmed both the recurring-total and burn-total stat cards recalculated live; edited a rate live (0→1.2¢ on `resume_analysis`) and confirmed the DB write, cost recompute, and re-sort-by-cost all happened correctly. All test data (the $26 expense, the rate edit) reverted and confirmed clean via a direct `db query` afterward — no test debris left in the live system.

Starting a second `next dev` instance on a different port (3002) still failed — confirmed live that Next.js 16/Turbopack enforces a single dev-server instance **per directory**, not per port, regardless of the `-p` flag. Verified this session's own code changes instead by navigating this session's Browser pane directly at the other session's already-running `localhost:3001` (same on-disk codebase, hot-reloaded by Turbopack), rather than killing another session's active server.

- **2026-08-19 (Phase 16, continued): a real, significant production auth bug found and fixed — `proxy.ts`'s middleware matcher was silently excluding most authenticated routes from session refresh.** User-reported: signed in with the admin test account on production, but `/admin` kept bouncing to the homepage as if unauthorized.
  - **Root cause**: `proxy.ts`'s middleware (`updateSession()`, which silently refreshes an expiring 15-minute access token using the 7-day refresh token) only ran on 3 route prefixes — `/dashboard`, `/profile`, `/find-jobs`. Every other authenticated route (`/admin`, `/missions`, `/saved-jobs`, `/career`, `/interview`, `/resume`, `/cover-letter`, `/settings`, `/notifications`, `/jobs/external`, `/waitlist`, `/api/settings/me`) never ran it — confirmed via `grep`ping every `requireUser()`/`requireAdmin()` call site against the matcher list. If a user's access token expired while they were on the app (e.g. browsing for >15 minutes) and they then navigated to any of these uncovered routes, the page saw no valid session and silently bounced them — indistinguishable from "not actually authorized," even though their refresh token was still fully valid.
  - **Reproduced and root-caused live, not guessed**: signed in via a real `curl` POST to `/api/auth/signin` against production, decoded the returned JWT to confirm the `sub` claim exactly matched the `admin_users.user_id` row in the live DB (ruling out a data/permissions issue), then deployed a temporary diagnostic build of `requireAdmin()` and pulled real Vercel function logs showing `getCurrentUser()` genuinely found no user — pointing at the auth layer, not the admin table. Traced to the middleware matcher by inspecting `proxy.ts` directly.
  - **Fix and verification, not just a guess**: expanded `matcher` to cover all 14 authenticated route prefixes. Verified with the strongest possible test — sent a request to `/admin` with a deliberately invalid/garbage access token alongside a real, valid refresh token cookie (simulating the exact post-expiry state that was broken): before the fix this would 307-redirect to `/`; after the fix it returns `200 OK` with a fresh `Set-Cookie: insforge_access_token=...` in the response, proving the refresh path now actually runs.
  - Diagnostic logging added to `requireAdmin()` during investigation was fully reverted before the real fix shipped — confirmed via `git diff` showing zero residual change to that file.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean. Deployed to production.

- **2026-08-19 (Phase 16, continued): real mobile-responsive bug found and fixed on Missions, plus a systemic safety net added — direct user request to audit job-detail + Missions at real mobile widths.**
  - **Root cause, a real flexbox gotcha, not obvious from any single file**: the root layout's `<body>` (`app/layout.tsx`) is `flex flex-col` (shared by every page). Missions' Kanban board (`components/missions/KanbanBoard.tsx`) already had its own `overflow-x-auto` row for its fixed-width (`w-72 shrink-0`) columns — but a flex-column *item* defaults to `min-width: auto`, so without `min-w-0` at every ancestor in the chain **and** an explicit `w-full` on the page's own `<main>`, the board's natural content width (1440px+) silently stretched every ancestor up through `<body>` instead of scrolling within its own row. Confirmed live at 375px: page-wide horizontal overflow of 1065px before the fix, `0` after — traced ancestor-by-ancestor via `getComputedStyle`/`getBoundingClientRect` in the browser, not guessed. `min-w-0` alone (added to `KanbanBoardLoader.tsx` and `MissionsView.tsx`'s root) was necessary but not sufficient — `<main>` itself (`app/missions/page.tsx`) needed an explicit `w-full` too, confirmed by forcing the style at runtime first and watching overflow drop to 0 before committing the real fix.
  - **A systemic safety net added, not just the one-off fix**: `app/layout.tsx`'s `<body>` now has `overflow-x-hidden` — clips any *future* similarly-wide descendant at the true root instead of letting it silently open a page-wide scrollbar, without affecting any element's own intentional internal scroll (Kanban's own `overflow-x-auto` row still works, confirmed: `scrollWidth 1504 / clientWidth 343` after the fix, still real horizontal drag-scroll).
  - **Same `w-full`/`min-w-0` fix applied to `/find-jobs/[id]`'s `<main>` by direct code-level analogy** (same shared `flex flex-col` body, same per-page `<main>` pattern missing `w-full`) — **not live-verified**, disclosed honestly: this session's local dev environment could not render `/find-jobs/[id]` at all (stuck indefinitely on its loading skeleton, in this specific browser-automation session only — ruled out via a fully clean tab, a full dev-server + `.next` cache wipe, re-authentication, and confirming every network request/chunk actually succeeded with zero console errors; production confirmed working fine by the user directly). Given the exact same structural bug pattern was confirmed and fixed on Missions, applying the identical fix to job-detail's structurally-identical `<main>` is a reasoned, low-risk fix, but flagged as code-review-confidence, not click-verified, until the user or a future session confirms it live.
  - **A real, separate bug found along the way, not fixed this pass**: `/dashboard`'s Recent Activity feed silently drops all "jobs found" entries — `app/dashboard/page.tsx` queries `agent_runs.completed_at`, a column that does not exist in the live table (confirmed via `information_schema.columns`; real columns are `started_at`/`created_at`/`updated_at`). Doesn't crash the page (the existing `?? []` fallback absorbs the query error), just silently under-shows the activity feed. Flagged, not fixed — separate bug, out of scope for this pass.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean on every touched file.

- **2026-08-19 (Phase 16, continued): full session regression audit, requested directly by the user after 3 real mistakes surfaced same-day (stale backlog offers, "Ask Orion" naming, the duplicate job-chat feature).** Diffed every commit this session (`5c215db..HEAD`) against pre-session `HEAD`, file by file, separating genuinely new files (low risk) from modified pre-existing files (real regression risk).
  - **One real regression found and fixed**: `instrumentation-client.ts` pre-existed this session and its entire job was calling `initPostHog()` (the only call site in the whole codebase — confirmed via grep). Wiring up Sentry's client init this session **overwrote the file wholesale instead of adding to it**, silently deleting the PostHog init call. Client-side PostHog analytics (pageview tracking, exception capture, `identifyPostHogUser`/`resetPostHogUser` elsewhere in the app) had been a no-op with zero errors since commit `c07d4bc` — `posthog-js` does nothing until `.init()` runs. Fixed by restoring the original `initPostHog()` call alongside Sentry's, verified via `tsc`/`eslint` clean and direct code comparison against the pre-session version (git show `5c215db:instrumentation-client.ts`). Could not live-verify against a real PostHog key in this environment (`.env.local` has none configured, so `initPostHog()` correctly no-ops locally either way) — confidence is code-review-verified-identical-to-working-original, not live-clicked.
  - **Everything else checked came back clean**: `lib/usage.ts` (the kill-switch/multiplier rework — confirmed `app_settings` seeds a real default row with `ai_enabled: true` and every `profiles` row gets `custom_usage_multiplier` defaulted to `1`, so pre-existing users see zero behavior change), `lib/interviewQuestions.ts` (`.max()`→`.transform()` is strictly more permissive, can't newly reject anything that passed before), `lib/practiceSandbox.ts`/`components/interview/PracticeSandbox.tsx` (the `SandboxResult` shape change is fully consistent across worker/consumer, the SQL `"NO_RESULT_ROWS"` sentinel is correctly unwrapped back to its original friendly message text in the UI), `actions/jobs.ts`/`types/index.ts`/`lib/errors.ts` (purely additive), `app/saved-jobs/page.tsx` (the `SavedJobsTabs` replacement correctly preserves `reappearanceSignal`/`index` per card — the only behavior delta is the entrance-animation stagger restarting per-tab instead of continuing a global index, cosmetic only), all 3 new migrations (only `ADD COLUMN`/new-table `ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`, zero `REVOKE`/`DROP`/altered grants on any pre-existing table).
  - **One separate gap flagged, then closed same session**: the `app_settings_select_all` RLS policy (needed for the AI kill switch to be readable by normal users at all) was applied live via `db query`, not captured in any committed migration file — same already-documented "`db query` changes don't land in migrations" pattern this project has hit repeatedly. New migration `20260819060000_app-settings-select-policy.sql` catches version control up to real state — `DROP POLICY IF EXISTS` then `CREATE POLICY`, safe to apply on top of the already-live policy (confirmed the exact live definition via `pg_policies` before writing it, per the new check-before-building rule this same audit produced). Applied via the standard migrations-tracking workaround (temporarily moved the other 60 migration files out of `migrations/`, ran `db migrations up --all`, moved them back) — verified post-apply that `pg_policies` still shows exactly one policy on `app_settings`, matching pre-migration state exactly, not a duplicate.

A 4th ("Ask Navigator" job chat) was built, then found to duplicate Navigator itself and removed — see its own entry below.** Follows a real self-caught process failure: the first shortlist offered 3 already-built items (Recently Viewed, external-job add, STAR Vault) as if unbuilt — the background agent that compiled the backlog trusted `build-plan.md`'s own 🆕 markers instead of cross-checking the live codebase, the same stale-marker failure mode already documented twice earlier in this file (Correctable skill tags, Job requirements split). Every init is gated on its own env var (`SENTRY_DSN` server/edge, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN` client) being set — a fresh clone or this deploy today, with no Sentry project configured, never calls `Sentry.init()` at all and never reaches Sentry's ingest endpoint, by design, not a try/catch swallow. `withSentryConfig` itself is safe to apply with no `SENTRY_ORG`/`SENTRY_PROJECT`/`SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` set too — it just skips source-map upload with a warning, confirmed via a real `npm run build` (all 49 routes compiled clean, no failure).

Removed before it could break anything — caught by checking the real installed types, not trusted from training-data memory of an older SDK version.

The code is inert and harmless until then.

  - **"Why I Left" — private post-rejection reflection** (`components/job-details/WhyILeftReflection.tsx`, new `jobs.reflection_loved`/`jobs.reflection_avoid` text columns via migration `20260819051805_why-i-left-reflection.sql`, `actions/jobs.ts`'s `updateJobReflection()`). Two blur-to-save textareas ("What I'd want again" / "What to avoid next time"), rendered only when `job.application_status === "rejected"` — a private self-reflection tool, not shown anywhere else, no AI involved. **A false-alarm caught during verification, not a real bug**: after saving via real `computer` keystrokes, `read_page`'s accessibility tree still showed the textareas' placeholder text as their "name" — looked like a save failure. Verified via a direct `document.querySelectorAll('textarea')[...].value` check that the actual DOM value was correctly populated; this was a display artifact of the accessibility-tree extraction (it surfaces placeholder as name regardless of real `.value`), not a functional bug. Also hit and worked around a real limitation this session: synthetic `dispatchEvent('input')` via the native value setter didn't reliably update React's controlled-input state (Save button stayed disabled) — real `computer` click+type actions did.

- **2026-08-19 (Phase 16, continued): Admin Panel v1.1 shipped** — the fast-follows explicitly deferred out of v1 (admin_notes, a full searchable/paginated user table, a per-user detail view, a global AI kill switch, and the two deferred `profiles` levers `custom_usage_multiplier`/`feature_flags`), all now built.
  - **New migration** (`20260819031051_admin-panel-v1-1-tables.sql`): `admin_notes` (same zero-anon/authenticated-policy RLS shape as `admin_users`/`admin_audit_log`), `app_settings` (a singleton table, `id` pinned to `1` via a CHECK constraint, holding the global `ai_enabled` flag), and `profiles.custom_usage_multiplier`/`profiles.feature_flags`. The existing `prevent_profiles_admin_field_change` trigger from v1 was extended (not duplicated) to guard both new columns the same way it already guarded `is_suspended`.
  - **A real RLS gap found and fixed before it mattered**: `app_settings` was created with the same "zero policies for anon/authenticated" lockdown as the other admin tables — but `lib/usage.ts`'s `checkAndConsumeUsage` runs on the normal cookie-authenticated client (every user needs to read the global flag to know if they're blocked), not the service-role client. Fixed with one added read-only policy (`app_settings_select_all`, `FOR SELECT TO authenticated USING (true)`) — readable by anyone logged in, writable only by the service-role client (project_admin), matching the "public read, admin write" pattern from this project's own access-control guide.
  - **Global AI kill switch** (`components/admin/AiKillSwitch.tsx`, `actions/admin.ts`'s `setAiEnabled`) — checked in `checkAndConsumeUsage` BEFORE per-user suspension and BEFORE the `ADMIN_EMAILS` exemption, with deliberately NO exceptions when off (a real operational lever for a runaway-cost incident needs to stop every call, including the founder's own admin-exempt account, not just most users). **Live-verified via a real isolated test against the actual function**: confirmed an `ADMIN_EMAILS`-listed account is still blocked when the switch is off, and that the custom disabled-reason text is surfaced correctly.
  - **`custom_usage_multiplier`** scales `DAILY_LIMITS` per user (`Math.max(1, Math.floor(limit * multiplier))` — floor of 1, since zeroing someone out is what suspend is for, not a 0x multiplier). **Live-verified with a real isolated test**: a user at count=2 against a 5/day limit is correctly blocked at a 0.5x multiplier (effective limit 2) and correctly allowed at 1x — the returned error message even reflects the scaled limit ("2/day"), not the base one.
  - **Full user table** (`app/admin/users/page.tsx`, `components/admin/UsersTable.tsx`) — server-side paginated via `.range()` (not fetch-all-then-slice, a real gotcha agy flagged: fine today, a crash risk at a few thousand users), searchable by email/full name via `.ilike()`/`.or()`.
  - **Per-user detail page** (`app/admin/users/[userId]/page.tsx`, `components/admin/UserDetailView.tsx`) — the "God-Mode Read-Only View" from the original §R spec: overview, a 14-day usage trend chart, the suspend/usage-multiplier levers, and admin notes (add + list, joined through `admin_notes` → `admin_users` → `profiles` for the authoring admin's email since InsForge's PostgREST layer doesn't expose implicit FK-embed joins the way this app's other reads assume).
  - **A real hydration-mismatch bug found and fixed via live testing, not assumed**: `UserDetailView`'s join-date formatter used `toLocaleString(undefined, {hour12: true, ...})`, which renders "9:12 PM" server-side (Node) but "9:12 p.m." client-side (browser) — a genuine ICU dayPeriod-formatting divergence between environments that pinning an explicit locale does NOT fix. Fixed by switching to a 24-hour clock (`hour12: false`), which has no dayPeriod token to diverge on — confirmed via a real server restart + fresh page load with the fix in place, verified clean in the dev server's own log (no hydration error attached to the two most recent renders, versus the error present on the one render before the fix landed).
  - **A real stale-Turbopack-graph gotcha hit and fixed the established way**: a earlier `export type { AppSettings }` re-export from `actions/admin.ts` (a `"use server"` file — only async functions may be exported from these) caused a real, persistent "Export AppSettings doesn't exist" compile error that survived a source fix, a file-touch nudge, AND a hard browser reload — only a full dev-server restart (`taskkill` + fresh `next dev`) cleared it, matching this project's own documented Phase 9 precedent for this exact bug class. Removed the invalid re-export (unnecessary — the one real consumer already imported the type from `lib/admin/queries.ts` directly).
  - **Every lever live-verified against the real DB and real UI, not assumed**: multiplier save confirmed in `profiles` + `admin_audit_log`; kill-switch and multiplier-scaling logic each verified with a real isolated Node script exercising the actual `checkAndConsumeUsage` function; admin note add confirmed to persist and to render with the correct authoring-admin email resolved through the two-hop join. All test data (multiplier reset to 1, test note deleted) cleaned up after.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint .` (whole project) clean.

User explicitly confirmed the free version over the expensive original.
  - `lib/marketReadiness.ts` + `actions/marketReadiness.ts` + `components/career/MarketReadiness.tsx` — same opt-in/never-eager/usage-capped shape as `lib/outcomeNarrative.ts`/`lib/bragDoc.ts`: one structured Gemini call over the user's own logged `accomplishments` (title/date/tags, last 30) plus their own stated `job_titles_seeking`, surfacing 1-2 grounded observations — never a fabricated market benchmark, and required to compare against the user's own stated targets when they've set any ("your last 6 months skew toward data infrastructure, not the frontend roles you've listed — intentional?"), or surface the next most useful pattern if not. New `market_readiness` usage action (5/day, same cap as `brag_doc`). Gated on ≥3 logged accomplishments — same `MIN_SAMPLE_SIZE`-style honesty convention as `lib/outcomeInsights.ts` (a "trend" off 1-2 data points is noise dressed as a pattern).

- **2026-08-19 (Phase 16, continued): Internal Admin Panel v1 shipped** — build-plan.md §R, researched (Perplexity + 2 agy passes across 3 sessions), deferred until asked, this session was the ask. A fresh agy re-review of the existing plan recommended cutting multi-admin/audit-log for a leaner v1 — **explicitly overridden, not applied**: that's the same simplification a prior session already rejected for a real reason (someone else needs to run this while the founder is away, and multi-person access without an audit trail is a real accountability gap). Everything else from that review (a global-kill-switch idea for later, hard-delete for later, server-side pagination noted for a future full user table, and the security gotchas below) was kept.
  - **New migration** (`20260819001453_admin-panel-v1-tables.sql`): `admin_users` (user_id FK auth.users, role enum owner/admin/support_readonly) and `admin_audit_log` (admin_user_id FK admin_users, action, target_user_id/table/id, before/after jsonb, note) — both RLS-enabled with **zero policies for anon/authenticated**, deliberately: only the service-role client (runs as `project_admin`, bypasses RLS as table owner) can touch them, so who-has-admin-access can never be self-servable through the normal app connection. `profiles.is_suspended` — the actual v1 intervention lever.
  - **A real security gap found and closed before it ever shipped**: `profiles`' existing RLS policy (`profiles_update_own`, `auth.uid() = id`) has no column-level restriction — the default broad `UPDATE` grant would let a user flip `is_suspended` back to `false` on themselves via a raw SDK call, completely undermining the lever. Fixed with a `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger (`prevent_profiles_admin_field_change`) that blocks any change to `is_suspended` unless the caller is `project_admin` — per this project's own access-control guide's "protected status field" pattern, chosen over enumerating every other legitimate profile column in a GRANT allowlist (safer against a future omission). **Live-verified as a real attack, not assumed**: extracted the test account's own JWT from `document.cookie` (`insforge_access_token`, not httpOnly) and issued a direct authenticated PATCH straight against the InsForge REST API attempting `is_suspended: false` — got back a real `400` with `"is_suspended can only be changed by an admin"`, confirmed the guard holds even completely outside the app's own UI.
  - **`lib/admin/`**: `client.ts` (`createAdminClient({apiKey: INSFORGE_API_KEY})`, the "God-Mode" service-role client), `auth.ts` (`requireAdmin()` — throws `AdminAuthError`, never redirects internally, so it works identically inside `app/admin/layout.tsx` (catches → redirects to `/`) and inside every Server Action in `actions/admin.ts` (own try/catch → `toUserMessage`) — a layout only blocks the rendered UI, it does NOT stop a Server Action from being called directly, a real gotcha flagged by agy's review and treated as load-bearing here, not decorative), `audit.ts` (`logAdminAction()`, fire-and-forget on write failure, same resilience pattern as the rest of this app), `queries.ts` (top-users-by-usage leaderboard, signups/usage 14-day trend series, all client-side JS aggregation over fetched rows — same pattern as `lib/outcomeInsights.ts`, no RPC/GROUP BY needed at this project's scale).
  - **`lib/usage.ts`'s `checkAndConsumeUsage` now checks `is_suspended` first, before the existing `ADMIN_EMAILS` exemption** — enforced at the one real choke point every AI-costing action already routes through, not just cosmetically shown in the admin UI (the exact gotcha agy flagged: checking suspension only in a layout lets a user keep clicking around on stale client-side cache). **Live-verified with a real Node script exercising the actual function** (not the UI) against three cases: a suspended user is blocked with the right message; a suspended user whose email IS in `ADMIN_EMAILS` is STILL blocked (proves suspension overrides the admin exemption, the actual design intent); a normal non-suspended user is unaffected and still records usage.
  - **`/admin`** (`app/admin/layout.tsx` + `page.tsx`, `export const dynamic = "force-dynamic"` so it's never served from Next's cache): single page, no route-module split yet (agy's build-order advice, kept) — 3 stat cards, 2 recharts `LineChart` trend cards (`components/admin/TrendChart.tsx`, same design-token conventions as `components/dashboard/AnalyticsCharts.tsx`), and the top-users-by-usage table (`components/admin/AdminDashboard.tsx`) with a Suspend/Unsuspend action per row (`ConfirmDialog` reused for the destructive Suspend confirmation, no confirm needed to Unsuspend).
  - **A real color-token mistake caught and fixed before shipping, not after**: initially used `var(--color-agent)` for the usage-trend chart's line color — wrong, that token is this app's strict "genuinely AI-generated content" signal (match reasoning, research findings), not a general "this is AI-usage-related" theming choice. Corrected to `var(--color-info)`, a neutral second-series color, before ever rendering.
  - **Full end-to-end live verification, every real path exercised**: signed in as the test account, granted it `admin_users` access via `db query`, confirmed the gate blocks an unauthenticated visitor (redirects to `/`) and admits the granted account; seeded a real `usage_daily` row and confirmed it appeared correctly in the leaderboard; suspended the account through the real UI, confirmed the DB row changed AND a real `admin_audit_log` row was written with correct before/after; confirmed the suspension-attack-bypass trigger test above; unsuspended through the real UI, confirmed the DB reverted. All seeded test data cleaned up after.

  - **What's deliberately NOT in this v1**, per agy's build-order advice, additive later per §R's own "each module is additive" structure: `admin_notes`, `profiles.custom_usage_multiplier`/`feature_flags`, a full searchable/paginated user table, per-user detail tabs, route-module splitting, and a global AI kill switch (agy's new suggestion this pass — worth a fast follow, not built).

- **2026-08-18 (Phase 16, continued): fixed the real pre-existing bug flagged in Phase 15 but not fixed there — behavioral-question study cards crashing with a raw Zod validation error.** Picked up autonomously while Java/C# were blocked on user input, per the "work through the already-diagnosed backlog" pattern, not a new feature.
  - **Root cause**: `lib/interviewQuestions.ts`'s `questionDetailsSchema` used `.max(2)` on `redFlags` and `.max(3)` on `edgeCases` — the AI is prompted for "up to 2"/"up to 3" but doesn't always comply, and `.max()` makes Zod's `.parse()` reject (throw) the ENTIRE response the moment it over-generates by even one item, rather than just being over-generous.
  - **Fix**: both changed from `.max(N)` (reject) to `.transform((arr) => arr.slice(0, N))` (truncate) — an over-long array from the AI now silently truncates to the intended length instead of crashing the whole generation.
  - **A second, more general bug found in the same investigation**: even with the truncation fix, `lib/errors.ts`'s `toUserMessage()` had no case for `ZodError` at all — Zod v4's `ZodError.message` is a raw JSON blob of issues by default, which doesn't match any of the denylist's vendor/stack-trace-shaped regexes, so it was passing straight through to the user un-rewritten (confirmed live: the raw JSON literally is `[{"origin":"array","code":"too_big",...}]`). Fixed with a direct `error instanceof ZodError` check, ahead of the regex rules — more precise than trying to pattern-match Zod's message shape, and now covers any future schema validation failure anywhere in the app, not just this one field.
  - **Both fixes independently verified by actually running them**, not assumed from reading the Zod API: a real Node script confirmed the transform truncates a 4-item array to 2 without throwing, and confirmed `toUserMessage()` now returns the clean fallback string instead of the raw JSON blob for a deliberately-triggered `ZodError`.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean.

- **2026-08-18 (Phase 16, continued): Java added to the Practice Sandbox — researched and live-tested in real depth, code NOT yet written, blocked on 2 things only the user can resolve.**
  - **Licensing resolved, genuinely free**: CheerpJ's Community License (confirmed via their real licensing page, not just a search snippet) explicitly covers "commercial applications developed by one-person companies... whether they generate income or not." Sortie is solo, confirmed by the user — this qualifies. The one condition: load the CheerpJ engine from Leaning Technologies' own CDN (`https://cjrtnc.leaningtech.com/4.3/loader.js`), never self-host those runtime files — same pattern this app already uses for Pyodide/ruby.wasm/sql.js.
  - **A real, separate dependency surfaced that the licensing research didn't cover**: compiling raw Java *source* (not a pre-built `.jar`) needs `tools.jar` (javac's own classes) — and Leaning Technologies does NOT host that file on their public CDN (confirmed by probing every guessed CDN path directly — all returned empty `204`s). Their own demo (JavaFiddle) self-hosts its own copy. This project would need to do the same — host a real, legitimately-obtained `tools.jar` (a standard, freely-redistributable OpenJDK 8 artifact, GPL-with-classpath-exception) as a static asset. **Blocked on the user supplying it** from their own JDK 8 install (`$JAVA_HOME/lib/tools.jar`) at `public/practice-sandbox/tools.jar` — deliberately not fetched from an unverified third-party source.
  - **A real architectural conflict found via live testing, not assumed**: read JavaFiddle's actual open-source implementation (`leaningtech/javafiddle` on GitHub, Apache-2.0) to get the exact real API shape — `cheerpjInit({status:'none'})`, `cheerpjAddStringFile(path, encodedBytes)` (not `cheerpOSAddStringFile` — a real wrong-function-name mistake caught by reading the actual source instead of guessing), `cheerpjRunMain('com.sun.tools.javac.Main', '/app/tools.jar:/files/', sourcePath, '-d', '/files/')`. Confirmed this exact call **succeeds on the main thread** (`compileExit: 0`, verified live against Leaning Technologies' own real `tools.jar`) but **fails inside a Web Worker** (`compileExit: 1`, "Could not find or load main class") — isolated cleanly by changing only that one variable, everything else identical. This conflicts with this app's established rule that every practice-sandbox language runs in an isolated Worker, never touching the main thread/DOM. **Not resolved unilaterally** — surfaced to the user as a real decision point (run CheerpJ on the main thread as a documented exception vs. don't ship Java) rather than silently breaking the isolation architecture while they were away.
  - Real CDN/API reference recorded here so this doesn't need re-deriving next session: loader script `https://cjrtnc.leaningtech.com/4.3/loader.js`; classpath format is colon-joined (`/app/tools.jar:/files/`); source files must be written via `cheerpjAddStringFile('/str/<name>.java', new TextEncoder().encode(code))` before compiling; a cross-origin absolute-URL classpath entry does NOT work (must be the app's own same-origin `/app/`-relative path) — confirmed by testing both.

- **2026-08-18 (Phase 16): Practice Sandbox reworked from "soft verification" to strict pass/fail grading, plus localStorage persistence and a Console tab — direct user feedback ("I didn't like the way we have implemented the coding practice"), researched via `agy` before any code changed.**
  - **Strict grading, no backend needed**: `lib/practiceSandbox.ts`'s `SandboxResult` now splits `stdout` (console.log/print/puts output) from `returnValue` (the final expression, canonicalized to a string). `gradeAnswer()` runs the reference solution first as runtime ground truth, then the candidate's code against the same test input, and compares — a real "Passed"/"Failed" verdict per test case instead of asking the candidate to eyeball two raw outputs. Falls back to an unlabeled side-by-side (the old behavior) only when the reference solution itself errors, tagged `reference_error` rather than silently claiming a verdict.
  - **JS/TS**: a duplicated-per-worker `canonicalStringify()` sorts object keys before comparing, so two structurally-equal-but-differently-key-ordered objects still grade as a match.
  - **Ruby**: real gotcha found and fixed — `DefaultRubyVM`'s public API has no custom-stdout-callback option, only a boolean `consolePrint` flag; its default printer captures a *reference* to `console.log`/`console.warn` once, at VM-creation time. Fixed by overriding `console.log` at the Ruby worker's top level, before the VM is ever created, so the capturing function is what gets captured — same live-code-reading approach that already caught the `"ruby-wasm-wasi"` export-key gotcha in the original build. **Live-verified in an isolated Worker test, not assumed**: confirmed `puts` output really does route through `console.log`, and caught a real second issue in the same pass — Ruby's own VM-startup boilerplate ("`RubyGems' were not loaded.", etc.) routes through `console.warn`, so an initial version that also overrode `console.warn` (to be safe) leaked that boilerplate into the Console tab on every single run. Fixed by only overriding `console.log`.
  - **SQL**: the existing "col=value" row-string formatter now doubles as the graded `returnValue` directly (already deterministic/canonical), plus a `"NO_RESULT_ROWS"` sentinel so a correctly-empty result still grades against a reference that's also empty, instead of comparing against `null`.
  - **`PracticeSandbox.tsx` rebuilt**: "Run Tests" (was "Run Code") grades every test case in parallel, not just the active one; test-case pills now carry a pass/fail/reference-error icon; a Result/Console tabbed panel replaces the old permanent side-by-side `OutputPanel`s — Console shows the candidate's own captured stdout separately from the graded value, addressing the same critique's "can't debug intermediate state" point.
  - **localStorage persistence**: debounced (500ms) save keyed on `${bankId}:${questionIndex}:${language}`, hydrated on mount — a page nav no longer silently discards typed code. Storage key threaded down from `QuestionBankPanel.tsx`'s `StudyView`, which already had both `bankId`/`questionIndex` in scope.
  - **Java/C# still not built** — researched again this session (agy): Java's CheerpJ can genuinely compile+run raw source client-side but needs a paid commercial license past a 1-person company; C# has no real client-only live-compile path at all (would mean self-hosting a custom Blazor build, not a CDN script). Both would need a self-hosted Piston judge — real infra researched in depth (needs `--privileged` Docker, rules out Railway/Render-style PaaS, realistic host is a Hetzner CX22 VPS ~$4.50/mo, no built-in auth so needs a bearer-token reverse proxy) — but provisioning the actual VPS is an account-creation-plus-payment step outside what this agent can do; user explicitly deferred it to focus on the grading rework first. Revisit once a VPS with SSH access is handed over.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint .` clean.

- **2026-08-18 (Phase 15, Tier 3, same day): Practice Sandbox expanded from 2 to 5 languages — TypeScript, Ruby, and SQL added — per direct user request, plus C#/Java researched and deliberately excluded.**
  - **TypeScript**: transpiles via the real TypeScript compiler loaded from CDN (`ts.transpileModule`, no type-checking — this is a run sandbox), then executes in the same JS Worker.
  - **Ruby**: executes via `ruby.wasm` (the official CRuby WASM port) loaded from CDN inside a Worker — real gotcha hit and fixed live: the UMD bundle exports under the literal string key `"ruby-wasm-wasi"`, not an obvious identifier like `RubyWasmWasi` (confirmed by probing `Object.keys(self)` after `importScripts`, not guessed twice).
  - **SQL**: executes via `sql.js` (SQLite-to-WASM) — required a real architecture adjustment, not just a new worker. SQL doesn't fit the "define a function, call it with fixed arguments" shape every other language here uses (nothing is equivalent to "call the same function with 3 different inputs" for a fixed schema). Redesigned `runSQL()` to run the WHOLE editable buffer as one script and return the last statement's result, rather than forcing SQL into the two-part code+callExpression split — `PracticeKit.functionName` is now optional (meaningless for SQL) and the UI hides the test-case picker/call-expression display for SQL specifically.
  - **C#/Java researched twice via `agy`, per direct user request, and deliberately NOT built.** First pass: both are technically buildable 100% client-side (Roslyn-in-WASM for C#, CheerpJ for Java — CheerpJ can genuinely compile+run arbitrary Java source, not just bytecode) but carry a real 20-30MB cold-boot payload and multi-second init pause on first "Run" click. Second pass, specifically hunting for a lighter-weight alternative (a subset interpreter or a transpile-to-JS approach, the way TypeScript works): confirmed there isn't one — unlike TypeScript (structurally just JS plus type annotations to strip), C#/Java have fundamentally different memory models and standard-library semantics, so every real transpiler (JSweet, TeaVM, H5/Bridge.NET) is a build-time-only tool with no lightweight client-side bundle. The only realistic paths are the heavy client-side compiler or a real server-side execution API (Piston/Judge0) — both a genuine architecture change, not something to build unilaterally. Reported to the user as a real decision point, not built.
  - **Every new execution engine independently verified with real running code before being wired into the UI, not assumed**: TypeScript (`add(2,3)` → `"5"` via a real transpile+execute round trip), Ruby (`add(2,3)` → `"5"` via a real CDN WASM load), SQL (a real `CREATE TABLE`/`INSERT`/`SELECT` round trip returning the correct row). Full UI integration re-verified afterward against a real, different Stripe technical question — confirmed the updated 5-language schema/prompt didn't break the existing JavaScript path (still real, correct side-by-side output).
  - Also surfaced, not fixed (out of scope, flagged only): a real pre-existing bug unrelated to this work — a `BehavioralQuestionDetails` question's study-card generation can fail with a raw, user-facing Zod validation error (`redFlags` array exceeding its own `.max(2)` schema limit) when the AI returns more than 2 items. Worth a dedicated fix in a future session.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint .` (whole project) clean.

- **2026-08-18 (Phase 15, Tier 3): Practice Sandbox (inline code editor) shipped — the last unblocked Tier 3 item, picked directly by the user over the 3 genuinely-blocked ones.**
  - **Only 1 new dependency, not 2**: the original 2026-08-14 feasibility research assumed both `@monaco-editor/react` and `pyodide` would be new npm dependencies. Built with only the first — Python executes via Pyodide loaded lazily from its own CDN *inside* a Web Worker (`importScripts`), not bundled as an npm package, since Pyodide's real payload (~10MB of WASM/data files) isn't in the npm package either; serving those yourself needs webpack/asset config this app doesn't otherwise need.
  - **New `lib/practiceSandbox.ts`** — two Worker-based execution engines (`runJavaScript`, `runPython`), both isolated (own thread, own global scope, 8s timeout that terminates the Worker), sharing one `runInWorker()` helper.
  - **New `PracticeKit` type + `generatePracticeKit()`** (`lib/interviewQuestions.ts`) and **`getPracticeKit()` action** (`actions/interviewQuestions.ts`) — exact same lazy-per-question, cache-hit-is-free-read shape as the existing `QuestionDetails`/`getQuestionDetails()`, just a separate field and a new `practice_kit_generation` usage-metered action (`lib/usage.ts`).
  - **A real design question surfaced live, not assumed**: this app's "technical" category questions aren't always clean algorithm problems — a real Stripe-generated bank included "Describe a time you had to debug a production issue involving multiple microservices," tagged `technical`. Confirmed live the AI handles this gracefully instead of failing: asked to build a runnable exercise from the theme, it synthesized a genuinely testable, related function (`findFailedDependency(logs)`) rather than something nonsensical.
  - **Gating decision, researched via `agy` per direct user request** — should the practice-sandbox button be gated by the viewing user's own profile background (skills/title), not just the question's category? Real coding-practice products (LeetCode, HackerRank, Pramp, interviewing.io) gate by question category, never by the user's own profile — profile-inference gating is a documented anti-pattern (false negatives for career switchers, technical PMs/QA without an "engineer" title, incomplete profiles). Decision: **no profile gating added** — the existing category-based gating (technical questions only, and the AI only generates those for roles where it's warranted) already achieves the goal without that risk.
  - **Live-verified end to end, both execution paths independently confirmed via real code, not assumed**:
    - Full UI flow: opened a real Stripe/Senior-SWE bank, clicked "Practice this question," confirmed Monaco rendered with real syntax highlighting and 3 real AI-generated test cases, ran the unmodified starter code and confirmed the side-by-side comparison showed real, different values (`null` for the incomplete starter vs `"payment"` for the real reference solution) — read directly from the DOM (`.view-line` text content), not screenshot-guessed.
    - Python/Pyodide path independently verified via a direct Worker test isolating just the execution mechanism (bypassing AI generation): `add(2, 3)` correctly returned `"5"` via a real CDN load + WASM init + `runPythonAsync` round trip.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint .` (whole project) clean.

- **2026-08-18 (Phase 15, Tier 3, same day): Dashboard redesign visual-hierarchy pass (v2), triggered by direct user feedback ("the dashboard is not looking as I thought").** Researched via `agy`, describing the actual v1 layout in detail rather than asking generically — diagnosis: every card shared identical visual weight (same flat background/border/padding/type-scale), so the grid read as "8 gray boxes" instead of an intentional composition, regardless of the content inside them.
  - **Two of agy's literal prescriptions were deliberately NOT taken** — a static per-card ambient radial-gradient "hero wash," and a noise-texture overlay. Both cut against this exact project's own prior, real research: a page-level ambient gradient wash was tried and reverted 2026-07-27 after direct computed-style comparison against Raycast's real UI found zero ambient gradient anywhere on it — the established answer in this codebase is that hierarchy comes from background/surface/border token steps, not decorative gradients. Substituted agy's underlying goal (make the anchor card read as heavier) with a token-step treatment instead: new `.dashboard-hero-card` (accent-tinted border + inset hairline, larger padding) and `.dashboard-well` (darker inset container for the heatmap) in `app/globals.css`.
  - **Kept and applied agy's legitimate widget-craft techniques**: Pipeline Funnel bars thinned from `h-2.5` to 3px micro-bars with an accent glow on the fill; heatmap zero-count cells now recede to a faint ring instead of a flat visible fill, and the top intensity tier gets a lighter inner ring to read as "lit"; Action Center gets a large hero number establishing it as the grid's anchor.
  - **A real, separate correctness bug fixed in the same pass, not just a v2 polish item**: Rejection Radar's category badge displays genuinely AI-generated content (`rejection_diagnosis`), so per this app's strict Agent-content Invariant ("if it came from the agent, it gets this treatment; if it didn't, it never does") it belongs in `bg-agent-light`/`text-agent-dark` — v1 shipped it in warning-orange, an inconsistency, not a deliberate choice. Fixed, plus a `border-l-2 border-l-agent` accent on the card itself (only when real diagnoses exist), adapting agy's "AI tinting" suggestion into the app's own established Agent-Content Callout convention rather than a new gradient wash.
  - **A 4th confirmed hit of the documented Lightning CSS `color-mix()` gotcha** (`ui-tokens.md`'s "Gotcha" section, previously confirmed 3 times) — the first version of `.dashboard-hero-card`/`.dashboard-well` grouped `box-shadow: inset ...` in the same rule as `border`/`background`, and both `box-shadow`s silently vanished (confirmed via `getComputedStyle()` returning `"none"`, not assumed from source). Fixed the same documented way: every `color-mix()`-bearing property isolated into its own single-property rule. Re-verified live after the fix — both classes' box-shadows now compute correctly.
  - **Live-verified end to end**: screenshotted the new hierarchy (hero card border/number, funnel glow, heatmap well all visually distinct from the flat v1); confirmed via `getComputedStyle()` that the fixed CSS actually compiles correctly, not just visually plausible; temporarily flipped a disposable test job to `rejected` with a real-shaped diagnosis to confirm the Rejection Radar's new agent-teal badge and left-border render correctly, then reverted.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint .` (whole project) clean.

- **2026-08-18 (Phase 15, Tier 3): Dashboard redesign (§P) v1 shipped — the first Tier 3 item, per direct user pick between it and Practice Sandbox.** Replaces the old flat 4-stat-tile + 2x2-chart "rearview mirror" layout with a bento-grid "cockpit": what needs attention next, not just what already happened.
  - **New `AIActionCenter.tsx`** (hero, span-3) — deliberately deterministic, not a live AI call per dashboard load (`lib/dashboardInsights.ts`). Surfaces up to 3 real signals: applications stuck in "Applied" 14+ days, tracked jobs whose listing signal suggests they're gone (reuses `lib/jobStatus.ts`'s existing `getListingSignal`, no new staleness logic), and profile-completion nudges. A dashboard visited on every session is exactly the wrong place to add per-load LLM latency/cost — same zero-marginal-cost philosophy as `lib/outcomeInsights.ts`.
  - **New `PipelineFunnel.tsx`** (span-1) — real Draft→Applied→Interviewing→Offered counts from `jobs.application_status`, each segment linking to `/missions?stage=X`. `MissionsView.tsx` now reads that URL param on mount to pre-select the matching List filter (and switch out of Board view) — honors the redesign research's "no dead ends" rule with real navigation instead of building a second, parallel drill-down UI.
  - **New `ActivityHeatmap.tsx`** (span-2) — a hand-built 12-week × 7-day GitHub-contribution-style grid, no new dependency. Replaces both day-of-week bar/area charts (`CompanyResearchChart`/`JobsOverTimeChart`, both deleted from `AnalyticsCharts.tsx` after confirming zero other usages) — those only ever showed the last 7 days; this shows real trend shape over 3 months.
  - **New `UpcomingInterviews.tsx`** (span-1, conditional) and **`RejectionRadar.tsx`** (span-2) — both reuse-only, zero new AI calls. Upcoming Interviews only renders when a real interviewing-stage job exists (the row collapses from 4 to 3 columns when absent, rather than showing an empty card). Rejection Radar surfaces jobs that already have a real `rejection_diagnosis` from the job detail page's existing "Why the silence?" flow — it never diagnoses on its own, and shows an honest empty state when nothing's been diagnosed yet.
  - `StatsBar.tsx` deleted (fully replaced, confirmed unused elsewhere first).
  - **Live-verified, not just compiled**: clicked the Pipeline Funnel's "Interviewing" segment and confirmed it landed on `/missions` with the List view active and the Interviewing filter pill selected, showing exactly the one real interviewing job (Stripe). Hovered a heatmap cell and confirmed the header label updated to the real count+date ("77 on 2026-08-13"), matching known seeded data. Screenshotted the full new layout to confirm visual correctness.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint .` (whole project) clean.

- **2026-08-18 (Phase 15, cont'd): Command palette (Cmd+K) shipped — closes out the last of Tier 2, "Command palette" and "Keyboard shortcuts" shipped as one combined feature.** `components/ui/CommandPalette.tsx` + `CommandPaletteLoader.tsx` (mounted once in `app/layout.tsx` via the established `ssr:false` wrapper pattern, same reasoning as `SettingsModalLoader.tsx`). Global `Cmd/Ctrl+K` toggles it open from anywhere; a small "⌘K" pill button was added to `Navbar.tsx` too, for discoverability, dispatching a plain `sortie:open-command-palette` window event rather than lifting state through a new context provider for one boolean. Lists every top-level route ("Navigate" group) plus Settings and a theme toggle ("Actions" group); typeahead filters by label + a keywords string; full keyboard nav (↑↓/Enter/Esc), mouse-hover stays in sync with keyboard selection.
  - **A real bug was caught and fixed during live verification, not left in**: the first version's Cmd+K-to-close and Escape handlers set `open` to `false` directly, bypassing the `close()` helper's query/activeIndex reset — a query typed before closing silently persisted, so reopening the palette and typing again produced a concatenated, nonsensical filter string (confirmed live: typed "res" → Enter → navigated to Resume correctly; reopened later, typed "light" → input showed "reslight", zero matches). Fixed by making both close paths always reset all three fields; re-verified live by reopening and confirming the input was genuinely empty.
  - **A real eslint-caught bug avoided the same session**: an initial draft reset `activeIndex` inside a `useEffect` keyed on `query` — this project's eslint config treats `setState`-in-effect as a hard error (cascading-render risk), caught immediately on lint. Fixed using the same "adjust state during render" idiom `Navbar.tsx` already established for its own `pathname`-change reset, not a new pattern.
  - **Live-verified end to end**: opened via the navbar button, typed to filter, pressed real Enter to navigate (confirmed landed on `/resume`); opened via a programmatically-dispatched `Ctrl+K` keydown event and confirmed the dialog actually rendered (a real DOM check, not assumed); confirmed Escape closes it; confirmed the theme-toggle action really flips the app's theme (screenshotted before/after); confirmed the query-reset fix via a second reopen check.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint .` (whole project) clean.

- **2026-08-18 (Phase 15, cont'd): "Should I apply?" quick verdict shipped — the last of Tier 2's originally-unbuilt items.** `lib/applyVerdict.ts`'s `computeApplyVerdict()` (deterministic, zero AI cost) synthesizes signals already computed elsewhere — `overall_grade`, the Legitimacy evaluation dimension, `title_scope_mismatch`, and `lib/jobStatus.ts`'s listing-staleness signal — into one decisive `apply`/`consider`/`long-shot`/`skip`/`unscored` verdict with a one-line reason, rendered via `components/job-details/ApplyVerdict.tsx` at the very top of the job detail page (above `JobActionBar`, before the fold). Reuses the app's already-established Match Score Colors tiering (`ui-tokens.md`) rather than a new palette — agent-teal for "apply", neutral for "consider", warning/error for the weaker tiers.
  - **Priority order, tested against real data**: a confirmed-unavailable listing always shows "Skip" regardless of grade; a poor Legitimacy grade (D/F) escalates straight to "Skip" even on an otherwise-mediocre overall grade; a title/scope mismatch flags "Long shot"; a "likely" listing-staleness signal (dropped from a repeat search) downgrades the grade-derived tier by one notch rather than overriding it entirely.
  - **Live-verified across 3 real grade tiers, not just compiled**: a real grade-A job showed "Apply" with the correct fit-description reason; a real grade-C job showed "Consider"; a real grade-D job correctly showed "Skip", not "Long shot" — because that specific job's Legitimacy dimension was itself graded poorly, confirming the priority-ordering logic actually works on real data (the legitimacy check runs before the plain grade-tier fallback).
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean.

- **2026-08-18 (Phase 15, cont'd): 8 more job platforms shipped for the extension — SimplyHired, Dice, CareerBuilder, RemoteOK, Monster, We Work Remotely, Built In, ZipRecruiter — per direct user request following the research pass in the entry below.**
  - **Discovery that changed the build approach**: live-checking each candidate site (not trusting the research alone) found that most publish real schema.org `JobPosting` JSON-LD — Google Jobs' own SEO rich-snippet markup — which is far more stable than any CSS selector since it doesn't depend on the visual DOM. New shared `extractFromJsonLd()` helper (`extension/content.js`) powers SimplyHired/Dice/CareerBuilder/RemoteOK/Monster; a new `stripHtml()` helper handles several of those sites shipping the JSON-LD `description` as raw HTML, not plain text.
  - **3 sites needed bespoke DOM selectors** (no JobPosting JSON-LD, confirmed live): We Work Remotely (`document.title` "X at Company" parsing, same trick as LinkedIn, plus `.lis-container__job__content__description`), Built In (real `<h1>`, `a[href*="/company/"]` for company, `.html-parsed-content` for description), ZipRecruiter (no stable page-level `<h1>` even with a job selected, confirmed live — but real `data-testid="job-details-scroll-container"` scopes reliable selectors to the open job specifically).
  - **Monster's selectors were NOT independently verified** — live verification hit Monster's own bot-detection challenge mid-research ("Verification Required... Use of developer or inspection tools"), a real, honestly-reported blocker, not a guess dressed up as confirmed. Built on CareerBuilder's confirmed JSON-LD pattern instead, since the two sites share the same underlying job listings (confirmed live: an identical job UUID appeared in both sites' search results for the same real posting).
  - **Backend**: `jobs.source`'s type extended (no new migration — `source` is already a plain `text` column) across `types/index.ts`, `lib/externalJob.ts`, and the capture route's Zod schema. `lib/jobSource.ts` gives all 8 a neutral "via {Platform}" badge (`bg-surface-secondary text-text-secondary`) — a deliberate scope decision, not an oversight: real per-platform brand colors + fetched logos (what LinkedIn/Indeed got) is a meaningfully bigger task than capture support itself and wasn't asked for this round.
  - `manifest.json` bumped to 1.5.0, `host_permissions`/`content_scripts.matches` extended to all 8 new domains.
  - **Live-verified end to end**: 8 real `curl` calls against the actual `/api/extension/capture-job` endpoint (real generated key), one per platform, confirmed all 8 stored the correct `source` value via direct `db query`, and confirmed all 8 badges render correctly on `/missions` (Board view + the "Source" filter dropdown, which correctly picked up all 8 new options). Test jobs and the test key deleted after.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint`/`node --check` (extension files)/manifest JSON validity all clean.
  - Full write-up: `extension/README.md`'s "v1.5" section.

- **2026-08-18 (Phase 15, cont'd): source badges upgraded to real brand colors + real logos, a Missions "Source" filter added, and a job-board expansion research pass — all direct follow-ups to the user's questions on the previous entry.**
  - **Missions "Source" filter** — new popover in `MissionsFilterBar.tsx` (same `FilterPopover`/`RadioOption` pattern as Location), wired into `MissionsView.tsx`'s existing client-side filter `useMemo` and the `KanbanBoardLoader` remount key. Options are computed from `SOURCE_FILTER_OPTIONS` (`lib/jobSource.ts`) filtered down to whatever sources actually exist in the loaded jobs — an account with no extension-captured jobs yet sees no Source filter at all, not a filter offering options that would return zero results.
  - **Real brand colors, not plain gray.** Turned out `ui-tokens.md` already speced this — a "Source Badges" table (`bg-linkedin-light`/`text-linkedin`) and an Invariant ("LinkedIn badge always uses `--linkedin` — never generic blue") already existed, just never wired up when the badge first shipped a few hours earlier. Added a matching `--color-indeed`/`-light`/`-foreground` token set to `app/globals.css` (light + dark-mode override, same shape as the existing LinkedIn tokens) — the hex (`#003A9B`) came from real research (`agy`), not a guess.
  - **Real logos, not hand-drawn approximations — per direct user request ("can't we use the real logos?").** LinkedIn keeps the existing hand-authored `LinkedInGlyph.tsx` (already proven, already in production use on `ConnectedAccounts`/`CompanyResearch`/`InsiderConnections`). New `components/shared/PlatformLogo.tsx` fetches Indeed's real current logo — initially attempted via the same `unavatar.io` proxy `CompanyLogo.tsx` already uses, but that returned a **real, reproducible `429` for both `indeed.com` and `linkedin.com` specifically** (confirmed live via direct `curl`, retried after a delay — still 429; unrelated domains like `stripe.com` resolved fine at 200). Switched to fetching `https://www.indeed.com/favicon.ico` directly instead (confirmed live: real `200`, real 32×32 image) — `www.indeed.com` added to `/api/logo`'s host allowlist for that one fixed URL, not opened to arbitrary paths.
  - **Live-verified end to end**, not just compiled: created 2 real test jobs via the extension capture API (one `linkedin`, one `indeed`), confirmed via `getComputedStyle()` that the badge colors exactly matched the new tokens (`rgb(10, 102, 194)` / `rgb(0, 58, 155)`), confirmed the Indeed `<img>` actually loaded (`complete: true`, `naturalWidth: 32`) after the unavatar→favicon.ico fix, and confirmed the new Source filter genuinely narrows the whole board (not just one column) when set to "LinkedIn". Test jobs and the test API key both deleted after.
  - **Job-board expansion research (agy), per direct user request — research only, nothing built.** Real per-platform feasibility breakdown for 10 more job boards (Glassdoor, ZipRecruiter, Monster, Dice, Wellfound, SimplyHired, CareerBuilder, Built In, We Work Remotely, RemoteOK): login-wall status, DOM stability, and what's behind the Apply button, plus official brand colors for Glassdoor/ZipRecruiter for if any get built later. Top 3 ranked candidates: We Work Remotely, Built In, SimplyHired — all zero/low login-wall risk and either excellent DOM stability or infrastructure this extension already has selector logic for. Glassdoor and Wellfound both flagged as hard-gated behind a login wall, a real buildability concern. Full table in `extension/README.md`'s new "Future platform expansion" section.
