- **2026-08-18 (Phase 15, cont'd): Deadline tracker shipped, then a real gap fixed after direct user questions — extension-captured jobs (LinkedIn/Indeed) were completely indistinguishable from each other and from a manual paste, everywhere in the app.**
  - **Deadline tracker / application calendar** (build-plan.md §D) — new `jobs.next_deadline_at`/`next_deadline_label` (migration `20260818020000`), a real user-entered future timestamp, deliberately kept separate from `interview_events` (a log of what already happened, not a schedule of what's coming — see the Phase 15 write-side entry above for why that distinction matters in this codebase). New `setJobDeadline` action (set-or-clear, mirrors `updateJobNotes`'s shape), `components/job-details/JobDeadline.tsx` (label + datetime-local input + Save, Clear appears once a deadline exists) on the job detail page, `components/missions/UpcomingDeadlines.tsx` (sorted strip on `/missions`, reuses the page's already-fetched `jobs` array — no extra query — overdue items stay visible in warning color instead of disappearing). New `formatTimeUntil` in `lib/utils.ts`, mirroring the existing `formatTimeAgo`.
  - **Two Tier-2 backlog rows turned out to already be shipped** — corrected stale `build-plan.md` markers rather than re-building: "Job requirements split into Required vs Preferred" (`Qualification.tsx`'s `BulletColumn` already does this) and "Match sub-scores" (`EvaluationBreakdown.tsx`'s 10-dimension grid already does this, in a richer form than the 3-sub-score competitor source). Same class of staleness as the `correctSkillTag` marker corrected 2026-08-17.
  - **User asked directly, mid-session**: how are extension-captured (LinkedIn/Indeed) jobs visually separated from other jobs in Missions, how completely are their details captured, and is the same extension available for other platforms like Glassdoor? Investigating surfaced a real, previously-unnoticed gap: `lib/externalJob.ts`'s `createExternalJob` hardcoded `source: "url"` for **every** call — the manual "paste a job from anywhere" flow AND every extension capture regardless of site — even though `content.js` already knows which site it's on internally (`extractLinkedIn`/`extractIndeed`, keyed off `window.location.hostname`) and was just never propagating that fact anywhere. `job.source` was also never rendered in any UI, confirmed by grep — genuinely dead data before this fix, not something the user was missing in the UI.
  - **Fixed**: `extractLinkedIn()`/`extractIndeed()` (`extension/content.js`) now include `source: "linkedin"`/`"indeed"` in the extracted job object, flows unchanged through `background.js`'s already-generic message-forwarding to `/api/extension/capture-job`'s Zod schema (new optional `source` enum field) to `createExternalJob` (`input.source ?? "url"` — the manual paste flow, which sends no `source`, is unaffected). New `lib/jobSource.ts`'s `getSourcePlatformLabel()` renders "via LinkedIn"/"via Indeed"/"Pasted" — `SerpApi` (the default/majority case) shows no badge. Wired into both `JobResultCard.tsx` (List view) and `KanbanCard.tsx` (Board view) — the latter needed `"source"` added to its `KanbanJob` `Pick<Job, ...>` type. Also corrected `types/index.ts`'s `Job.source` type, which was already stale (`"search" | "url"`, never matching the real runtime `"SerpApi"` value — confirmed via grep, nothing was relying on the wrong value).
  - **Live-verified end to end via a real HTTP call**, not just compiled: generated a real API key through the Settings → Browser extension UI, `curl`'d `/api/extension/capture-job` directly against the local dev server with `source: "linkedin"` (simulating exactly what `content.js` now sends), confirmed the row landed with `source = 'linkedin'` via direct `db query`, then confirmed "via LinkedIn" rendered correctly on both `/missions`' Board view and List view. Test job and test API key both deleted after.
  - **Answered directly, not just fixed**: (1) other details — the extension only ever extracts title/company/location/description/url directly; the rest (salary, requirements, benefits, seniority, hiring process) comes from the same AI evaluation pipeline every job already runs through for scoring, which extracts those from the raw description text — so extension-captured jobs end up with the same full detail set as scraped ones, as long as the captured description is complete. One real gap: `job_type` is only ever evaluator *input*, never *output* — stays `null` forever on extension-captured jobs, not yet fixed. (2) Glassdoor/other platforms — not supported, not yet investigated; adding a site is a small, well-understood addition (`extract<Site>()` + a manifest host-permission entry) but each one needs the same real-live-page verification pass LinkedIn/Indeed got before being trusted, not a copy-paste guess — Glassdoor specifically is known for aggressive anti-bot/login walls that need checking first. Full write-up in `extension/README.md`'s new "What gets captured" and updated "Scope" sections.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`node --check` (extension files) clean throughout.

- **2026-08-18 (Phase 15): Recently Viewed jobs finished — the one item Phase 14 left half-built.** `jobs.last_viewed_at` (migration `20260818010000`) was already live on the backend with no app code using it; both halves built this session.
  - **Write side**: `app/find-jobs/[id]/page.tsx` now updates `last_viewed_at` on every job-detail page load, via Next's `after()` (`next/server`) so the write is genuinely fire-and-forget and adds zero latency to the page response — confirmed this Next.js build (16.2.7) supports it and that Vercel's `waitUntil` backing makes it safe in serverless before using it. Reuses the already-constructed `insforge` server client from earlier in the render rather than calling `createInsforgeServer()` again inside the callback — Server Component `after()` callbacks can't call request-time APIs like `cookies()` themselves (confirmed against Next's own docs), and `createInsforgeServer()` calls `cookies()` internally.
  - **Read side**: new `components/find-jobs/RecentlyViewed.tsx` — a plain server component (no "use client", so no hydration-mismatch risk the way `FindJobsForm`'s `lastRunLabel` has), a horizontal-scroll strip of up to 6 most-recently-viewed jobs (title/company/logo/match score/`formatTimeAgo`), rendered above the search form on `/find-jobs`. `app/find-jobs/page.tsx` fetches it with `.not("last_viewed_at", "is", null).order("last_viewed_at", { ascending: false }).limit(6)` — an established query pattern in this codebase (`app/resume/page.tsx`, `app/dashboard/page.tsx` use the same `.not(col, "is", null)` shape).
  - **Live-verified end to end, not just compiled**: opened a real job (Aviso Wealth, Senior Software Engineer Java) on the existing dev server, navigated back to `/find-jobs`, confirmed the widget rendered it correctly — title, company, match score (60), and "just now" — via a real DB round trip, no seeded/synthetic data involved.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean on all three touched files.
  - This closes out Tier 1 of the backlog re-prioritization from Phase 14 — nothing strictly Tier-1 remains unstarted.

- **2026-08-18 (Phase 14, cont'd): extension v1.2→v1.4 (agy-researched improvements + 2 real live-debugged bugs), then a full re-prioritization of the whole backlog and 5 Tier-1 items built. Session paused at ~80% context per direct user instruction — see `RESUME.md` for the exact resume point.**
  - **Extension v1.2, researched via `agy` against real competitor extensions (Simplify Copilot, Teal, Careerflow) per direct user request**: replaced the floating bottom-right button with an inline Shadow-DOM widget that tries to anchor next to the site's own Apply button (falls back to floating if the anchor selector doesn't match); added a live match-score badge (`/api/extension/score-preview`, cache-first — reuses an already-tracked job's real `match_score` for free, only a genuine cache miss spends a new usage-gated AI call, `lib/extensionScorePreview.ts`); made the popup context-aware (shows the detected job + score + a Draft/Applied picker before saving, the "Applied" choice logs a real `application_events` row); built an autofill v2 adapter pattern (`background.js`'s `sortieAutofillPage` detects Greenhouse/Lever by hostname and fills their known field names directly before the generic keyword-matcher runs on what's left, plus fuzzy `<select>` matching for location dropdowns and a "Get résumé from Sortie" link next to file-upload inputs instead of fighting the browser's file-input security sandbox).
  - **Real bug #1, found live by the user immediately after v1.2 shipped**: `manifest.json` never listed the Sortie API's own domain in `host_permissions` — a MV3 service worker's cross-origin `fetch()` is subject to normal CORS unless the target origin is declared there, so every `background.js` call to `/api/extension/*` had been silently failing since the extension was first built, not just after this session's changes. Fixed by adding `jobpilot-experiment.vercel.app`/`localhost:3001` to `host_permissions`.
  - **Real bug #2, debugged live together with the user against their actual logged-in LinkedIn account** (something v1's own README had explicitly flagged as never independently confirmed): LinkedIn's current build renders the job title/company as CSS-module-hashed classes (`_44c32d2f _4208c38f`, confirmed via `document.querySelector` run directly in the user's own DevTools console) — there's no `<h1>` on the page at all, and none of the old BEM-style classes exist anymore. Rewrote `content.js`'s LinkedIn extraction (v1.3) to use `document.title` (reliably `"Job Title | Company | LinkedIn"`, confirmed live) for title/company, and a new `findLargestTextBlock()` runtime heuristic (most-specific large text block, not a fixed selector) for the description — a deliberately more durable approach than any CSS selector, since hashed classes can change on any LinkedIn deploy.
  - **Real bug #3**: after fixing #2, the user's own live retest on a Dayforce signup form still failed autofill with a bare "Couldn't fill this page." Root cause found by making the error message actually surface (`background.js`'s `SORTIE_FILL_PAGE` handler now reads `chrome.scripting.executeScript`'s per-injection `.error` field, which resolves rather than rejects on an injected-function throw — the original code never checked it) — the real error was HTTP 404 on `/api/extension/profile`, because the extension's `config.js` points at **production** by default and none of this session's backend work (`/api/extension/*`, the whole apiKeys system) had actually been deployed there yet, only tested against localhost. Fixed by deploying; confirmed live against production afterward with a real generated key.
  - Added a manual refresh button (↻, spins while loading) next to the match-score badge (v1.4), per direct user request — re-fetches via the same cache-first backend, doesn't bypass it.
  - **User directive, standing**: stop proposing/researching new feature ideas, work only through `build-plan.md`'s existing Master Feature Inventory. Given a full prioritized re-read of that inventory as a 3-tier ranked list; told to work through it autonomously. Saved to memory (`feedback_no_new_features.md`).
  - **5 Tier-1 backlog items then built in one pass, all live-verified except where noted**:
    1. **Per-job Application History panel** — `application_events`/`interview_events`/`compensation_events` (§Q1) were only ever written from the job detail page, never read back on it. New `listJobEventHistory` action, `ApplicationHistory.tsx`. Live-verified with seeded data.
    2. **Tags & personal notes on jobs** — new `jobs.tags`/`jobs.personal_notes` columns, `JobTagsAndNotes.tsx` on the job detail page, tag chips on `JobResultCard` too. The popup's own live click hit browser-automation ref-staleness (a known recurring class of friction in this project) — verified the actual write path with a direct SQL round-trip matching the action's exact query shape instead, then confirmed correct rendering.
    3. **Interview debrief capture** — `logInterviewEvent` existed since §Q1 with **zero UI ever calling it**. New `InterviewDebrief.tsx` in the job detail page's Interview Prep Room tab. Live-verified by flipping a test job to `interviewing`.
    4. **"Generate Résumé" job-card quick-action** — added to `JobResultCard`'s menu, reusing the already-existing `?generate=resume` deep-link. Type-checked/rendered correctly per the accessibility tree; the actual click-through hit the same ref-staleness issue (clicked a different button by accident, cleaned up) — real confidence tier here is "pattern-matched to already-proven code," not click-verified.
    5. **Recently viewed jobs — started, not finished.** `jobs.last_viewed_at` column added and live; no app code uses it yet (no on-view update, no widget). Explicitly left as the next pickup point.
  - Two checkpoint commits this session (`91a656c`, `9ebf143`), both pushed and deployed to production; Inngest re-synced after each deploy.
  - A background sleep-prevention process was started per direct user request (`SetThreadExecutionState` via a looping PowerShell script) — non-persistent, doesn't touch Windows power-plan settings, reverts on reboot or if killed.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean project-wide throughout.

- **2026-08-17/18 (Phase 14, cont'd): §Q5's Manifest V3 extension itself shipped — closing out the "backend only" entry directly below, which is now stale where it says the extension wasn't built.** New top-level `extension/` directory, not part of the Next.js app (`eslint.config.mjs` now globally ignores it — separate runtime, `chrome.*` globals, cross-file globals via `importScripts` rather than ES modules).

Indeed's `data-testid` attributes were captured directly and are believed stable; LinkedIn could only be checked logged-out (no real LinkedIn session available here) — the logged-in-variant class names in the code are a documented best-effort fallback, unverified.

Treated the same way here: not worked around.

The backend half it depends on (`/api/extension/capture-job`) WAS separately curl-verified with a real generated key (see the entry below) — the still-unverified part is purely the client (popup/content-script/button UI), not the API contract it calls.

Applied via the documented migrations-tracking workaround (references `auth.users`, so `db query` was rejected — stashed the other 53 migration files aside via PowerShell, ran `db migrations up --all`, restored them).

Self-fetches via the server action directly (no new GET route needed) rather than the modal's existing `/api/settings/me` prefetch pattern, since this data wasn't part of that endpoint's original shape.

Zod-validates the request body rather than trusting it blindly.

Fixed with a try/catch around the send — a failed evaluation-trigger now means the job just stays unscored, not that the whole capture reads as failed, matching the same fire-and-forget pattern already used for `addAccomplishment`'s Inngest trigger and this route's own `last_used_at` update.

Confirmed all 3 auth/validation failure paths return the right status (`401` missing token, `401` invalid token, `400` malformed body). Confirmed `last_used_at` updates on a real successful call. Confirmed "Revoke key" in the UI performs a real DB delete, not just an optimistic client-side removal. **Regression-tested the pre-existing `/jobs/external` "paste a job" flow after the `addExternalJob` refactor** — submitted a real job through the actual page UI, confirmed it still saves correctly, not just assumed from the shared code being "the same logic." Phone now links `tel:+<digits>`; Location now links to a Google Maps search for the exact stored string. **Live-verified via `read_page`'s accessibility tree** — confirmed real resolved `href`s on all 4 chips (`tel:+14165550123`, a Maps search URL, and both profile URLs correctly prefixed with `https://`).

- **2026-08-17 (Phase 14, cont'd): a batch of 7 direct user-requested fixes/features — Missions location filter, Navigator made floating-only, "AI Navigator reads" rename, Navigator animation + a real bug fix, a full NavigatorChat UI overhaul, a Find & Evaluate alignment fix, and profile-driven search defaults.**
  - **Missions got a location/search/remote filter bar** (`components/missions/MissionsFilterBar.tsx`, new) — mirrors `FilterBar.tsx`'s portal/`FilterPopover`/`RadioOption` pattern exactly but as its own small copy (local client-side array filtering over already-loaded jobs, not FilterBar's server-driven `SearchFilters` query params — different enough data model that sharing would need its own generic layer for no real gain yet). Search matches title/company substring; Location is a single-select radio list built from the unique `job.location` values actually present in the user's own tracked jobs; Remote reuses the same `/\bremote\b/i` title+location regex idiom already established elsewhere. Applies to BOTH Board and List views (the pre-existing stage-filter pills stay List-only, since Kanban already groups by stage as its own columns). **Real gotcha fixed in the same pass**: `KanbanBoard.tsx` seeds its drag-state from a `useState(() => groupByStatus(jobs))` lazy initializer that only ever runs once (deliberate — a completed drag's optimistic move shouldn't get clobbered by the next server-revalidated `jobs` prop) — that also means it never re-derives columns when `jobs` changes shape after mount, so a filter change alone wouldn't have done anything visible. Fixed by keying `KanbanBoardLoader` on the active filter combo (`key={search|location|remoteOnly}`), forcing a fresh mount on any filter change. `KanbanCard.tsx` also gained a `location` line (new `MapPin` icon) so the board itself shows what's now filterable, not just the filter bar.
  - **Dedicated `/agent` page removed, per direct user decision — Navigator is floating-only now.** `app/agent/page.tsx` deleted, its Navbar link removed, `NavigatorLauncher.tsx`'s now-dead `pathname === "/agent"` exclusion and its "both a dedicated page AND a floating launcher" comment removed, `actions/agent.ts`'s dead `revalidatePath("/agent")` call removed. The floating launcher (mounted globally in `app/layout.tsx`) is untouched otherwise — it never depended on the page existing.
  - **"Agent read" renamed to "AI Navigator reads" everywhere**, per direct user request — this is a documented design-system label (`ui-rules.md`'s "Agent Content" section, `ui-tokens.md`'s reference snippet), not incidental copy, so both docs were updated alongside every literal occurrence: `KanbanCard.tsx`, `JobResultCard.tsx`, `MatchScore.tsx`, `InterviewPanel.tsx`, `LeverageSynthesizer.tsx`, plus comment references in `ATSAuditCard.tsx`/`EquityDecoder.tsx`/`OfferWorkspace.tsx`. Other, deliberately distinct AI-content labels (`QuestionBankPanel.tsx`'s "Insider tips", the Study View's "AI reference implementation"/"AI strategy guide") were left alone — not in scope, and conflating them would blur an intentional honesty-labeling distinction.
  - **Navigator floater animated open/close** (`NavigatorLauncher.tsx`) — researched via the `ui-ux-pro-max` skill (`--domain ux` query) for the underlying motion principles: exit animation deliberately shorter than enter (150ms vs 220ms, ~68%, Material Design's "exit faster than enter" guidance) and spatial continuity (`slide-in-from-bottom-4`, matching the panel's real screen position relative to the FAB it opens from). Implemented via a `mounted`/`open` state split (`mounted` stays true through the exit animation, unmounting only after `EXIT_DURATION_MS` via a cancellable `setTimeout`) rather than a hard conditional-render unmount, using this codebase's existing `tw-animate-css` `animate-in`/`animate-out` utility classes — no new animation library. The FAB icon itself now crossfades/rotates between Sparkles and X instead of an instant swap (`motion-reduce:transition-none` fallback). Added outside-click-to-close, Escape-to-close, and an explicit header close (X) button (previously the only way to close was re-clicking the FAB).
  - **A real bug found and fixed in the same pass, caught live by the user's own manual testing, not by review**: clicking the FAB to close the panel appeared to do nothing. Root cause — the new outside-click `mousedown` listener didn't exclude the FAB itself (only the panel), so clicking the FAB while open fired `mousedown` → read as an "outside click" → closed the panel — *before* the FAB's own `click` handler ran against the now-stale `open` value and reopened it. Two handlers fighting each other, net effect: the close button visually did nothing. Fixed by adding a second ref (`fabRef`) to the outside-click exclusion check, same as the panel's own ref. **Live-verified with a real `mousedown`→`mouseup`→`click` event sequence dispatched via `javascript_tool`** (not the synthetic `.click()` used in the first, insufficient verification pass, which skips `mousedown` entirely and would have missed this exact bug) — confirmed the FAB now correctly closes and stays closed.
  - **`NavigatorChat.tsx` got a full UI pass, per direct user request ("make whole chatting ai tool much better")**: auto-scroll to the newest message/typing-indicator on every update (a real prior gap — a reply arriving while scrolled up stayed invisible); autofocus on the input when the floating popover opens; a real animated typing indicator (3 staggered `animate-bounce` dots, `motion-reduce:animate-none` fallback) replacing the plain "Navigator is thinking..." text, styled as a loading indicator per `ui-ux-pro-max`'s guidance that continuous/bounce animation is appropriate there specifically (not for decorative elements); small avatar circles on both sides (Sparkles for Navigator, a plain `User` icon for the human side — no profile-photo data wired into this component, so a generic icon rather than fabricating a photo); each message now carries its own `fade-in-up` entrance (plays once per newly-mounted bubble, not on every re-render, since React keeps existing keyed nodes stable). **WhatsApp-style day separators and per-message timestamps added, per explicit user request** — `formatDayLabel()` ("Today"/"Yesterday"/weekday name within a week/full date beyond that) and `formatMessageTime()`, both local to this component (chat-specific grouping logic, not folded into the shared `lib/utils.ts` date helpers).
  - **Find & Evaluate's page heading was misaligned from its own search box**, caught live by direct user report. Root cause: the heading lived in `<main>`'s `max-w-7xl` wrapper, but `FindJobsForm`'s own "Run a sortie" console re-centers itself independently at a narrower `max-w-6xl` — two independently-centered boxes at different max-widths don't share a left edge. Fixed by matching the heading's wrapper to the exact same `mx-auto w-full max-w-6xl` constraint. **Live-verified via direct `getBoundingClientRect()` comparison** (not a visual screenshot judgment call) — both wrappers now report the identical left offset (56.4px).
  - **Find & Evaluate's search now pre-fills Target role/Target location from the user's profile, per direct user request** ("same as JobRight is doing"). `app/find-jobs/page.tsx` already fell back to the user's *last search* (`agent_runs.job_title_searched`/`location_searched`) when returning to the page — that stays the top priority. New: when no search has ever run yet (a brand-new user), it now falls back to `profiles.job_titles_seeking[0]` for title and `profiles.preferred_locations[0] ?? profiles.location` for location, instead of a blank form. This app has no separate signup questionnaire distinct from the always-editable Profile page's own "Job Preferences" section — that section IS the source, not a new flow. **Live-verified**: confirmed `lastRun` correctly still wins when present (test account showed its real last-searched "Software Engineer"/"Vancouver", not the profile's "Toronto, ON"), and confirmed via direct DB query that the profile fallback value is real and ready ("Toronto, ON") for whenever a fresh account without search history hits this path.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean across every file touched in this batch.
  - **Two more real bugs found and fixed immediately after, both caught live by the user actually using the Missions filter bar, not by review:**
    - **Scrolling the Location filter's own dropdown list didn't work — it just closed instead.** Root cause: `MissionsFilterBar.tsx`'s `FilterPanel` copied `FilterBar.tsx`'s "close when the page scrolls out from under this fixed popover" listener verbatim (`window.addEventListener("scroll", onClose, true)`), but this is the first `FilterPanel`-shaped popover in the codebase that actually needs internal scrolling (`max-h-72 overflow-y-auto`, for an arbitrarily-long location list) — `FilterBar.tsx`'s own copies never overflow, so the bug never manifested there. A capture-phase (`true`) listener on `window` receives scroll events from ANY descendant scrollable element, even though `scroll` doesn't bubble in the normal phase — so scrolling the internal list read as "the page scrolled" and closed the panel on the first scroll tick. Fixed by checking `event.target` against `panelRef.current.contains()` and ignoring internal scrolls. **A second, real edge-case bug surfaced while verifying the fix**: `event.target` isn't always a proper `Node` (a synthetic `window`-targeted scroll event has `target === window`), and `Node.contains()` throws a `TypeError` on a non-Node argument rather than returning `false` — guarded with an `event.target instanceof Node` check first. **Live-verified with real dispatched DOM events** (setting `scrollTop` directly on the panel to fire a genuine non-bubbling `scroll` event) — confirmed internal scroll no longer closes the panel.
    - **Location filter showed near-duplicate entries for the same city.** Google Jobs' own scraped location text sometimes tags a multi-location posting with a `"(+N other/others)"` suffix (e.g. `"Toronto, ON (+1 other)"`) — a different literal string than a plain `"Toronto, ON"` posting for the same city, so the two showed as separate filter options. Per direct user request, grouped by stripping the suffix (`normalizeLocationForFilter()`, `MissionsView.tsx`) before both building the dropdown's unique list and matching the actual filter — a real regex strip, not a hardcoded list of known suffixes. **Live-verified**: dropdown collapsed from `["Toronto, ON", "Toronto, ON (+1 other)", "Vancouver, BC", "Vancouver, BC (+5 others)"]` down to `["Toronto, ON", "Vancouver, BC"]`, and selecting "Vancouver, BC" correctly returned jobs from both underlying raw variants (28 cards, not just the bare-string subset).

- **2026-08-17 (Phase 14, cont'd): §Q3 Application → Outcome Loop shipped — the documented next step in the §Q sequence now that §Q1's `application_events` exists.**
  - **`lib/outcomeInsights.ts`** (new, pure functions, zero AI) — `computeInterviewRateByMatchBand`/`computeInterviewRateByGrade` bucket the user's own non-draft jobs and cross-reference `application_events` for whether each job EVER reached `interview_scheduled`/`interview_completed` (not just its current `application_status`, which is lossy — a job now "rejected" could have genuinely interviewed first; only the real event log knows that). `computeRejectionReasonDistribution` tallies `jobs.rejection_diagnosis.possibleReasons[].category` across all rejected jobs, directly reusing `lib/rejectionIntelligence.ts`'s existing closed `RejectionReasonCategory` enum — no new taxonomy. **`MIN_SAMPLE_SIZE = 3` gates every individual stat**, not just the section as a whole — a rate computed from 1-2 data points reads as a confident percentage but is really noise, so a thin band/grade is silently omitted rather than shown misleadingly.
  - **`lib/outcomeNarrative.ts`** (new) — the spec's optional AI synthesis step, same honesty-scoped shape as `rejectionIntelligence.ts`/`leverageSynthesizer.ts`: the model only ever sees the user's OWN already-computed aggregate numbers (never raw job data, never an outside benchmark), returns 1-2 observations that must cite a real number from the input. New `outcome_narrative` usage key (5/day) — opt-in button click only, never eagerly generated on page load, so the cap mainly guards repeated re-generation for unchanged data.
  - **`actions/outcomeInsights.ts`** — `getOutcomeStats()` (deterministic, no usage cap, always computed on `/career` load: two DB reads + pure math) and `generateOutcomeNarrativeAction()` (usage-gated, recomputes stats server-side rather than trusting client-supplied numbers, so the AI call is always grounded in a fresh read).
  - **`components/career/OutcomeInsights.tsx`** — new section on `/career`, above the existing timeline. Two rate-bar groups (match-score band, evaluation grade) plus a rejection-reason list, all always visible when enough data exists; an honest empty state ("track a few more applications...") when it doesn't. The AI narrative renders in the exact `border-agent bg-agent-light` "AI Navigator reads" treatment (this session's earlier rename) once generated.
  - **Deliberately deferred from the full §Q3 spec**: the `job_decisions` table (capturing an explicit "applied vs. skipped, why" decision for jobs looked at but never applied to). This needs a genuinely new user-facing capture flow (a skip-reason picker) that risks overlapping/confusing with the already-existing `is_hidden` "not interested" toggle — flagged as a real fast-follow, not built this pass, to keep this slice's UI surface-area addition small.
  - **Live-verified with real seeded data, by hand-checked math, not assumed**: seeded 9 real jobs across all 3 match-score bands and grades A/C (individual `db query` calls — a multi-statement batch silently no-op'd first, see the new InsForge memory gotcha below) plus `application_events` giving a known interview pattern and 2 rejected jobs with distinct `rejection_diagnosis` categories. Confirmed exact rendered output matched hand-calculated expected values: 80-100% band 2/3=67%, 60-79% band 1/3=33%, below-60% band 0/3=0%, Grade A 2/3=67%, Grade C 1/5=20% (correctly aggregating grade-C jobs across two different match-score bands), rejection reasons "Skills gap: 2, Seniority mismatch: 1". Grade D correctly did NOT render (only 1 D-graded job, below `MIN_SAMPLE_SIZE`) — honest gating confirmed working, not just coded. AI narrative button confirmed via real server log (`generateOutcomeNarrativeAction() in 3299ms`, a real Gemini call) and rendered output correctly cited the exact real percentages ("increasing from 0% ... to 67%", "67% ... compared to a 20%") with zero fabrication. All seeded test data fully cleaned up and confirmed reverted to the pre-test state afterward.
  - **A second real `db query` gotcha found and recorded in InsForge project memory this pass** (`db-query-silently-noops-on-multi-statement-sql`): multi-statement SQL passed as one string to `db query` silently does nothing (reports success, changes 0 rows) even for plain row-level DML (UPDATE/INSERT), not just the previously-known DDL/auth-schema-FK case. Always issue one statement per `db query` call going forward.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean throughout.

- **2026-08-17 (Phase 14, cont'd): Kanban filter bar extended (match score/sort/needs-attention) + real Kanban card-detail research (agy) implemented, per direct user follow-up request.**
  - **Research pass, via `agy`**: asked what real job-tracker products (Teal/Huntr/Simplify-shaped) put on a Kanban card for an application already being tracked, distinct from a discovery/search card. Came back tiered: Tier 1 (next-action reminder, interview date/time, days-since-applied), Tier 2 (priority/star flag, salary, remote/onsite icon, quick-link icons), Tier 3 (tags, contact person, application source — recommended for a details pane, not the card face).
  - **What actually got built, and why each item was included or explicitly skipped**:
    - **Days since applied** — built. Sourced from `application_events` (§Q1, shipped earlier this session) filtered to `event_type = 'applied'`, not `application_status_updated_at` (which only reflects the CURRENT stage's timestamp — genuinely different once a job has moved past "Applied"). Only renders when a real event row exists; older jobs applied to before §Q1 shipped simply show nothing rather than a fabricated number.
    - **Interview date/time** — explicitly NOT built, despite being agy's #1 Tier-1 recommendation. Reasoning: `interview_events.event_date` (§Q1) is stamped `now()` at the moment a user logs the transition, not a real future-scheduled calendar time — building an "upcoming interview" card element from it would misrepresent a past logging timestamp as a forward-looking deadline, violating this app's standing honesty-in-UI convention. Flagged as a real future feature, but only once there's an actual scheduling/calendar input to back it.
    - **Priority/star flag** — built. New `jobs.is_priority boolean default false` column (tiny migration, applied directly via `db query` since it doesn't touch the `auth` schema), `toggleJobPriority` action mirroring `toggleSaveJob`/`toggleHideJob`'s exact shape, a `Star` toggle button on each card (`fill-warning` when active).
    - **Salary, job type/remote badge** — built, and required zero new schema: `jobs.salary`/`job_type` already existed and were already being fetched via the page's existing `select("*")`, just not rendered on the Kanban card before. Remote badge reuses the same `/\bremote\b/i` title+location regex idiom already established on the job-details page and `JobActionBar` — no structured work-mode field exists in the source data, same caveat documented there.
    - **Quick-link icons, tags, contact person, application source** — skipped. The original-posting link already exists as the card's "View job" link; a résumé-version quick-link would need a new join per card (deferred, not zero-cost); tags/contact/source all need genuinely new data-entry systems agy's own Tier-3 ranking said belong in a details pane, not the card face — out of scope for this pass.
  - **Filter bar extended** (`MissionsFilterBar.tsx`): min match-score threshold (50/70/85, mirrors `FilterBar.tsx`'s own salary-threshold pattern), a "Needs attention" toggle (reuses `lib/jobStatus.ts`'s existing `getListingSignal` — zero new staleness logic), and a sort control (`SortValue`: `found` default / `match` / `stage`, where `stage` sorts oldest-status-update-first so the most-gone-quiet jobs surface at the top). `FilterPopover`'s previously-hardcoded `MapPin` icon was generalized to an `icon` prop so it could be reused for the new Target/ArrowUpDown-iconed popovers.
  - **Data threading**: `app/missions/page.tsx` now also queries `application_events` (event_type='applied') once and builds a `job_id -> earliest applied date` map, threaded as a plain prop (not merged into the `Job`/`KanbanJob` type) through `MissionsView` → `KanbanBoardLoader` → `KanbanBoard` → `KanbanColumn` → `KanbanCard`, since `appliedAt` isn't part of the base `jobs` row.
  - **Live-verified**: seeded real salary/location/an `application_events` row on a real tracked job — confirmed the card rendered `$180,000 - $220,000`, a `Remote` badge, and `Applied 12d ago` correctly, alongside every other real job's already-scraped salary rendering correctly across the board (not just the seeded one). Priority-star toggle confirmed via server log + direct DB read (`toggleJobPriority(..., true)` executed and persisted). Match-score/needs-attention/sort controls confirmed rendered and are type-checked/lint-clean, sharing the exact same tested filter-application pattern as the already-verified location/remote filters; not independently click-verified due to repeated browser-session/login friction in this tool this session — a lower-confidence tier than the salary/remote/star/applied-date items above, noted explicitly rather than silently assumed. All seeded test data cleaned up after. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean throughout.

- **2026-08-17 (Phase 14, cont'd): §Q2 Correction Memory → Adaptive Fit Scoring shipped.**
  - **Confirmed live, not assumed**: §B's "correctable skill tags" (`correctSkillTag`, `Qualification.tsx`) was already built and shipped in an earlier session, despite `build-plan.md`'s table still marking it 🆕 (stale doc, now known-stale — the table itself wasn't fixed this session, just noted as unreliable). Q2 builds directly on top of that existing action rather than re-building anything.
  - **New table `skill_corrections`** (user_id, job_id nullable/`ON DELETE SET NULL` — the memory deliberately outlives any single job, role_family text, skill text, correction_type enum `confirmed_have`/`confirmed_missing`, corrected_at). RLS own-row, no UPDATE policy (append-only, matches the "memory" framing — a correction is never edited, only ever added to).
  - **`correctSkillTag`** (`actions/jobs.ts`) now inserts a `skill_corrections` row alongside its existing in-place `matched_skills`/`missing_skills` mutation — `role_family` computed via `lib/interviewQuestions.ts`'s already-built `normalizeRoleFamily()`, zero new normalization logic. Best-effort: a failed insert doesn't fail the correction itself.
  - **`lib/evaluator.ts`'s `evaluateJobCompatibility`** gained an optional 5th `corrections: SkillCorrection[]` param. New `buildCorrectionsHint()` filters the full list down to the job's own `normalizeRoleFamily(title)` and appends "Candidate has previously confirmed for this kind of role — has: X; does not have: Y" to that job's own text block (not the shared candidate-profile context, since which corrections apply is per-job-role-family). Framed as a bias, not a hard override — the system prompt already treats it as reliable self-reported signal, not gospel.
  - **`lib/inngest/functions.ts`** fetches the user's full `skill_corrections` list once per evaluation run (cheap, typically small per user) and passes it through every chunk's `evaluateJobCompatibility` call.
  - **UX**: no new logging UI (the existing tag-click already does this) — `Qualification.tsx` gained a small line, only rendered when corrections exist for this job's role family: "Sortie remembered N thing(s) you told it about your {role family} skills." Count computed server-side in `app/find-jobs/[id]/page.tsx` via a cheap `{count:"exact", head:true}` query (same pattern already established in `lib/access.ts`'s signup-cap check).
  - **Live-verified (read side)**: seeded a real `skill_corrections` row via InsForge CLI for the Mastercard/Lead SDET test job, reloaded the page, confirmed the exact line rendered ("Sortie remembered 1 thing you told it about your Software Engineer (Java - SDET) skills."), cleaned up after. **Write side (the click → insert) verified via code review + `tsc`/`eslint` clean, not a live click** — repeated browser-automation timing flakiness this session (HMR-related, same class of issue hit earlier in Phase 14) made the actual click unreliable to drive; the change itself is a one-line addition after the pre-existing, already-proven `correctSkillTag` update call, same shape as the `application_events` insert that WAS live-verified earlier this session. Evaluator bias wiring is type-checked but not exercised against a real paid Gemini call (would burn real usage to confirm string interpolation that's already correct by inspection).

- **2026-08-17 (Phase 14): §Q1 Career Timeline/Graph — first slice shipped (real per-event application history), plus an admin-panel architecture researched and logged for later.**
  - **Scope call, made explicit up front**: §Q1's full spec (`build-plan.md` §Q1) also calls for a `career_roles` table migrating `profiles.work_experience` off jsonb — deliberately deferred. That's a separate, riskier migration that also means rewriting the existing Work Experience editor UI, and none of the three event tables need it to exist (`compensation_events.career_role_id` stays a bare nullable uuid, unused, until that table lands). This slice ships the genuinely new data — the real event history that didn't exist before.
  - **New tables**: `application_events` (job_id, event_type enum `applied`/`interview_scheduled`/`interview_completed`/`offer_received`/`rejected`/`ghosted`/`withdrawn`, event_date, notes), `interview_events` (job_id, panel_member_id FK `interview_panel_members` nullable, outcome enum, notes), `compensation_events` (user_id, career_role_id bare uuid unused for now, job_id nullable, event_type enum `offer`/`raise`/`bonus`/`equity_grant`, base_salary/bonus/equity_value, effective_date, notes). All RLS-enabled, own-row policies, mirrors `accomplishments`' shape.
  - **`actions/careerEvents.ts`** (new) — `logApplicationEvent`/`logInterviewEvent`/`logCompensationEvent`/`listApplicationEvents`/`listCompensationEvents`, plain CRUD, zero AI, mirrors `actions/accomplishments.ts` exactly.
  - **`setApplicationStatus`** (`actions/jobs.ts`) now writes a matching `application_events` row in the same call whenever the new status maps to a real event type (not `draft` — going back to draft is a correction, not an outcome) — best-effort, a failed event insert doesn't roll back or fail the status change itself.
  - **New "log outcome" note prompt** (`JobActionBar.tsx`'s `NotePromptPanel`) — picking a real-event status now opens a one-field optional/skippable note ("Marked as {Status} — add a note?", Skip / Log note), same portal/fixed-position idiom already established for `StatusMenuPanel`. Dismissing without a choice (click-away) still commits the status change with no note — the status transition itself was never optional, only the note is.
  - **`/career` gained a "Career view" / "Full timeline" toggle** (`CareerTimeline.tsx`) — epoch view stays the default; the new flat view (`lib/careerTimeline.ts`'s `buildFlatTimeline`) merges accomplishments + education + real `application_events` (with notes) into one reverse-chronological list, distinct dot colors per kind. This is where the real per-event history actually becomes visible — the epoch view's "Job search activity" section still only shows each job's current status, not the full transition history.
  - **A real migration-tooling gotcha hit and resolved, recorded in InsForge project memory**: this project's remote `db migrations` tracking table had 0 applied migrations despite ~48 tables already live (all prior schema changes were applied historically via `db query` raw SQL, migration files kept only as documentation) — `db migrations up --all` demanded replaying the entire history from `20260603114532_create-profiles.sql` ("relation already exists"). Fixed by temporarily moving all pre-existing migration files out of `migrations/`, applying just the new one via the tracked system (so it becomes the new remote head), then restoring the old files — future migrations should apply normally now. Separately confirmed `db query` explicitly rejects any DDL whose column `REFERENCES auth.users(id)` ("rejected for security reasons") — any table referencing the auth schema must go through `db migrations`, not `db query`, even for a read-only FK reference.
  - **Live-verified end to end** (test account, Mastercard/Lead SDET job): status changed draft→applied (note skipped, confirmed via DB the row landed with `notes: null`), then applied→interviewing with a real note ("test") logged via the prompt — confirmed both the `application_events` row and the `/career` flat timeline rendering "Interview scheduled — Lead Software Engineer (Java - SDET) at Mastercard / test / August 17, 2026" correctly merged above the older accomplishment. Test events deleted and job status reverted to draft after verification (job-status revert blocked mid-cleanup by a transient real InsForge 502 — confirmed via direct `curl` to the backend host, not an app bug; retry pending). `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean throughout.
  - **Admin panel — researched and logged in `build-plan.md` §R, explicitly not built.** User requested two independent research passes (a Perplexity generic-SaaS-admin write-up, then a live `agy` cross-check grounded in this app's real InsForge/Next.js stack) after asking for the admin panel to be UI-heavy, multi-admin-capable (someone else can run it while the founder is away), and structured for future tool integrations — a real pivot from agy's original "aggressively minimal, single hardcoded admin email" recommendation. Synthesized architecture: `admin_users` table (not an env-var email check) for multi-admin auth, `admin_audit_log` kept in (agy's original advice was to cut it, overridden once multi-admin is real), service-role-key client bypassing RLS entirely inside `/admin` (agy's core insight, kept), a real dashboard UI with charts/nav instead of "unstyled data tables," and route-grouped modules (`/admin/users`, `/admin/usage`, `/admin/billing` stub, `/admin/integrations`) sharing one `lib/admin/` data layer. **Deliberately sequenced last** per explicit user decision — build only after the rest of the feature/bug backlog is worked through.

- **2026-08-17 (Phase 13): §K Trust/legal fundamentals — Privacy Policy + Terms pages, a real gap fixed in account deletion, and a ground-truth multi-tenancy RLS audit.**
  - **Privacy Policy (`/privacy`) and Terms (`/terms`) shipped as real pages**, not stubs — grounded in this app's actual data practices (every real vendor named: InsForge, Gemini/OpenAI/Anthropic, SerpApi, Apify, Perplexity, Vercel, PostHog), explicitly states the no-auto-apply policy as a trust point, and documents the real export (Career Record) and deletion (Settings) rights that already exist. `Footer.tsx`'s two dead `href="#"` links now point at them.
  - **Real gap found and fixed in the existing `deleteAccount()` action** (`actions/account.ts` — already built and previously live-verified in an earlier session, not new this session): `resumes` (added 2026-07-30, after this action was last touched) has **no FK constraint to `auth.users` at all**, so its rows — and their storage files — were silently orphaned on account deletion instead of being erased. Fixed: `resumes.storage_path` added to the best-effort storage cleanup, `DELETE FROM resumes` added to the SQL sequence. Verified the fix's SQL directly against a synthetic disposable row (not the real test account) — confirmed zero rows remain after delete. The 4 other user-data tables added since this action was last verified (`accomplishments`, `interview_panel_members`, `star_stories`, `agent_messages`) all reference `auth.users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE` directly from the migrations that created them, so they were already covered without needing an explicit delete — confirmed live via `information_schema.referential_constraints`, not assumed from migration-file text (which this session has repeatedly found can drift from live schema state).
  - **Multi-tenancy RLS isolation audit — ground truth, not migration-file trust.** Queried `pg_class.relrowsecurity` directly: all 13 `public` tables have RLS enabled, none missing. Queried `pg_policies` directly: every real user-data table has `auth.uid() = user_id` (or `= id` for `profiles`) scoping every policy; the only unrestricted (`USING (true)`) policies found are the two already-documented intentional ones on `interview_question_banks` (the one deliberately shared, non-personal reference table). Missing DELETE policies on `agent_messages`/`rate_limit`/`usage_daily` are consistent with intentional design (prevents users tampering with their own quota/rate-limit counters), not an isolation gap. **No real cross-user isolation gaps found.**
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean throughout.

- **2026-08-14 (Phase 12, cont'd): Navigator shipped — build-plan.md §O, both a dedicated `/agent` page and a floating global launcher.**
  - **Reversed a prior scope decision, per direct user correction.** §O originally concluded (2026-08-13) that JobRight's "Orion" was page-only, not a floating widget, and scoped Navigator the same way. The next day, direct user feedback on Orion's real UI reversed this — v1 ships both.
  - New `agent_messages` table — a real persisted, shared conversation (not ephemeral like `useDocumentChat`'s local-only state) — the full page and the floating popover read/write the same history, live-verified (send from one, see it on the other after refresh).
  - `actions/agent.ts`'s `sendAgentMessage` assembles a real server-side data snapshot (profile, live Kanban tracker staleness via the same `daysSince()` pattern `rejectionIntelligence.ts` established, the focused job's real evaluation if launched from a job page) into one grounded Gemini call (`lib/agentAssistant.ts`), Zod-validated `{reply, action}` output. `confirmAgentAction` is the only place a proposed action actually executes, gated behind an explicit user click — action-confirm card styled like `EditorTab.tsx`'s `BulletDiffCard` (agent-teal Accept/Discard), not the red `ConfirmDialog` treatment.
  - Floating launcher (`NavigatorLauncher.tsx` + `NavigatorLauncherLoader.tsx`, mounted in `app/layout.tsx`) follows the exact `dynamic(..., {ssr:false})` wrapper pattern `SettingsModalLoader.tsx` already established for the same cold-load hydration reason. Gated off public routes (`/`, `/login`, `/waitlist`, `/preview/*`) via pathname check, since every Navigator action requires a logged-in user and this app has no shared authenticated-layout to read auth state from at the root.
  - v1 ships all 3 planned actions: match-score diagnosis, tracker triage, accomplishment logging.
  - **Real UX bug caught live and fixed before shipping**: replies rendered as a flat `<p>`, collapsing newlines — a "what can you do?" answer came back as one dense paragraph instead of a real bulleted list (user caught this with a side-by-side JobRight screenshot). Fixed with an explicit system-prompt formatting rule plus `NavigatorChat.tsx` parsing `- `-prefixed lines into a real `<ul><li>`.
  - **Live-verified with the real seeded test account**: capability listing rendered as 3 real `<li>` elements; tracker triage correctly cited the real seeded Stripe job's actual `interviewing` status; a real accomplishment was proposed and, on Accept, actually inserted a real `accomplishments` row (confirmed via direct DB query) — one retry was needed after a transient InsForge network error surfaced honestly rather than failing silently.
  - Migration `20260814040000_add-agent-messages-table.sql`, applied live via `db query`. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean throughout.

- **2026-08-14 (Phase 12): Engagement War Room Phase 2 "Arsenal" shipped — Trap Door Predictor, The Interrogation Plan, STAR Story Matrix — plus a full job-detail-page restructure into a dedicated "Interview Prep Room" tab, a major Question Bank depth upgrade, and a real production bug found and fixed live.**
  - **Trap Door Predictor** (`lib/trapDoorPredictor.ts`, `components/job-details/TrapDoorPredictor.tsx`) — predicts 2-4 tough/uncomfortable interview questions grounded ONLY in a job's own already-stored risk signals (`company_research.culture`/`gapsToAddress`/`recentUpdates`, `strategic_moat.existentialThreats`, `title_scope_mismatch`), same honesty idiom as `lib/rejectionIntelligence.ts` — every prediction must cite specific supplied data, falls back to grounded-but-generic prep questions rather than fabricating risk when none is present. New `jobs.trap_door_predictions`/`trap_door_predicted_at` columns, new `trap_door_prediction` usage key (5/day).
  - **The Interrogation Plan** (`lib/interrogationPlan.ts`, `components/job-details/InterrogationPlan.tsx`) — synthesizes questions the CANDIDATE should ask, purely from two already-shipped data sources: `strategic_moat.smartQuestions` (general) + each `interview_panel_members` row's `researched_background` (personalized per named interviewer, citing only their real prior companies/summary). New `jobs.interrogation_plan`/`interrogation_plan_generated_at` columns, new `interrogation_plan` usage key (5/day).
  - **STAR Story Matrix** (`lib/starStoryMatcher.ts`, `actions/starStories.ts`, `components/interview/StarStoryMatrix.tsx`) — candidate-tied, not job-tied, lives on the global `/interview` page. New per-user `star_stories` table (title/situation/task/action/result/tags, zero-AI CRUD, mirrors `accomplishments`' shape) plus an on-demand matcher (`matchStoriesToRole`) that reuses the existing cached Question Bank and runs one Gemini call mapping stories to questions — result is ephemeral (not persisted), since it's a function of the user's current mutable story set. New `star_story_matching` usage key (5/day).
  - **Job detail page restructured, per direct user request mid-session**: `InterviewPanel`/`QuestionBankPanel` (previously stacked inline in the Overview tab) plus the two new features above were carved into a new "Interview Prep Room" tab, only present when `application_status === "interviewing"` and set as the page's default tab in that case (closing a real UX gap the user flagged — `/interview`'s "Enter prep room" link went to the generic Overview tab, not anything actually prep-specific). **Layout researched via `agy`**: a 2-column "Command Center" (main 65%: Question Bank + Interrogation Plan; sidebar 35%: Trap Door Predictor + Interview Panel), reading order Risk → Context → Defense → Offense. **Real accessibility bug caught and fixed before shipping**: the first pass used CSS `order` classes to get this reading order, but `order` only changes *visual* layout, not DOM/accessibility-tree order — a screen reader or `get_page_text` extraction would've heard Question Bank first regardless of the visual arrangement. Fixed by making the actual DOM order risk-first (Trap Door + Panel mounted first) and using `md:order-*` only to flip the visual column position on desktop.
  - **Question Bank got a major depth upgrade, per direct user request** ("I need everything JobRight gives, not just questions") after the user shared a live JobRight screenshot showing per-question Insider Tips / step-by-step Approach / a labeled solution. Researched via `agy`: lazy, per-question generation (not bundled into the bulk bank call — keeps the initial fetch fast and avoids quality degradation toward the end of a long list). New discriminated-union `QuestionDetails` type in `lib/interviewQuestions.ts` (`technical`: insiderTips + approachSteps + a code solution w/ complexity; `system_design`: insiderTips + approachSteps + a prose design walkthrough, deliberately no code; `behavioral`/`culture_fit`: insiderTips + a STAR framework outline, not a canned answer). New `getQuestionDetails` action (`actions/interviewQuestions.ts`) — addressed by array index (not a new id column, so it works against every already-cached bank in production with no backfill), a cache hit is a free read same as the bank itself. New `question_detail_generation` usage key (20/day). `QuestionBankPanel.tsx` gained category-pill filtering and a click-to-open "Study View" — honestly labeled ("AI reference implementation" / "AI strategy guide", never "verified", explicit disclaimer footer) per the same research pass. **Real bug caught live during verification**: the Study View is a `position: fixed` panel, but it's mounted deep inside the job-details page's `.fade-in-up`-animated tab tree — an `animation-fill-mode: both` ancestor with a `transform` in its keyframes permanently establishes a new containing block, silently breaking `fixed` positioning for any descendant (confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()`: the backdrop rendered at the ancestor's scroll-relative offset instead of the viewport). Fixed by portaling to `document.body` via `createPortal` — same fix class this codebase already uses for portal-clipping bugs elsewhere. **This same containing-block trap likely affects other `position:fixed` elements already nested under any `.fade-in-up` wrapper in this codebase (e.g. `ConfirmDialog` usages on this same page) — not audited/fixed here, flagged for a future pass.**
  - **Auto-chain prerequisites, per explicit user direction mid-session**: Trap Door Predictor and Interrogation Plan's buttons initially hard-blocked (disabled button / error message) when their prerequisite data didn't exist yet (Company Research, Strategic Moat Briefing). Per direct user correction — "if it requires and there was no call, you can automatically call it unless it cost us" — both actions now auto-generate the missing prerequisite internally on click rather than forcing a trip to another tab first: `getTrapDoorPredictions` auto-runs `researchCompany` if `company_research` is null, `getInterrogationPlan` auto-runs `researchStrategicMoat` if `strategic_moat` is null. Justified as *not* a new cost category — both share the same free-primary-path (Jina Reader)/real-$-fallback (Perplexity, only when blocked) profile already accepted everywhere else these run (e.g. `CompanyResearchAutoLoader`). The buttons themselves stay manual/opt-in — nothing auto-fires on page load, only on an explicit click.
  - **Real, pre-existing production bug found and fixed, caught live by the user testing on `jobpilot-experiment.vercel.app` mid-session (unrelated to this session's own changes — nothing had been deployed yet)**: Company Research was failing with a 500 on a real production job. Root-caused via `vercel logs` to `TypeError: e.matched_skills is not iterable` inside `agent/research.ts`'s `buildFallbackDossier()` — the *fallback* path itself (meant to gracefully recover when the primary AI response fails to parse) crashed on a job whose `matched_skills`/`missing_skills` were `null` instead of `[]`, turning a soft recovery into a hard 500. Same bug class as a fix already shipped for `CompanyResearchAutoLoader` in Phase 11, but this specific spot (`buildFallbackDossier`'s `techStack`/`gapsToAddress` construction, `agent/research.ts:620`/`630`) was missed. Fixed with the same `?? []` guard. **Fixed locally, not yet deployed** — production will keep failing on this exact path until this fix ships.
  - **Migrations**: `20260814010000_add-jobs-trap-door-predictions.sql`, `20260814020000_add-jobs-interrogation-plan.sql`, `20260814030000_add-star-stories-table.sql` — all applied live via `db query` (this project's remote migration ledger remains untracked by the CLI's own `db migrations` system — confirmed again this session via `db migrations list` returning "No database migrations found" despite 14+ prior migrations already live), then written to match.
  - **`tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean throughout.** Live-verified via seeded test data (InsForge CLI raw SQL: a disposable Stripe/Senior Software Engineer test job set to `interviewing`, a researched panelist, a seeded STAR story) — Trap Door Predictor generated real, correctly-grounded predictions (citing the exact seeded Adyen-competition/scope-mismatch/layoffs signals); the Interview Prep Room tab, category-pill Question Bank, and the Study View's lazy-generated STAR framework content all confirmed rendering with real data after the portal fix. **One test-data gap caught mid-verification**: the seeded `company_research` was missing the required `sources` field, which briefly 500'd the Company tab the same way — not an app bug, a test-fixture gap, patched via `db query`. Dev-server/browser session was lost once mid-session (external process restart, not an app issue) and successfully re-verified after restarting.

- **2026-08-13 (Phase 11, cont'd): Cached AI Interview Question Bank shipped — build-plan.md §N Phase 1's last unbuilt piece, closing out Reconnaissance.**
  - **First genuinely shared, non-user-owned table in this schema**: new `interview_question_banks` (cache_key unique, company, role_family, seniority, questions jsonb, generated_at) — RLS is SELECT/INSERT for any authenticated user, no `user_id` column at all, since the whole point is one generated bank gets reused across every user asking about the same (company, role_family, seniority) combination. Applied directly via `db query` (single-statement calls — a multi-statement query got rejected as unparseable, and separately `db migrations up --all` failed with "relation profiles already exists," confirming this project's already-documented migration-ledger gap is still present).
  - **`lib/interviewQuestions.ts`**: `normalizeRoleFamily()` strips seniority words ("Senior", "Staff", "Lead", etc.) from a job title via regex so "Senior Software Engineer" and "Staff Software Engineer" collapse to the same cache key (seniority is tracked as its own dimension) — a coarse heuristic, not a full taxonomy classifier, good enough for cache reuse. `generateQuestionBank()` — one Gemini call per cache miss, explicitly instructed it has no access to real leaked questions and must generate realistic *predicted* ones, reuses `HUMANIZED_WRITING_RULES` from the writing-style work earlier this session.
  - **`actions/interviewQuestions.ts`**: `getOrGenerateQuestionBank()` checks the cache first (free read, no rate limit, no usage consumed) and only gates/generates on a genuine miss. Handles the concurrent-miss race (two users asking about the same new combo at once) by falling back to reading whichever request's insert actually won the unique-constraint race, instead of surfacing a spurious error to the loser.
  - **`components/interview/QuestionBankPanel.tsx`** — one component, two modes, per the agy research on page structure (§O below): `locked=false` (global `/interview` page) shows editable Company/Role/Seniority inputs; `locked=true` (job detail page embed) auto-fetches on mount, pre-filled from the job's own `company`/`title`/`seniority_level`. **Real lint bug caught by `eslint`, not review**: the auto-fetch effect's `runLookup()` called `setState` synchronously before its first `await`, tripping `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` — fixed by moving the entire function body (including the validation branch) inside `startTransition()`, matching the exact pattern `CompanyResearchAutoLoader.tsx` already established for this same class of component.
  - **`/interview` page rewritten** from its `ComingSoon` placeholder into a real "Command Center" — an Active Missions list (jobs at `application_status = 'interviewing'`, linking into each job's own detail page) above the global Question Bank search, per agy's researched structure ("data can be global, but the UX should be contextual" — Panel Topology/Trap Door Predictor/Interrogation Plan stay job-scoped, only the Question Bank and the future STAR Story Matrix belong on the global page).
  - **Live-verified with real generation, not just compile-checked**: a real "Stripe / Software Engineer / Senior" search produced genuinely Stripe-specific questions (idempotent webhook design, distributed-database CAP-theorem consistency, rate-limiting at scale) grounded in their actual business, not generic filler. **Cache hit confirmed via server logs**: first call 6.7s (real Gemini generation), identical second call 1.25s (pure DB read, zero AI calls, confirmed via `SELECT count(*)` staying at 1 row). **Job-embed confirmed** by temporarily setting a real test job (Mastercard, Lead SDET role) to `interviewing` status — the panel auto-fetched with zero clicks, correctly normalized "Lead Software Engineer (Java - SDET)" down to role_family "Software Engineer (Java - SDET)" at seniority "Lead," and produced genuinely Mastercard/PCI-compliance/payments-specific questions. Test job status reverted after verification; the Stripe cache entry was left in place since it's real, reusable content, not test pollution.
  - **v1 scope, deliberately not the full §N/§O plan**: STAR Story Matrix, Trap Door Predictor, and the Interrogation Plan are explicitly deferred — see build-plan.md §N/§O for the full remaining roadmap.


## Current Status

- **2026-08-13 (Phase 11, cont'd): Find & Evaluate got a real filter bar (9 filters, replacing 2 loose text boxes), and the Career page was restructured from a flat timeline into an epoch-based nested structure per the agy research already logged in build-plan.md §E.**
  - **Filter bar shipped**: `lib/jobFilters.ts` + `components/find-jobs/FilterBar.tsx` — Date Posted (goes to SerpApi's `chips` param, debounced 700ms auto-fire since it's the one filter that costs a real paid call), Remote Policy, Job Type, Min Salary, Experience Level, Min Match Score, Hide Keyword, Company, and Visa Sponsorship (client-side, free — everything already fetched/scored). **3 real bugs caught live by the user, not code review**: the "More filters" panel's outside-click listener checked the wrong DOM container (closed on any click inside it, including the very interactions it was supposed to allow) — fixed by relying on `FilterPanel`'s own already-correct check instead of a duplicate buggy one; a genuinely empty Date-Posted-narrowed search hit "Insforge upsert did not return any saved jobs" (an empty result treated as a hard failure instead of a legitimate empty state) — fixed in `lib/actions/scraper.actions.ts` to return `[]` early; the visa-sponsorship toggle switch's knob had no explicit `left` position and low-contrast colors, reading as "just changes color" rather than a real slider — fixed with explicit positioning and a bordered/shadowed knob.
  - **Career page epoch restructure**: new `lib/careerTimeline.ts` (`buildCareerEpochs`, date-range containment matching accomplishments to whichever work-experience role's `[start_date, end_date ?? today-if-current]` range contains them — auto-computed, no manual role picker needed) replaces the old flat `buildTimeline` in `app/career/page.tsx`. `CareerTimeline.tsx` rebuilt around `EpochCard`s (one per role, accomplishments nested inside, collapsible) plus separate Unassigned/Education/Job-Search-Activity sections. New inline `QuickAddBar` — a single text input + Enter, no modal, always dated today, naturally auto-nests into whichever role is current. **Live-verified with real seeded test data** (InsForge CLI raw SQL: 2 roles, 3 accomplishments spanning both roles' ranges plus one pre-career date) — confirmed correct nesting into each role, correct Unassigned fallback for the pre-career entry, and the quick-add bar correctly auto-nesting a new entry under the current role. Test data cleaned up after verification (both the seed and the cleanup DELETE required explicit user approval — the classifier blocks destructive SQL by default).
  - **Real pre-existing bug found and flagged, not fixed in this pass (separate concern)**: `lib/utils.ts`'s `formatDate()` parses a bare `"YYYY-MM-DD"` string as UTC midnight, then renders via `toLocaleDateString()` in the local timezone — rolls back one calendar day on any machine behind UTC. Affects every date display across the whole app (not Career-specific), confirmed live during this verification (`"2022-07-01"` displayed as "June 30, 2022"). Logged here as a real, separate bug to fix, not folded into this restructure's scope.
  - **A real InsForge platform outage hit mid-session** (confirmed via direct network unreachability — curl/ping timeout to the backend host — plus InsForge's own automated "InstanceDown" alert email and a 404 on their own dashboard for a verified-correct project ID) — affected production and local dev identically since both hit the same backend. Resolved on InsForge's own side without any app-side fix; DNS/connectivity re-confirmed healthy afterward. Not a Sortie bug — logged here only so a future session doesn't waste time re-diagnosing the same class of symptom (OAuth "could not start sign-in method," stuck job polling) as an app issue before ruling out a live InsForge incident first.

- **2026-08-13 (Phase 11, end of session): SerpApi fallback generalized to a 3-key chain, every résumé/cover-letter AI prompt humanized with a Google XYZ bullet formula, and a real mobile bug fixed with a new standing rule.**
  - **3-key SerpApi fallback chain** (`lib/jobScraper.ts`) — the 2-key version from earlier this session (entry below) was refactored into `getSerpApiKeyChain()`, a filtered array of `SERPAPI_KEY`/`SERPAPI_KEY_FALLBACK`/`SERPAPI_KEY_FALLBACK_2` tried in order via a loop, advancing only on a real quota-exhaustion error. A third real SerpApi account key (user's own) added to both `.env` and Vercel production env (`vercel env add`, piped via stdin, never printed to the transcript). `tsc`/`eslint` clean; deployed to production.
  - **Every résumé/cover-letter AI generation prompt humanized, per explicit user direction — "go to Wikipedia, find the signs of AI writing, list every pattern, and never do any of those things."** Fetched Wikipedia's real "Signs of AI writing" essay live (not recalled from memory) and filtered its patterns down to what applies to prose. New `lib/writingStyle.ts` exports two shared constants, wired into 6 call sites across `agent/documents.ts` (`generateTailoredResume`/`generateCoverLetter`/`reviseTailoredResume`/`reviseCoverLetter`), `actions/documents.ts` (`rewriteResumeBullet`), and `actions/resumes.ts` (`rewriteResumeSlotBullet`): `HUMANIZED_WRITING_RULES` bans em dashes for asides, the "delve/boasts/crucial/meticulous/tapestry/pivotal/leverage/foster/seamless"-class AI vocabulary, "not just X but Y" contrast constructions, vague unquantified "-ing" sentence tails, mechanical rule-of-three padding, and copula avoidance ("serves as" instead of "is"). `BULLET_QUALITY_RULES` applies Google's XYZ formula ("Accomplished X as measured by Y, by doing Z") to work-experience bullets, requires real quantified achievements with no negative framing, and mirrors the target job posting's own action words. **Initially also wired into `actions/profile.ts`'s 3 base-profile bullet tools (`rewriteBullet`/`splitBullet`/`generateBullets`), then explicitly reverted per direct user correction mid-session** — "you don't have to show this xyz formula in the work experience under profile, this all are just for resume and cover letter generation." The profile page stays plain source-of-truth data entry; the polish is scoped to the actual output surface. **Live-verified with a real `generateBullets` call** (Profile → Work Experience → "Generate bullet options from a note", a test entry added and discarded without saving) — all 4 generated options had zero em dashes, zero banned vocabulary, no "not just X but Y" constructions, result-first framing, and correctly did not invent a metric ("3 junior engineers" — the only real number in the input note — was kept; no cost/time percentage was fabricated).

Fixed with a real `sm:hidden` stacked-card layout carrying identical data/actions to the desktop table (now `hidden sm:block`); the row-actions menu's shared open-state needed a `view: "mobile" | "desktop"` tag since both layouts mount simultaneously in the DOM (one CSS-hidden) and would otherwise both respond to the same trigger. New "Mobile / Responsive" section added to `ui-rules.md` as a standing rule for every future component. **User explicitly narrowed scope mid-session** — not a full-app mobile audit ("I guess all other pages are seems accessible in mobile as well"), just this concrete bug plus the standing rule going forward; a planned task-list for auditing Missions/Profile/Résumé pages was created then deliberately deleted per that direction.

- **2026-08-13 (Phase 11): Job-scoring poll bug fixed on production, SerpApi fallback wired in, and a professional error-handling sweep shipped.**
  - **Real production bug, user-reported and live-verified fixed**: `getJobsByIds` (`lib/actions/scraper.actions.ts`) — the function the find-jobs page's 3-second poll calls — built a fresh **anon-key** InsForge client with no auth/cookies instead of the cookie-authenticated `createInsforgeServer()` used everywhere else in that file. `jobs` has RLS (`auth.uid() = user_id`), so the poll's every-3-second query silently returned zero rows forever — no error, just nothing to update — which is exactly why cards got stuck on "Scoring…" until a full page reload (clicking into a job and back) picked up the real score via the authenticated server-render path instead. Fixed by swapping to `createInsforgeServer()`. Deployed to production (`jobpilot-experiment.vercel.app`) and confirmed live with real searches ("Software Developer"/Toronto, "Software Engineer"/Vancouver) — cards visibly flip from "Scoring…" to real scores in place, no navigation needed.
  - **SerpApi fallback key wired in** (`lib/jobScraper.ts`) — user is close to their monthly SerpApi quota. `serpApiProvider` now retries with a second `SERPAPI_KEY_FALLBACK` key when the primary reports quota exhaustion, detected by error text (SerpApi returns HTTP 200 + a `data.error` string, no distinct status code to key off) — an unrelated error (e.g. no results) still only retries location resolution on the same key, never burning the fallback needlessly. Verified with a mocked-fetch test (no live API calls) before deploying. Fallback key added to both `.env` and Vercel production env.
  - **Alternative-provider research, via 2 `agy` passes + a live test**: DataForSEO confirmed as the real long-term fallback (same Google Jobs structured data — city-level location, real employer-vs-aggregator apply links — pay-as-you-go, no subscription); Serpdog as a thinner free-tier option; **Serper.dev ruled out** (no `google_jobs` endpoint at all, confirmed against their own docs — the old dead `serperProvider` stub was deleted, not implemented). **Jooble live-tested with a real user-obtained API key and rejected for this app specifically**: real free job data, but location only resolves to country-level ("Canada", not "Toronto, ON" — verified with 2 separate city-scoped queries), and every apply link is a `jooble.org` redirect rather than the employer's own page — breaks both `normalizeLocationForSerpApi`'s city filter and `pickApplyUrl`'s employer-over-aggregator preference, this app's two real, deliberate product decisions. DataForSEO not yet implemented (needs an account/key). Full comparison table and reasoning in `build-plan.md` §25.
  - **Professional error-handling sweep, researched via 2 `agy` passes** (structure: title/body/action, never leak vendor names or HTTP codes; zero-results is an empty state, not an error; central normalizer over per-component fixes). New `lib/errors.ts` — a **denylist**, not whitelist: `toUserMessage()` only rewrites text that looks like a leaked technical/vendor error (SerpApi quota/location/no-results phrasing, AI rate limits, network failures, raw JS exceptions); everything else — and this codebase's server actions turned out to already be quite disciplined about clean, hand-authored error copy — passes through unchanged. Concrete fixes: (1) SerpApi's "hasn't returned any results" now returns `[]` instead of throwing — a real search outcome, not a failure — with `FindJobsForm.tsx` gaining an actual empty-state message instead of silently rendering nothing; (2) new `app/error.tsx` root error boundary, replacing Next.js's raw "An error occurred in the Server Components render…" leak (hit live this session) with the app's own ConfirmDialog-style icon+title+body chrome and a real Try Again action; (3) 3 duplicated ad-hoc 429/quota-detection regexes (`actions/profile.ts` ×2, `lib/resumeQuality.ts`) consolidated into shared `isRateLimitError()`/`rateLimitMessage()`; (4) all ~14 API routes that either leaked InsForge's raw `error.message` (3 auth routes) or returned a cold literal `"Internal server error"` (10 routes) now route through `toUserMessage()`. Verified via mocked tests (no live network calls) for both the no-results fix and the normalizer's rule set, plus `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint .` clean across the whole project. **Not yet click-tested live** — Browser pane compositing was unreliable this session (a tooling issue, not traced to the app); the mocked tests substitute for that but a real click-through of the empty-state message and the error boundary is still owed next session.
