Fixed and live-verified end to end: clicking "Continue with Google" on the production URL now correctly reaches Google's real account-chooser page.
  - **A separate, pre-existing config gap surfaced (not caused) by this work**: `config apply` for any auth change now fails on `[auth.smtp]` — `auth.smtp.password references env(SMTP_PASSWORD) but no such secret exists`, because the Brevo SMTP password was originally set via a raw admin API call back in Phase 9, never registered as a named InsForge secret via `config apply`'s own expected flow. Worked around by omitting `[auth.smtp]` from `insforge.toml` entirely for this session's redirect-URL change — confirmed InsForge's apply treats an omitted section as "don't touch," not "reset to default," so this was safe. Still open: register the real Brevo password as the `SMTP_PASSWORD` secret (value not available this session) so future `config apply` runs touching auth don't need this workaround.
  - **Wife's account**: per explicit user decision, she'll create her own separate account rather than share the existing one — intentionally not pre-provisioned this session.
  - **ATS-scoring research done via `agy`, zero code written.** User asked how to add ATS (Applicant Tracking System) compatibility scoring for résumés/cover letters "free or with very minimum cost." Research came back with a hybrid architecture matching this app's own established pattern (cheap deterministic checks + one grounded LLM call, same shape as `lib/evaluator.ts`): zero-cost structural checks this app can already compute exactly (column-layout risk per `ResumePDF.tsx` template, font safety, non-standard section headers, missing contact fields) plus a Gemini free-tier call for keyword-match/quantification scoring — not a paid third-party ATS API. Full rubric and a draft `lib/atsChecker.ts` came back from the research pass; a real candidate for a future session, not started.
  - **Two more real production bugs, both caught live by the user actually searching on the deployed site (not caught by any automated check), both root-caused via `vercel logs`/source inspection rather than guessed:**
    - **`.env` was silently uploaded as part of `vercel deploy`** despite being gitignored (Vercel CLI's default file-selection doesn't reliably mirror `.gitignore` for this command) — Next.js loaded it during the build, and since `INNGEST_DEV=1` wasn't set anywhere else (Vercel's dashboard vars correctly didn't include it), dotenv's "don't override an existing var" rule didn't apply and it defined `INNGEST_DEV` fresh, silently forcing Inngest into local-dev mode in production. Confirmed by reading the Inngest SDK's own source in `node_modules` (`Inngest.js`'s `get mode()`) rather than guessing, and by `/api/inngest`'s own diagnostic response literally reporting `"mode":"dev"`. Fixed with a new `.vercelignore` (`.env`, `.env.local`, `.env.*.local`, `.insforge`), confirmed via the same endpoint flipping to requiring signed auth (real cloud behavior) after redeploy.
    - **A genuine pre-existing bug in `lib/inngest/functions.ts`, exposed only by Inngest Cloud's stricter engine**: `evaluateJobsAsync`'s per-chunk loop called `step.sleep("delay-between-ai-calls", "3s")` with the literal same step ID on every iteration (and `step.run` had the same latent issue, keyed only by `chunk.length` which collides whenever two chunks happen to be the same size). Inngest Cloud logged `Duplicate step ID "delay-between-ai-calls" detected across parallel chains` (`AUTOMATIC_PARALLEL_INDEXING`) and the function silently stalled after evaluating only the first chunk of any multi-chunk batch — every job past the first 5 stayed on "Scoring…" forever, with no error surfaced to the user. This class of bug apparently never manifested against the local Inngest Dev Server (less strict about ID reuse), so it was real and pre-existing, just never triggered until this session's first production traffic. Fixed by keying both step IDs on the loop index (`evaluate-chunk-${chunkIndex}`, `delay-between-ai-calls-${chunkIndex}`) — genuinely unique per run regardless of chunk sizes. **Live-verified end to end after redeploy + re-sync**: inserted 7 fresh test jobs (splits into 2 chunks at the existing chunk-size-5), manually POSTed a real `jobs/evaluate` event to Inngest's Event API (extracted the real event key via `vercel env pull` into a gitignored temp file, used it in one script, deleted the file immediately after — never printed), and confirmed all 7 received real match scores, not just the first 5.
    - Also had to manually trigger `PUT /api/inngest` twice this session (once after the mode fix, once after the step-ID fix) to force Inngest Cloud to re-sync the app's registered functions — the Vercel-Inngest Marketplace integration's auto-sync-on-deploy either didn't fire or hadn't caught up either time. Documented as a standing troubleshooting step in `RESUME.md`'s top block if this recurs.
    - **Old test jobs from before the step-ID fix are permanently stuck** (their Inngest function run checkpointed against the buggy duplicate-ID code path and won't recover) — harmless leftover test data in the disposable test account, not a real user's data, safe to ignore or delete.
  - **A fourth real production issue, root-caused AND fixed live, with 3 compounding causes**: user reported Strategic Moat Briefing failing with "Could not find recent information about this company" on a real job (Microsoft). (1) `PERPLEXITY_API_KEY` was dead — direct `curl` to Perplexity's API with the literal `.env` value returned `401 invalid_api_key`, confirmed independent of Vercel (failed locally too). User regenerated a fresh key at perplexity.ai/settings/api; validated it directly via `curl` (real $0.005 test call, disclosed), then swapped it into both Vercel (`env rm` + `env add`) and local `.env` — no redeploy needed since it's a server-only var Vercel injects per-request. (2) Separately, the free path (`researchStrategicMoat` builds a `google.com/search?q=...` URL, fetches via Jina Reader) is being CAPTCHA-blocked by Google — confirmed by fetching the identical URL directly and getting a real "unusual traffic detected" wall (~1000 chars) instead of search results. (3) **The actual code bug**: that ~1000-char block page sailed past the existing `sourceText.length < 200` check, so even with a working Perplexity key, the fallback never triggered — the CAPTCHA wall was silently treated as real content. Fixed with a new `looksLikeSearchBlockPage()` helper in `agent/research.ts` (checks for Google/Jina's own block-page phrases), wired into both `researchStrategicMoat` and `researchInterviewerBackground` (Interview Panel Topology's lookup — identical vulnerable pattern, same fix applied preemptively). Explicitly decided NOT to attempt any CAPTCHA-evasion workaround (rotating IPs/user-agents, etc.) — out of scope, adversarial-scraping territory this project stays out of. **Live-verified end to end after all 3 fixes + redeploy + Inngest re-sync**: a real Strategic Moat Briefing for Microsoft returned specific, correctly-grounded strategic priorities (Azure/Copilot AI capacity, agentic workflows, custom silicon, AI governance) — not an error, not generic filler. Also fixed in passing: the Company Research auto-loader's `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'join')` crash when `matched_skills`/`missing_skills` are `null` (real scraper→evaluator jobs never hit this; only a raw-SQL test job did) — added `?? []` guards.
  - **ATS Compatibility Score shipped for résumés** (`lib/atsChecker.ts`, `components/documents/ATSAuditCard.tsx`), the feature researched via `agy` earlier this session, now actually built — inside `ResumeWorkspace`'s existing AI Rewrite tab, next to the fit-score gauge and `QualityGradeCard`. Deliberately zero-cost and always-live rather than a usage-gated AI action: a deterministic formatting audit (multi-column-template risk, non-standard section headers, missing profile contact fields) computed directly from `ResumeStyle`/`ResumeSection[]`, plus a keyword-match score that reuses `scoreJump.matchedKeywords`/`missingKeywords` — data the fit-score gauge already computes — instead of a second AI call re-deriving the same thing. No new DB columns, no new usage key. Font-safety and bullet-glyph checks were deliberately left out of the formatting audit — `ResumePDF.tsx` only ever renders the PDF standard-14 fonts and `bulletStyle` is constrained to 3 safe glyphs, so those risks can't actually occur in this app; checking for them would be fabricating a risk. **Live-verified with real math**, not just a clean render: a disposable test job/application with the "split" template and a "My Career Journey" header scored exactly 14 (50 − 20 multi-column − 6 header − 10 missing contact), then switching the template live in the Style tab moved the score to exactly 34 in real time, no reload, confirming the `useMemo`-driven reactivity. Note for whoever picks this up next: it only appears on résumés generated/edited *after* this deploy — the score is computed live from data already in the workspace, so it works retroactively on old tailored résumés too the moment they're opened, nothing to backfill.
  - **Cover letter ATS scoring shipped same session, as a real fast-follow** (`analyzeCoverLetterATS` in `lib/atsChecker.ts`, `components/documents/CoverLetterATSAuditCard.tsx`), wired into `CoverLetterWorkspace.tsx`'s Editor tab above the Salutation field. Cover letters have no `ResumeStyle`/`ResumeSection[]`-equivalent structure and no existing keyword-match infrastructure to reuse the way the résumé version does, so this is fully deterministic instead — word count (200-400 sweet spot), whether the letter actually names the company anywhere (a real, cheap personalization proxy), a generic-salutation phrase check ("To Whom It May Concern" and variants), and the same multi-column `"split"`-template sidebar risk — confirmed real for cover letters too by reading `CoverLetterPDF.tsx` directly (it genuinely renders a contact/skills sidebar for that one template, not assumed from the résumé's behavior; "executive" does NOT trigger it for letters, unlike for résumés). **Live-verified with real math**: a disposable test letter (41 words, "split" template, no mention of the test company "Initech," salutation "To Whom It May Concern") scored exactly 15/100 = 100 − 20 (too short) − 30 (multi-column) − 20 (no company mention) − 15 (generic salutation). Deployed to production same session as this entry.
  - Subscription/pricing model remains explicitly deferred to right before launch, unchanged from the Phase 9 note below.

- **2026-08-12 (Phase 9 continued): Career Record shipped, 4 of 6 new differentiation features shipped, 3 real bugs caught live and fixed, "Pipeline" renamed to "Missions" with a List/Board toggle.** Same session as the entry directly below (spans 2026-08-11→12) — this entry covers everything after the Kanban/rejection-intelligence work. Two agy research passes this session (13 candidate differentiation ideas total, cross-checked against the existing roadmap and against real buildability — 3 of the 6 "new" ideas needed honest rescoping, detailed in the Master Feature Inventory in `build-plan.md`) plus a third agy pass specifically on per-stage navigation UX and a fourth on the "Missions" rename.
  - **Career Record (`/career`, spine #2 — portable career identity MVP).** New `accomplishments` table (RLS 4-policy own-row), `actions/accomplishments.ts` CRUD, `CareerTimeline.tsx` merging three sources into one chronological dotted-list: `profiles.work_experience`/`education`, manually-logged accomplishments, and real job outcomes from the tracker (`jobs.application_status != 'draft'`) — deliberately scoped to just the data-model + page rather than all ~11 unscoped Career-OS sub-features in that inventory section. Full JSON data export (`/api/career/export`). Live-verified: signed into the real test account, added/edited/deleted an accomplishment, confirmed every write against the DB directly.
  - **Feature 1 — Bait-and-Switch Risk Scorer, rescoped.** agy's original pitch ("posting rewritten multiple times") needed posting-history tracking that conflicts with this app's deliberate no-re-scrape design philosophy (`lib/jobStatus.ts`). Shipped instead: a `titleScopeMismatch` field riding on the *existing* evaluator call (zero marginal AI cost) — `lib/evaluator.ts`'s schema/prompt extended, new `jobs.title_scope_mismatch` column, rendered as an amber callout in `MatchScore.tsx` next to the existing Legitimacy-dimension warning (distinct signal: Legitimacy asks "is this real," this asks "does the level match the actual work").
  - **Feature 2 — Reappearing Requisition Signal, descoped from "Churn Seat Tracker."** Real turnover data has no free/legal source (researched via agy: SEC filings only cover named executives, Glassdoor/Indeed scraping violates ToS, WARN Act only covers mass layoffs) — the one legitimate free signal is whether the same (company, title) fingerprint reappears across a user's own accumulated search history over time. New `lib/churnSignal.ts`, zero AI cost, zero new dependency. Surfaced as a badge next to the existing stale-listing signal in `JobResultCard`/`JobActionBar`/job-details page. Live-verified with 3 synthetic rows spanning a real 50-day range — correctly flagged "Reappeared in 3 of your searches over 7 weeks."
  - **Feature 3 — Strategic Moat Briefing.** New `researchStrategicMoat()` in `agent/research.ts` — recent-news/strategic-priorities lens distinct from the existing culture/tech-stack company dossier. Tries a free Jina-Reader fetch of a Google search-results page first, falls to the existing Perplexity path (real ~$0.005/call, same fallback already used elsewhere in this file) only when that's too thin. New `jobs.strategic_moat`/`strategic_moat_researched_at` columns, `strategic_moat` usage key (3/day). Live-verified against a real company (Microsoft) — grounded, accurate output.
  - **Feature 4 — Interview Panel Topology.** New `interview_panel_members` child table (a job can have multiple panelists; RLS 4-policy own-row, `ON DELETE CASCADE` from both `jobs` and `auth.users`). `researchInterviewerBackground(name, company)` in `agent/research.ts` — same free-search-then-Perplexity shape as Feature 3, adapted for a named individual; extraction deliberately conservative (genuinely public professional facts only, never personality/bias speculation). New `InterviewPanel.tsx` card, gated to show only once `application_status === "interviewing"`. **Live-verified against a real public figure** (Satya Nadella at Microsoft) — accurate career facts, a grounded interview-prep note tied to his actual background, real cited sources, confirmed cascade-delete on job removal.
  - **Real bug, live-caught while testing Feature 3: hydration mismatch.** `formatTimeAgo(foundAt)` computed inline in JSX renders a different string at SSR-time vs. client-hydration-time whenever real wall-clock time crosses a bucket boundary between those two moments — confirmed the trigger was this page's own 24s "Get strategic briefing" round trip, easily enough for "just now" to become "1 min ago." Fixed in all 3 call sites that had it (`JobActionBar.tsx`, `KanbanCard.tsx` — introduced earlier this same session, `FindJobsForm.tsx` — pre-existing) by deferring to client-only via `useEffect`, using the project's own established `setTimeout(...,0)` fix for the `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint rule.
  - **Real bug, user-caught: job details page had no way to advance past "Applied."** `JobActionBar.tsx` only ever exposed a one-way "Mark as applied" button even though `setApplicationStatus` (built earlier this session) already supported all 5 stages. Replaced with a proper status dropdown. **First version had its own real bug**, also live-caught: a plain absolute-positioned child menu painted *underneath* the `JobInfo` card below it instead of over it (this page always has more content below the action bar, unlike list-card dropdowns that sit at the bottom of their list). Fixed with the exact pattern already proven in this codebase for the same bug class (`StyleTab.tsx`'s Theme/Page-size dropdowns): portal to `document.body`, `position:fixed` computed from the trigger's real `getBoundingClientRect()`, close on scroll. Live-verified end to end: menu renders above all page content, selecting "Interviewing" persists to the DB and the pill updates.

New `MissionsView.tsx` wraps the existing `KanbanBoardLoader` plus a new flat filtered list (reusing `JobResultCard`, same as Liked/External/Recommended already do).

Needs a real restart of the dev server process (not just a `.next` clear, per this gotcha's own established resolution) before the Missions rename and List/Board toggle can be click-tested live again. Code is otherwise complete and was working correctly before this cache issue appeared (the status-dropdown portal fix immediately above this was fully live-verified before it started).

- **2026-08-11 (Phase 9): Application Tracker (Kanban) + Rejection Intelligence shipped — the "spine #1" feature from every strategy doc, plus the Resend→Brevo SMTP migration and a real signin bug fix that preceded it.** Full session also included JobRight-differentiation feature research (`agy` + WebSearch) folded into `build-plan.md`'s Master Feature Inventory — not detailed here, see that file directly.
  - **Email/password auth finally closed out.** Resend's sandbox tier only delivers to the account owner's own email, permanently blocking real signup testing (open since 2026-07-29). Switched SMTP provider to Brevo (`smtp-relay.brevo.com`, real delivery to arbitrary addresses, single-sender verification only) via InsForge's `PUT /api/auth/smtp-config` admin endpoint — config updated live, verified by reading it back, not just trusting the write response. **Real bug found and fixed in the same pass**: `app/api/auth/signin/route.ts` reported `requireVerification: true` on an unverified login attempt without ever triggering a new code send — the UI's "we sent you a code" message was false whenever the original signup-time send had failed or expired. Fixed by calling `resendVerificationEmail` inside the signin route's unverified branch. Confirmed live: a fresh signup delivered a real code via Brevo; a follow-up sign-in attempt correctly re-triggered a send and got legitimately rate-limited by InsForge's own `minIntervalSeconds: 60` (not a bug — the original code was still valid). Brevo's own dashboard flagged that sending from a `@gmail.com` address isn't DMARC-aligned — real domain verification is the correct long-term fix, still open.
  - **Schema**: `jobs.application_status_updated_at`/`rejection_diagnosis`/`rejection_diagnosed_at` + a CHECK constraint on `application_status` that had been missing since the column was created (migration `20260811030000_add-jobs-application-pipeline.sql`, applied live then written to match).
  - **`setApplicationStatus`** (`actions/jobs.ts`) replaces the old one-way `markApplied` — reversible, follows `toggleSaveJob`'s shape, fires the `application_status_changed` PostHog event spec'd back in the original Phase 15 plan but never implemented since the board never existed. `JobActionBar`/`JobResultCard` refactored onto it directly, `markApplied` deleted (2 call sites).
  - **`lib/applicationStatus.ts`** consolidates what had drifted into three independent local copies of the same 5-value union/color-map (`types/index.ts`, `app/resume/page.tsx`, `ApplicationDocumentsCard.tsx`) into one shared source.
  - **`lib/rejectionIntelligence.ts`** — AI diagnosis of why an employer likely went silent, grounded entirely in a job's own already-stored 10-dimension evaluation (no new data collection). Same zod-schema-plus-fallback idiom as `lib/evaluator.ts`. Deliberately honest about its own limits: system prompt forbids generic filler ("market conditions" unless no dimension grades C or below), every reason must cite a specific stored grade/note, output is framed as "possible explanations" never a verdict. New `rejection_intelligence` usage key (5/day cap). **Live-verified with real synthetic test data** (not just read for correctness): inserted a fake rejected job with a deliberately weak evaluation (D on skills/tech match, C on seniority), triggered the diagnosis, and confirmed the model's output correctly cited those exact grades rather than inventing generic reasons — persisted to `jobs.rejection_diagnosis`, usage_daily incremented correctly.
  - **New `/pipeline` page, replaces `/jobs/applied`** — `KanbanBoard.tsx`, the first genuinely cross-column `@dnd-kit` board in this codebase (every prior use, `EditorTab.tsx`'s `SortableList`, is single-list `arrayMove` reorder only; multi-container drag needed its own `DndContext`+`useDroppable`-per-column build from scratch). `/jobs/applied` deleted, "Pipeline" added as a top-level nav item (not folded into the Jobs dropdown like the page it replaces — meant to be a daily-return surface, matching the spine rationale).
  - **Two real bugs caught live, not just in code review:**
    - A genuine SSR hydration mismatch (`@dnd-kit`'s auto-generated `aria-describedby` instance-counter id differed between the server render and the client's first render — confirmed via a genuinely fresh, un-hydrated browser tab, ruling out stale dev-server cache first). An explicit `id` prop on `DndContext` did **not** fix it. Fixed the same way `SettingsModalLoader.tsx` already fixed an analogous SSR-vs-client-only problem: a small `KanbanBoardLoader.tsx` wrapper using `next/dynamic(..., {ssr:false})` so the board never participates in SSR at all.
    - `useSortable`'s `attributes` sets `role="button"` on whatever it's spread onto — spreading it on the whole card (which contains a real title `Link`, a "Why the silence?" `<button>`, and a "View job" `Link`) produced literally invalid HTML, a `<button>` nested inside a `<button>`, and the nested button's clicks silently never fired. This is the exact failure mode `EditorTab.tsx`'s own in-code comment already warns about ("avoiding accidental parent-drag") — missed on the first pass despite reading that file during planning. Fixed by moving `{...attributes}{...listeners}` onto a small dedicated `GripVertical` drag-handle button, matching that established pattern exactly; the rest of the card is now listener-free and its interactive elements work normally.
  - **Verification**: `tsc`/`eslint` clean throughout. Live-verified via two real inserted test job rows (one draft, one pre-rejected with fake evaluation data) against the real test account — confirmed "Mark as applied" (job-details page) correctly writes `application_status`/`application_status_updated_at` and the Pipeline board re-groups correctly on next load; confirmed the rejection-intelligence round trip end to end. **The drag gesture itself was not confirmed via browser automation** — three careful attempts with `left_click_drag` (hover-verified coordinates, generous drop targets) never registered with `@dnd-kit`'s pointer sensor, a known-hard category for synthetic-pointer-event automation generally, not traced to an app bug (the exact same `setApplicationStatus` call the drag handler uses was proven correct via the click-based path instead). The two test jobs were left in the test account specifically so a human can try the actual drag gesture next.

- **2026-08-07 (Phase 8, immediate follow-up): StyleTab's Theme/Page size went through 2 real live bugs — native `<select>` unreadable, then the fix's own popup invisible.** User clicked through the new template/theme system live and caught both. Bug 1: native `<select>` dropdown popups are OS/browser chrome — Chrome renders every non-highlighted `<option>` in a washed-out grey regardless of this app's dark theme, reading as disabled even though it's just default browser UI, and can't be fixed via CSS on the `<select>` itself. Fixed by replacing both selects with a custom-rendered `Dropdown` component, full control over contrast. Bug 2, immediately caught in the same session: that first version rendered nothing at all when opened — it lives inside `Group`'s `overflow-hidden` accordion wrapper (needed for that component's own rounded corners), which silently clips any child that visually extends past it, including an absolutely-positioned popup. Fixed by portaling the panel to `document.body` with `position:fixed` computed from the trigger's real `getBoundingClientRect()` — the exact pattern (mousedown-before-click ordering included) already proven in `ResumeManager.tsx`'s own row-action menu, which has this exact failure mode documented in its own comment. `tsc`/`eslint` clean, `/resume/tailored/[jobId]` confirmed live.

- **2026-08-07 (Phase 8, immediate follow-up): `DocumentGenerator.tsx`'s Edit/View went through 2 more rounds live, ending on real outline buttons — researched via `agy`.** Round 1 (title-as-link) turned out undiscoverable — only colored accent on hover, read as plain text at rest, user couldn't find the way into the editor at all. Round 2 (revert to a plain heading + explicit "Edit"/"View" text links) fixed discoverability but read as unfinished next to the solid "Regenerate" button. Round 3, researched: real SaaS products use a primary (solid) + secondary (outline) button hierarchy for exactly this "1 primary + N secondary actions" shape — plain text links next to a filled button under-weight visually. `Edit` (`SquarePen` icon) and `View` (`Eye` icon) are now `border border-border` outline buttons matching this app's own existing outline-button recipe (the same classes `ResumeWorkspace`'s "Regenerate" button already uses), sitting left-aligned after the primary Generate/Regenerate button. Since `DocumentAction` is one shared component instantiated for both `kind="resume"` and `kind="cover_letter"`, this — and the already-existing `DocumentChatEditor` "Refine with AI" box, rendered identically whenever `hasDocument` is true — applies to both cards with zero cover-letter-specific code; confirmed live, not just by design (the earlier screenshot's cover-letter card only showed "Generate" because no cover letter had been generated for that job yet, not a real gap).

- **2026-08-07 (Phase 8, immediate follow-up): two things fixed after seeing the listing-status feature live — a real `posted_at` bug, and "make it professional" polish on ApplicationDocumentsCard.**
  - **Real bug, user-caught from a live screenshot**: `JobActionBar.tsx`'s "Posted 2 days ago" badge never changes — `job.posted_at` is Google Jobs' own relative text, captured once at scrape time and never refreshed, not a live date. A job found weeks ago can permanently show "Posted 2 days ago." This also silently broke `lib/jobStatus.ts`'s `isStaleByAge`: `new Date("2 days ago")` is an Invalid Date, so the staleness check quietly no-op'd for any job with `posted_at` set (the common case) instead of falling through to the reliable `found_at`. Fixed both: `isStaleByAge` now only ever reads `found_at`; `JobActionBar`'s badge now shows `Found {formatTimeAgo(foundAt)}` (a real, ever-accurate relative time) instead of echoing the frozen SerpApi string. `postedAt` prop removed from `JobActionBar`/`DocumentGenerator` — no longer used anywhere.
  - **"Make it professional"**: `window.confirm`/`window.alert` (the raw "localhost:3001 says…" browser dialog) replaced with a new reusable `components/ui/ConfirmDialog.tsx` — same glass-modal chrome as `SectionModal.tsx` (scrim, `glass-panel-strong` card, tw-animate-css entrance), a warning icon, and a real destructive-red confirm button. Applied to `ApplicationDocumentsCard.tsx` (the component the user was looking at) and, for consistency, the two other places in the app that still used the browser dialog — `ResumeManager.tsx` and `ResumeAnalysisView.tsx`'s résumé-slot delete. Also fixed the underlying complaint that the Résumé/Cover letter labels in `ApplicationDocumentsCard.tsx` didn't read as clickable — they were structurally real `Link`s already, just styled as plain text; now `text-agent` colored with hover-underline and a hover-revealed `ChevronRight`, unmistakably a navigation link.
  - `tsc`/`eslint` clean, `/resume` and `/resume/[id]` confirmed live (307s).

- **2026-08-07 (Phase 8, new feature): stale/unavailable job-posting detection shipped — researched via `agy`, then refined with a real free signal found by reading this app's own scraper code.** User asked how to handle postings that go stale, then specifically "what if it's not even in the list on a re-search anymore." Research (agy): don't try to re-scrape/HEAD-check external listings — Workday/Greenhouse-class ATSes return soft-404s and sit behind anti-bot walls, and real job *trackers* (Teal/Huntr/Simplify) don't do this either, they lean on user action + cheap heuristics. But `lib/actions/scraper.actions.ts` already had everything needed for a genuinely free, precise third signal: every search creates an `agent_runs` row and stamps its id onto every `jobs` row it returns (`run_id`, upserted on `user_id,external_id`); a job from an earlier EXACT repeat of the same (title, location) search that isn't in the newest run's results was actively dropped by Google Jobs — no extra scraping, just comparing what SerpApi already returned across two runs.
  - Two new nullable `jobs` columns (migration `20260807010000_add-jobs-listing-status.sql`, applied live then written to match): `marked_unavailable_at` (user-confirmed, highest confidence) and `dropped_from_search_at` (the free signal above). Both null means "no signal," never "confirmed active" — this app has no way to positively confirm a listing is live.
  - New `lib/jobStatus.ts`: `isStaleByAge` (posted_at/found_at > 45 days, lowest-confidence fallback for jobs never re-searched) + `getListingSignal` resolving all three into one tiered badge (confirmed > likely > possible).
  - `scraper.actions.ts`: after a search's upsert, queries the user's prior `agent_runs` with an EXACT string match on `job_title_searched`/`location_searched` (not fuzzy `ilike` — this file's own existing comment already flags "Software Engineer" vs "Software Developer" as a real mismatch risk elsewhere in this exact file), finds jobs tied to those older runs missing from the new result set, and stamps `dropped_from_search_at`. The upsert payload also now clears `dropped_from_search_at: null` for every job in the new results, so a listing reappearing un-flags it automatically. Never overrides a user's own `marked_unavailable_at`.
  - New `markJobUnavailable`/`unmarkJobUnavailable` actions (`actions/jobs.ts`), mirroring `toggleSaveJob`'s exact shape — reversible, not a one-way flag.
  - **UI, deliberately status-aware per the research**: `JobResultCard.tsx` (shared by Find Jobs/Saved Jobs) gained a signal badge and a "No Longer Available" item in the existing "..." menu (alongside Already Applied/Not Interested/Report Issue). `JobActionBar.tsx` + `find-jobs/[id]/page.tsx` gained the same badge alongside Posted/Remote/Hidden, plus a "Mark unavailable"/"Available again?" toggle pill alongside Save/Hide/Mark-applied. `DocumentGenerator.tsx` gained a dismissible warning banner above the Generate buttons — but **only** when `application_status === "draft"`; once applied (or further), nothing changes, matching the research's strongest point: never disrupt or block access to a job's real documents just because the source posting might be gone, since those documents may be the only surviving record of what was actually applied to.
  - `Job` type gained `marked_unavailable_at`/`dropped_from_search_at` — all existing job queries already `.select("*")`, so no query changes needed beyond the type.
  - `tsc`/`eslint` clean, all affected routes confirmed live (307s, not 500s) via the port-3001 dev server.

- **2026-08-06 (Phase 8, immediate follow-up): `DocumentGenerator.tsx`'s (job-details page) document titles now link directly to their editors.** User's own call after seeing the card live — the small "Edit" text link tucked among Regenerate/View was less discoverable than just making the title itself the primary way in. "Tailored Resume"/"Cover Letter" (icon + heading) is now a `Link` to `workspaceHref` once the document exists; the separate "Edit" text link is gone (redundant now). Before a document exists, the title stays a plain non-interactive div (nothing to edit yet). `tsc`/`eslint` clean, route confirmed live.

- **2026-08-06 (Phase 8, immediate follow-up): cover-letter pill on `/resume` gained download/delete parity with the résumé pill, plus a "generate on arrival" deep link for the ghost slot.** New `deleteTailoredCoverLetter` (`actions/documents.ts`) mirrors `deleteTailoredResume`'s exact shared-row handling (a résumé and cover letter for the same job can live on one `applications` row — only the cover-letter-specific columns/storage file are cleared, the row itself only deleted once neither document references it; `resume_style` deliberately left untouched since a surviving résumé still needs it). `ApplicationDocumentsCard.tsx`'s cover-letter pill now has the same download/delete icon pair as the résumé pill (separate `deletingResume`/`deletingCoverLetter` loading state, was one shared `pending` boolean). The "+ Add cover letter" ghost slot now links to `/find-jobs/${jobId}?generate=cover_letter` instead of a bare job link — `DocumentGenerator.tsx`'s `DocumentAction` reads that query param on mount and fires generation automatically (scrolling itself into view first) rather than landing the user on the page and making them find the button, then strips the param via `router.replace` so a refresh/back-nav doesn't re-trigger it. `tsc`/`eslint` clean, both routes confirmed live (307s).

- **2026-08-06 (Phase 8, latest): the `/resume` page's per-job list redesigned from résumé-only rows into per-application "document tray" cards.** User asked to show both the résumé and cover letter per job here — researched via `agy` first: a job seeker's real mental model is "my materials for Job X," not a flat pile of PDFs, and real trackers (Teal, Huntr) treat the job as the root entity with documents as attached assets. Renamed `TailoredResumeCard.tsx` → **`ApplicationDocumentsCard.tsx`** (old file deleted, fully unreferenced after the swap) and rebuilt: the card header now links to the job itself (`/find-jobs/[jobId]`, previously it linked straight into the résumé editor) and shows a real `CompanyLogo` (now that `jobs.company_logo_url` is actually selected here — it wasn't before) plus an `application_status` badge (Draft/Applied/Interviewing/Offer/Rejected — explicitly requested, not dropped as a "nice to have"; `jobs.application_status` already existed but was never surfaced on this page). Below a divider, a horizontal "document tray": a résumé pill (existing download/delete actions, now siblings of the label `Link` rather than nested inside one — nesting an `<a>` inside another `<Link>`'s `<a>` is invalid HTML and breaks click targeting) and a cover-letter pill that's either solid (links to `/cover-letter/tailored/[jobId]`) or a dashed "+ Add cover letter" ghost slot (links to the job page) when none exists yet for that job — a missing document reads as an invitation, not a broken state. `app/resume/page.tsx`'s query extended to select `cover_letter_pdf_url` plus `jobs(company_logo_url, application_status)` alongside the existing `title`/`company`. `tsc`/`eslint` clean, `/resume` confirmed live via the port-3001 dev server (clean 307, not a 500).

- **2026-08-06 (Phase 8, later same day): Cover Letter Editor Workspace shipped — a full second workspace mirroring the résumé one — plus 3 new résumé templates, 3 new themes, and cover letter/résumé style unification.** User asked for more résumé templates/themes, then for the same editing experience on cover letters, then specifically for a real workspace UI (not just passive style sharing) — each researched via `agy` before building, in order:
  - **3 new résumé templates** (`ResumeTemplate` grew from 3 to 6 — `structured`/`centered`/`split` plus new `timeline`/`executive`/`block`), all implemented in `ResumePDF.tsx` within the existing ATS constraints (single-column-per-region, no tables/images, standard-14 fonts only — never relaxed): **timeline** gives each work-experience entry its own 20% date column instead of a title/dates header row; **executive** mirrors `split` but with the sidebar on the RIGHT (65/35, not 32/68) and a full-width header rendered above the two-column row instead of inside the sidebar; **block** renders a solid-`accentDark` header banner (hardcoded white text — it sits on the accent color regardless of theme) with centered, thicker-ruled section titles. `renderSection`'s signature changed from taking a bare `bulletMark` string to the whole `ResumeStyle` object, so it can branch on `style.template` (needed for timeline's alternate work-experience layout).
  - **3 new themes** (`ResumeTheme` grew from 3 to 6 — `modern`/`classic`/`minimal` plus new `slate`/`editorial`/`sage`), cheap additions (just new `ThemeTokens` entries) vs. the templates: `slate` (steel-blue/dark-slate, airy name letter-spacing), `editorial` (deep-crimson serif on pure black, heavy rules), `sage` (sea-green/forest, no header rule). Added to `StyleTab.tsx`'s labels and the older global `ThemeSelector.tsx` (`profiles.preferred_resume_theme`) for consistency.
  - **Cover letter now shares the tailored résumé's exact `ResumeStyle`** (same template+theme per job) instead of a bare `theme` string read from the global profile preference — researched via `agy`: real products (Novoresume/Enhancv/Zety) treat résumé+cover-letter as one visually matched suite, and mismatched styling reads as unprofessional. `resolveTokens`/`mapRange`/`SPACING_RANGES` exported from `ResumePDF.tsx` for reuse. `CoverLetterPDF.tsx` rewritten to accept `style: ResumeStyle` (was `theme?: ResumeTheme`) with a 3-way structural branch: `split` gets a real left sidebar (contact + up to 8 base-profile skills — not the tailored résumé's specific edited set, since a cover letter isn't tied to one résumé snapshot); `centered`/`block` center the header; everything else keeps the original left-aligned block-letter layout. Also gained a `salutation` prop — the recipient line was hardcoded `"Hiring Team, {company}"` with zero way to address a specific hiring manager; now overridable, `null` falls back to the computed default (new `applications.cover_letter_salutation` column, migration `20260806230000_add-cover-letter-salutation.sql`, applied live then written to match). `app/api/documents/generate/route.ts` and `app/api/documents/chat/route.ts` updated to fetch/pass `resume_style`+`cover_letter_salutation` instead of the old bare theme.
  - **`CoverLetterWorkspace.tsx` — the real editor, not just shared styling under the hood.** Same 2-column shell as `ResumeWorkspace` (live `PDFViewer` left, tabs right) but sized to a cover letter's much simpler content (no sections array to reorder/hide): **Editor tab** — an editable Salutation input (placeholder shows the computed default) + an auto-expanding Body textarea bound directly to the existing `\n\n`-paragraph-split string (no new content data model needed), plus `RefinementChips` (cover-letter-specific tone/length/opening/closing presets, researched via `agy` — distinct from the résumé's bullet-wording presets) and `DocumentChatEditor` underneath for free-form AI revision. **Style tab** — reuses `StyleTab.tsx` directly against the same shared style, with a new `documentType` prop hiding the two résumé-only controls (Skills columns, Bullet style) that don't apply to a letter. New route `app/cover-letter/tailored/[jobId]/page.tsx` mirrors the résumé page's exact data-fetching/redirect pattern (redirects to `/find-jobs/${jobId}` if no cover letter has been generated yet for this job). New `saveCoverLetterContent` action mirrors `saveResumeSections`' shape — plain DB update, no PDF re-render (same standing behavior as the résumé side: the live preview renders fresh from local state, but the *downloadable* PDF file only refreshes on regenerate/AI-chat-revise). `RefinementChips`/`DocumentChatEditor` both already took a generic `kind` prop; `RefinementChips` just had it hardcoded to `"resume"` internally — now a real prop. `useDocumentChat`'s `RevisedData` type gained `letterBody?: string` (and is now exported/shared, not redeclared per-file) so a chat/chip revision can push the new letter text into the cover-letter workspace's own local state — `router.refresh()` alone can't, same reasoning `sections` already existed for on the résumé side.
  - **Navigation, researched via `agy`**: deliberately did NOT add a top-level "Cover Letter" nav item — unlike `/resume` (a real standalone multi-résumé manager), a cover letter only ever exists in relation to one specific job, so there's no natural non-job-scoped page for such a link to land on, and real job-tracker products (Teal, Huntr) keep this scoped to the job/application context rather than main nav. Instead: new `DocumentSwitcher.tsx` — a small "Résumé / Cover Letter" pill toggle added to both workspace pages' headers, cross-linking the two per-job documents (if the target hasn't been generated yet, its own page's existing redirect-to-job-details guard handles that, no extra logic needed here). `DocumentGenerator.tsx` (the job-details page's compact generate/view card) gained an "Edit" link on both the résumé and cover-letter cards, pointing at their respective workspace routes — previously only a raw-PDF "View" link existed there for either document.
  - **Also this session, user-requested nav cleanup**: "Profile" removed from the top-level nav array and moved to the existing `UserCircle` icon in the right-hand cluster (now a real `Link` to `/profile`, was decorative) — one fewer competing item in an already-full horizontal bar. Re-added as a plain link inside the mobile menu (which rendered from the same now-shortened array and would otherwise have lost Profile access entirely on mobile).
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean throughout (checked incrementally after each piece). **Not click-tested against a live authenticated session** — same standing limitation as everything else in this project; both workspace routes and a Navbar-rendering page (`/dashboard`) were confirmed live via the existing port-3001 dev server to compile and correctly 307-redirect, but the actual template/theme rendering, the cover-letter editor's auto-expanding textarea, the Résumé/Cover Letter switcher, and the moved profile icon all still need a human pass.

- **2026-08-06 (Phase 8, immediate follow-up): Certifications promoted to a real first-class section, not a generic custom one.** User pushed back on the previous entry's "Certifications" custom-section preset — they specifically wanted Certifications to get the same full add/edit/remove treatment as Education, not be lumped into the generic catalog. New `CertificationEntry` type (`{name, issuer, date}`) and a 6th `ResumeSection` variant, `"certifications"`. `lib/resumeSections.ts`'s `buildDefaultSections` now seeds it from `profile.certifications` (a flat `string[]` — issuer/date aren't captured anywhere upstream, so entries start with just a name, same spirit as education entries seeded from an upload before the user refines them); `visible` follows the same has-any-data convention as skills/education. Renders in `ResumePDF.tsx` with the same simple degree/details layout as Education, and joins `skills`/`education` in the `split` template's sidebar. `EditorTab.tsx` gained `CertificationEntryEditor` (Name/Issuer/Date fields, drag-reorder/duplicate/remove via the same `SortableList` helper) and full section delete (not just hide, same as custom sections — not everyone has certifications, unlike the 4 original always-present types). Removed "Certifications" from the generic custom-section preset catalog to avoid two parallel paths to the same concept; the "Add section" catalog instead offers a dedicated "Certifications" option (filtered out once one exists) specifically so tailored résumés generated *before* this feature existed can still get one added. `tsc`/`eslint` clean, live-checked against the same port-3001 dev server (clean `307 → /login`).

- **2026-08-06 (Phase 8, later same day): Editor tab gained real editing control — custom sections, entry add/reorder/duplicate, editable work-experience metadata.** Follow-up to the visual redesign below, same session — user asked to "give more editing features and control to the user." Researched via `agy` again, grounded in the actual gaps found by re-reading the just-shipped `EditorTab.tsx` (work-experience title/company/dates were a read-only label; no way to add or remove education/work-experience entries at all, only edit in place; no reordering within a section, only whole sections; only 4 fixed section types with no escape hatch). Report: real builders (Enhancv, Novoresume, Rezi, Kickresume, Teal) treat add/edit/remove entries as table-stakes, entry+bullet reordering as high-value, and custom sections as a small preset catalog (Projects/Certifications/Languages/Awards/Volunteer Experience) layered on **one generic entry shape** plus a blank "Custom section" escape hatch — not a bespoke data model per preset. Also flagged real nested-drag-and-drop pitfalls (accidental parent-section drags, cross-level drops, handle ambiguity) to design around.
  - **`types/resumeEditor.ts`**: new `CustomEntry` type (`{title, subtitle, date, bullets}`) and a 5th `ResumeSection` variant, `type:"custom"` (own required `title`), plus an optional `label?: string` override on all 4 original fixed-type sections (so "Work Experience" can be renamed to "Professional Experience" etc). New `sectionDisplayLabel(section, defaultLabel)` helper resolves which one applies. Purely additive — confirmed via grep that `lib/scoreJump.ts`/`lib/resumeQuality.ts` only ever `.find()` for the 4 original types and silently ignore anything else, so custom sections don't participate in fit-scoring or the quality grade yet (never AI-authored, by design — same as skills/education). `saveResumeSections` writes to a plain `jsonb` column with no schema validation, so the new shape needed no migration.
  - **`components/documents/ResumePDF.tsx`**: renders `custom` sections with the same jobEntry-style layout as Work Experience (title/date header, subtitle, bullets) — one generic renderer covers the large majority of real custom-section content rather than a bespoke layout per preset. All 4 original `sectionTitle` texts now resolve through `sectionDisplayLabel` instead of a hardcoded string. Custom sections default to the main column (not the sidebar) in the `split` template.
  - **`components/documents/EditorTab.tsx`**: the read-only `"{title} at {company}"` label in `WorkEntryEditor` became 4 real editable fields (title/company/start date/end date-or-"Present"+current-checkbox). New generic local `SortableList`/`SortableListItem` helper (its own `DndContext` per section instance, `EntryDragHandle` — a distinctly smaller grip than the section-level one) powers entry reordering for Work Experience, Education, and Custom sections alike — a fully separate drag scope from the outer section-level `DndContext`, never sharing droppable ids, precisely to avoid the nested-DND pitfalls the research flagged. Bullet-level reordering deliberately got simple ▲/▼ `MoveButtons` instead of a *third* nested `DndContext` — bullets already carry a lot of per-row ephemeral state (AI diff cards, instruction inputs, keyboard-shortcut refs), and a third drag layer there was judged not worth the added risk for this pass. "Add work experience"/"Add education"/"Add entry" buttons append a blank entry (education entries could not be added *or removed* at all before this — `EducationEntryEditor` was never given an `onRemove` in the first place). Duplicate-entry (`Copy` icon) added to all 3 entry types. Section headers gained inline-rename (pencil icon → text input, Enter/blur commits, Escape cancels) and a new "+ Add section" control (click-outside-closes dropdown, same pattern as `/preview/resume`'s `ActionsMenu`) offering the preset catalog plus a blank "Custom section" — inserted section auto-opens with one blank entry pre-seeded so the user isn't looking at an empty shell.
  - Deliberately **not** built this pass: AI rewrite/diff for custom-section bullets (the `rewriteResumeBullet` prompt is tuned for achievement-focused work-experience bullets specifically, doesn't fit e.g. a Languages entry) and application-level undo/redo (agy's research rated it medium-value/high-complexity — native per-field undo already covers most accidental edits).
  - `tsc --noEmit` clean across the whole project (not just the touched files — this change touched a shared type, worth the full check), `eslint` clean on all touched files. **Not click-tested against a live authenticated session** — verified the same way as the visual redesign above: hit the existing port-3001 dev server directly, got a clean `307 → /login` (not a `500`), and its log shows only successful compiles at current timestamps.

- **2026-08-06 (Phase 8): Style tab + Editor tab redesigned for a more professional, interactive feel — researched via `agy` first, then built.** User asked specifically to make the résumé workspace's Style and Editor tabs "better and professional and interactive." Research prompt to `agy` was grounded in the actual current component code (not generic advice) and named real competitors (Rezi, Novoresume, Kickresume, Enhancv, Teal, Zety, JobRight) plus general editor-design references (Figma's inspector, Canva's property panel, Notion's block editor). Full report saved by agy at `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/brain/3c03c3ac-d779-43f9-85c5-ba3c9cd43a77/resume_editor_ui_ux_report.md`. Built all of it in one pass, not just the "quick wins":
  - **`components/documents/ResumePDF.tsx`**: exported `mapRange`/`SPACING_RANGES` (previously private to `createStyles`) so `StyleTab` can show the exact same real units (pt / line-height ×) next to each spacing slider that actually land on the rendered page — before this the 4 spacing sliders were raw 0-100 with no visible value at all.
  - **`components/documents/StyleTab.tsx`** rewritten: controls now grouped into 3 collapsible sections (Template & theme — open by default; Typography & colors; Layout & spacing — both collapsed) via a small local `Group` accordion, deliberately not a new `components/ui/` primitive or the installed-but-unused `@base-ui/react` collapsible — reading `app/globals.css` confirmed the scaffolded `components/ui/*` shadcn set (`Button`, `Select`, etc.) resolves through a *separate*, disconnected grayscale/oklch palette (`--primary`/`--secondary`/`--destructive`), not this app's real `--color-accent`/`--color-agent` tokens, so using it here would have quietly introduced off-brand colors. Template thumbnails made structurally accurate to each real template (header alignment, split's actual ~32/68 sidebar ratio) instead of 3 interchangeable bar-chart mockups — stops short of rendering the actual PDF to an image (a genuinely bigger lift, no cheap react-pdf thumbnail path) and that gap is intentionally left open, not silently skipped. Header alignment and skills-columns controls are now icon buttons (`AlignLeft/Center/Right`, glyph column-count previews) instead of text. Font sizes consolidated into a Small/Medium/Large `Text size` control (matched against the 4 raw values live — falls back to "no option highlighted" for a hand-edited custom combination) with the original 4 raw inputs demoted behind an "Advanced" toggle. Accent color is now 8 curated recruiter-safe swatches (independent hex, same category as `RESUME_THEMES`' own hardcoded hex — résumé *content* color, not app UI chrome, so not subject to the no-hardcoded-hex rule) plus a native-color-input "Custom" swatch tile for power users, replacing the bare OS color picker as the primary control.
  - **`components/documents/EditorTab.tsx`** rewritten: bullet textareas are borderless at rest and only show a border/background on hover or focus (Notion-style canvas feel) instead of a permanently-bordered form field; delete/AI-action buttons stayed always-visible rather than hover-only, a deliberate deviation from the research pass's literal suggestion — hiding functional controls behind hover-only harms discoverability on touch devices, where there is no real hover. `Enter` at the exact end of a bullet now spawns a new bullet below it and focuses it (Shift+Enter or Enter mid-text still inserts a literal newline); `Backspace` on an empty bullet deletes it and refocuses the previous one's end — both use the file's existing deferred-setState-in-effect pattern for the post-render focus. Empty bullets show placeholder guidance text (`"Achieved [metric] by doing [action], resulting in [outcome]"`), same for the summary textarea. Each bullet shows a live character-count hint, ambering below 40 or above 150 chars as a rough one-line-fit proxy (not a hard ATS rule). **Biggest behavior change**: "Edit with AI"/"Regenerate" no longer overwrite a bullet directly — the result becomes a `pending` state rendered as an inline `BulletDiffCard` (Was/Now, agent-teal for the AI-suggested side per this app's own AI-content color rule) with explicit Accept / Try again / Discard actions, so an AI rewrite can never silently replace wording the user didn't approve.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` both clean. **Not click-tested against a live authenticated session** — same standing no-OAuth-here limitation as everything else in this project. Verified as deep as this environment allows: a genuinely fresh second `next dev` instance couldn't be started (Next.js's own single-instance lock refused to bind alongside the user's pre-existing long-running server on port 3001), so verification instead hit the *existing* port-3001 process directly — `curl -i http://localhost:3001/resume/tailored/test-id-check` returned a clean `307` to `/login` (not a `500`), and that process's own `.next/dev/logs/next-development.log` had zero errors referencing `StyleTab`/`EditorTab`/`ResumePDF` at the current session's timestamps (only stale, unrelated `RefinementChips`/`improvementPrompts` errors from hours earlier). Confirms the full import chain compiles; does not confirm drag/hover/keyboard/diff-accept interactions actually behave correctly on screen — still needs a human pass, same as the rest of the workspace.

- **2026-08-05 (Phase 7, later session): Résumé Editor Workspace shipped — the full `context/build-plan.md` §C1 feature** ("click Generate → get a PDF blind" replaced with a real live-preview editing surface), including all 3 mockup templates, not a cut-down version. Large build, full plan at `~/.claude/plans/jaunty-jingling-bear.md`. In order:
  - **Data model** (per build-plan.md's own explicit warning about base-vs-tailored corruption): new `applications.resume_sections`/`resume_style`/`updated_at` columns (`migrations/20260805211857_add-resume-editor-workspace.sql`, applied live then written to match, per this project's established pattern). A tailored résumé is now a real independent 4-section snapshot (summary/skills/work_experience/education) the moment it's generated — skills/education were **never** stored per tailored copy before this, only ever pulled live from `profiles` at render time. Editing a tailored copy now only ever touches its own row, never `profiles`.
  - **Document engine relocated**: `ResumePDF.tsx`/`CoverLetterPDF.tsx` (from `app/api/resume/generate/`, `app/api/documents/generate/`) and `DocumentChatEditor.tsx` (from `job-details/`) moved to `components/documents/` — the real prerequisite `build-action-plan-2026-07-28.md` Phase 1.2 called for. `DocumentGenerator`/`ResumeFitSection`/`ResumeGapAnalysis` stayed in `job-details/` — that page still owns "kick off generation," the new workspace is the post-generation editing surface. Also extracted `TagInput`/`FormInput`/`FormSelect`/`FormLabel`/`DEGREE_OPTIONS` out of `ProfileForm.tsx` (never exported before) into `components/ui/FormControls.tsx`, reused by the new Skills/Education section editors instead of duplicating.
  - **`ResumePDF.tsx` fully generalized**: takes `{profile, sections, style}` now, not a fixed `generated`/always-profile-pulled shape. Three real, structurally distinct templates share the same per-section-type render helpers: `structured` (today's layout, now with every style knob live), `centered` (centered header/section titles), `split` (two-column sidebar — Contact+Skills+Education in a ~32% sidebar, Summary+Work Experience in the main column — modeled directly on the real LinkedIn-export PDF layout this session's earlier résumé-sync debugging already surfaced as test data, not invented from scratch). Page size default changed A4→Letter (this project's actual jobs are Canadian/US postings — Letter was always the correct default, just never exposed as a choice before). All 3 existing document routes (`/api/resume/generate`, `/api/documents/generate`, `/api/documents/chat`) migrated to the new shape in the same pass — no parallel old/new content model left half-alive.
  - **`lib/resumeSections.ts`**: `buildDefaultSections`/`buildDefaultStyle` (first-ever generate, or lazy upgrade of an older row that predates this feature) and `mergeGeneratedContent` (regenerate/AI-revise only ever refreshes the AI-authored summary/work_experience content in place — preserves ids, order, visibility, and any hand-edited skills/education instead of rebuilding the array from scratch and silently discarding those edits).
  - **Score-jump changelog, `lib/scoreJump.ts`**: real gap found — `jobs.resume_analysis` (the fit score) was computed against the raw base **profile**, never the tailored résumé itself, so editing a tailored copy could never move the score. `rescoreAgainstTailoredResume` now scores against whatever's actually visible in the workspace (falling back to profile for hidden sections/fields sections don't cover) and persists back to the same `jobs.resume_analysis` column. Wired into regenerate, AI chat revise, and manual section saves (`actions/documents.ts`) — style edits deliberately don't trigger a rescore, since style never changes what the résumé says. `ScoreJumpBanner.tsx` shows the delta, dismissible.
  - **Live preview**: `ResumeLivePreview.tsx` wraps `@react-pdf/renderer`'s `PDFViewer` directly around the same `ResumePDF` component the real download uses — genuinely WYSIWYG, no separate HTML approximation to keep pixel-matched by hand. Loaded via `next/dynamic(..., {ssr:false})` (this project's established browser-only-rendering pattern, see `SettingsModalLoader.tsx`'s history) — the wrapper-file requirement only applies when `dynamic()` is called from a Server Component, so it's called directly inline since `ResumeWorkspace` is already a Client Component. Debounced ~400ms so typing/slider-dragging doesn't thrash it.
  - **Editor tab**: drag-to-reorder via new `@dnd-kit/core`/`@dnd-kit/sortable`/`@dnd-kit/utilities` dependency — no drag library existed in this repo before (the `GripVertical` handles elsewhere, e.g. `ProfileForm.tsx`'s education editor, were always decorative-only). Hide is soft (kept in the array, `visible:false`) so "Show X" always restores it — no arbitrary custom section types, only the 4 existing ones can be shown/hidden/reordered.
  - **Style tab**: template picker (3 real thumbnails), theme, page size, accent-color override, font sizes, header alignment (hidden for `centered` — that template always centers, the knob would be a no-op), skills columns, bullet style, spacing sliders (0-100 stored, mapped to real point ranges only at render time), reset-to-default.
  - **Refinement chips + shared chat hook**: `useDocumentChat` extracted out of `DocumentChatEditor` (send/receive/`onRevised` callback logic) so `RefinementChips.tsx`'s one-tap presets ("stronger action verbs", "shorten summary", "cut filler words") go through the exact same `/api/documents/chat` round trip as typed messages — nearly free, per build-plan.md's own note. Chips and the chat box currently keep independent message-log state (a chip-triggered revision doesn't visually appear in the chat transcript below it) — a known, deliberate simplification, not an oversight; noted for a future pass if it turns out to matter.
  - New route `app/resume/tailored/[jobId]/page.tsx` + `components/documents/ResumeWorkspace.tsx` (the shell). `TailoredResumeCard.tsx` (this same session, built earlier for delete) now links its title to the new editor instead of straight to download; download moved to its own small icon button alongside delete.

(1) Restructured the workspace from 2 tabs (Editor/Style, with score/chips/chat permanently visible below) into **3 tabs** — new `AIRewriteTab.tsx` is now the default first tab, bundling a semicircle score gauge (agent-teal, not the reference's rainbow gradient — matches this app's own established "AI scores use `--color-agent`" rule from `ui-tokens.md`), the score-jump delta, matched/missing-keyword counts, refinement chips, and the chat box together. Deleted the now-unused `ScoreJumpBanner.tsx`. (2) The reference also showed a hover-on-a-bullet "Edit With AI"/"Regenerate" overlay directly in its live preview — **not reproducible as shown**, since `ResumeLivePreview` renders a real PDF inside `PDFViewer`'s iframe, not HTML, so there's no DOM to hover or overlay a button on. Built the same capability in the Editor tab instead (already-interactive HTML, not the PDF pane): new `rewriteResumeBullet` action in `actions/documents.ts` (reuses the existing `bullet_rewrite` feature flag/usage cap from the profile's own single-bullet rewriter, `actions/profile.ts`'s `rewriteBullet` — same quota bucket, deliberately not a second one — but adds real job context: title/company/missing-skills, since these bullets target one specific posting). Each bullet in `EditorTab.tsx`'s `WorkEntryEditor` now has its own "Edit with AI" (opens an inline instruction box) and "Regenerate" (one-click, no instruction) controls.
  - **Real bug found live, immediately after the restart**: the AI Rewrite tab showed nothing at all for a résumé that had never had its fit score computed — `jobs.resume_analysis` is only ever populated as a *side effect* of a save/regenerate/chat-revise, so a freshly-generated, untouched tailored résumé has no score and `AIRewriteTab` rendered a completely empty section instead of any empty state. Fixed with a proper empty state ("No fit score yet" + "Check my fit score" button) wired to a new `analyzeResumeFit` action — gated the same way the pre-existing (profile-scoped) `/api/documents/analyze` route already was (`resume_analysis` feature flag, `documents/analyze` rate-limit key, `resume_analysis` usage cap), just scored against the tailored résumé via `rescoreAgainstTailoredResume` instead of the raw profile.

Deep-research synthesis: best-in-class résumé tools (Resume Worded, Teal, Rezi) rank fixes by real impact instead of a flat error list, and let you jump straight to a "focus mode" on the specific weak spot. Two things from that research deliberately **not** built as literally researched: a "predicted +N points before you commit" number — this app's score only exists as the output of a full LLM call, not a deterministic point system, so a precise pre-commit number would need either a second AI call or a fabricated guess; this project has avoided invented numbers all session (see `AIRewriteTab`'s own "no see-what's-changed list" comment), so impact is shown as a qualitative "High impact"/"Medium impact"/"Quick win" badge from real severity data instead. New `ActionPlan.tsx` merges three already-computed-but-previously-scattered real signals into one ranked, capped-at-6 list: urgent/critical per-bullet issues from the quality grade (this data existed since the quality-analysis feature shipped but was never surfaced in the tailored-résumé workspace before — only on the old `/resume/[id]` page), missing keywords from the fit-score check, and low-graded (C/D/F) dimensions. Clicking a bullet-issue item switches to the Editor tab and **scrolls to + highlights the exact bullet** (new `FocusTarget` type threaded `ResumeWorkspace` → `EditorTab` → `SortableSectionRow` → `SectionEditor` → `WorkEntryEditor`, matched case/whitespace-insensitively by company + substring-matched bullet text, learning from this same session's earlier résumé-sync casing bug). Clicking a keyword/dimension item sends a targeted chat instruction through the existing `useDocumentChat` pipe. Replaced the older "Close the gap" keyword-only chip row (now redundant with the Action Plan's keyword items). Two real `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` violations caught by lint while wiring the focus/highlight effects (same class of bug fixed twice already this session) — fixed by deferring the `setState` calls via `setTimeout(...,0)`, with the highlight's own fade-out timer tracked on a *separate* ref so the defer timer's cleanup can't cancel it early.
  - **Follow-up round 2: the 10-dimension whole-résumé quality grade, brought into the workspace, deliberately NOT live on every edit.** User asked for it to update live on regenerate/edit; real constraint surfaced and confirmed with the user before building: `resume_quality_analysis` is capped at 3/day (a much bigger AI call than the fit-score check, 15/day) — true per-edit live updates would exhaust the daily cap in 3 edits. Agreed design: show the last-computed grade with a manual refresh, auto-refresh only after a full Regenerate (and only if a grade already existed — regenerating shouldn't silently spend a credit on someone who never asked for a quality grade). New `applications.quality_analysis`/`quality_analyzed_at` columns (`migrations/20260806150314_add-applications-quality-analysis.sql`). Extracted the AI-call/parse/count logic out of `actions/resumes.ts`'s `analyzeResume` (the résumé-slot version) into `lib/resumeQuality.ts`'s `runResumeQualityAnalysis` — mechanical refactor, zero behavior change to the existing résumé-slot feature — so the new tailored-résumé version (`analyzeTailoredResumeQuality` in `actions/documents.ts`) shares the exact same prompt/schema instead of a copy-pasted duplicate, and deliberately shares the same `resume_quality_analysis` usage bucket too. New `components/documents/QualityGradeCard.tsx` (compact grade badge + 10-dim grid + "analyzed X ago", not the full page-level `ResumeAnalysisView.tsx`). Two real bugs caught and fixed while wiring this in: (1) `handleAnalyzeQuality`/`handleAnalyze` both needed `sections` in their `useCallback` deps, not just `jobId` — an earlier draft would have silently scored/graded stale content on a manual re-check made shortly after an unsaved edit; a second staleness bug also fixed in `handleRegenerate` itself, which now passes the freshly-regenerated sections into `handleAnalyzeQuality` explicitly rather than relying on its (still-stale, since `setSections` is async) closure. (2) The mount-effect auto-triggering the fit-score check called `setState` synchronously in the effect body, which this project's own `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint rule correctly flagged (same class of bug as `SettingsModalLoader.tsx`'s documented history) — fixed by deferring via `setTimeout(..., 0)`.
  - **Follow-up round 1, real user-reported gaps**: (1) auto-triggers the fit-score check once on workspace mount if no score exists yet (`analyzeResumeFit` lifted to `ResumeWorkspace` as `handleAnalyze`, shared by both the mount effect and the manual button). (2) "Re-check score" upgraded from a text link to a real bordered button. (3) New "Close the gap" chip row in `AIRewriteTab` — one chip per missing keyword (`RefinementChips` gained a `presets?: ChipPreset[]` prop, `{label, prompt}` pairs, so a short chip label like "Add: React" can carry a longer, explicitly-non-fabricating prompt). (4) Real robustness bug fixed in `EditorTab.tsx`'s per-bullet AI rewrite: `applyRewrite` had no try/catch — a thrown network/server error (not a returned `{success:false}`) would leave the spinner stuck forever with zero visible feedback.
