- **2026-08-05 (Phase 7, later session): Résumé→profile sync gained a real "update this entry?" path for Education/Work Experience**, closing the gap flagged at the end of the prior session (additive-only sync could never upgrade an existing entry, even when a fresh extraction was clearly better). Chose the per-entry-consent design over silent auto-replace, both to match trust-sensitive framing already established by the sync modal ("nothing else is touched") and because the `/preview/resume` mockup's `SyncModal` already implied a reviewable diff, not a blind overwrite.
  - `actions/resumes.ts`: `diffSection` now matches Education/Work-Experience entries by `dedupeKey` (was raw case-sensitive string keys before — inconsistent with the real sync logic, fixed as part of this same change) and, for matched-but-content-differs entries, returns a new `replacements: ReplacementCandidate[]` list (`{key, label, current, proposed}`) alongside the existing pure-addition `summary`. "Different" is: for Work Experience, `responsibilities` text or `end_date`/`is_current` changed; for Education, `field` or `graduation_year` changed.
  - `syncResumeToProfile(resumeId, sections, replaceKeys?)` — new third param, `Partial<Record<SyncSection, string[]>>`, defaults to `{}` (fully backward compatible, existing additive-only behavior unchanged if omitted). Only dedupeKeys explicitly present in `replaceKeys` get their existing entry's content replaced with the fresh extraction; every other matched entry is left untouched, same as before.
  - `components/profile/ResumeManager.tsx`'s `SyncModal`: each replacement candidate renders as its own checkbox (default **unchecked** — replacing overwrites existing content, so it needs explicit opt-in, unlike additions which are always safe to apply when the section is selected), with the old text struck through in `error` red and the proposed text in `agent` teal — reuses the same visual language the `/preview/resume` mockup's `SAMPLE_DIFF` already established.
  - `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean. **Not click-tested against a live authenticated session** — same standing environment limitation as before (no completable OAuth login here); dev server was confirmed to compile and serve the changed routes with zero console errors, but the actual checkbox interaction and a real "entry gets replaced" round trip still needs a human pass.
  - **Immediately caught by you live, same day**: the matching key above (`dedupeKey(company, title)`) was still too brittle — you synced a second, differently-worded résumé (a condensed one-page version) on top of an already-synced LinkedIn export, and it appended two duplicates instead of offering to replace: "HCL Technologies"/"Full-stack Developer" vs the new résumé's "HCL America Inc"/"Developer" (same job, same February 2021 start, completely different company/title wording), and "You softech"/"Web Development Intern" vs "You Softech"/"Web Developer Intern" (same job, title reworded). Root cause: matching on company+title text assumes Gemini extracts those consistently across different résumé documents — it doesn't; it paraphrases company display names and job titles freely. **Fixed by re-keying Work Experience matching on `start_date` instead** (confirmed the one field that stayed identical across both real extractions) — `diffSection` and `syncResumeToProfile` in `actions/resumes.ts` both changed to match on `dedupeKey(w.start_date)`, and the replacement candidate's `current`/`proposed` text now shows the full `"{title} at {company}\n{responsibilities}"` (not just responsibilities) so the user can visually confirm two differently-named entries really are the same job before approving. Education's institution+degree matching was left as-is — not implicated by this bug, no evidence it needs the same treatment yet. **The duplicates already live in your profile aren't auto-cleaned** — this fix only prevents new ones; existing dupes need manual deletion via Profile → Work Experience tab → trash icon on the entry (`removeWorkEntry` in `ProfileForm.tsx`, already existed).

- **2026-08-05: Profile rebuilt as a tabbed "Command Center" (real, not just previewed), multi-résumé slot system shipped, and a full résumé quality-analysis feature built + debugged live against real data.** Very long session, several distinct threads, starting from a LinkedIn-import research question and ending on a real, working AI résumé grader. Full detail in `context/jobright-resume-scan-2026-07-30.md` (a live Chrome-extension scan of JobRight, not just pasted screenshots — see `feedback_competitor_scan_with_extension` in cross-session memory for why that distinction matters going forward).

  **LinkedIn import — researched, decided, and closed as "already solved."** True LinkedIn "sync" isn't achievable outside their enterprise Partner Program (consumer OAuth only returns name/email/photo). Confirmed via `agy` that a Data Archive ZIP importer was the only legally-clean path considered — but then live-tested the existing `extractProfile()`/résumé-upload pipeline against a real LinkedIn "Save to PDF" export and found it **already works end-to-end** (pdf-parse handles LinkedIn's two-column layout correctly, Gemini extraction correctly separates real bullets from LinkedIn's page-width hard-wraps). No new code needed for this path — just relabel the upload UI. Two real gaps the live test did surface: `education` was a single object (dropped a second degree), and `certifications`/`languages` had no field at all — both fixed the same session (`education` is now an array — see `migrations/20260729211549_profiles-education-array-certifications.sql` — and `certifications text[]` was added).

  **Profile page rebuilt for real as a tabbed "Command Center"** (`components/profile/ProfileForm.tsx`, `components/ui/Tabs.tsx`, new `components/profile/SectionModal.tsx`) — Personal/Education/Work Experience/Preferences tabs, each a read-only `SummaryCard` with a pencil-edit button opening a scoped `SectionModal` (mirrors `SettingsModal`'s centered glass-chrome recipe, not a new pattern). Design direction chosen via a real Gemini research pass (3 candidate directions scored — Command Center won for reusing existing primitives with the least new engineering risk) and validated against JobRight's own live profile page structure during the later scan (confirmed identical tab set). Real AI features added to the Work Experience bullet editor: `rewriteBullet` (single-bullet AI rewrite), `splitBullet` (turns one dense run-on sentence into several real bullets — needed because LinkedIn/résumé PDFs routinely bundle multiple responsibilities into one grammatical sentence that no regex can safely split), `generateBullets` (note → candidate bullet options, pick-and-add UI). Real width bug found and fixed along the way: `Tabs.tsx`'s tabpanel had no explicit width and was silently shrink-wrapping to whichever tab's content was currently widest — fixed with inline `style={{width:"100%"}}` after a plain `w-full` Tailwind class alone didn't reliably win (this build has a documented history of Lightning CSS/utility-layer quirks; inline style was the one fix confirmed to actually work via live computed-width checks).

  **Multi-résumé slot system shipped** (`actions/resumes.ts`, `components/profile/ResumeManager.tsx`, new `resumes` table — `migrations/20260730182151_add-resumes-table.sql`): up to 5 named résumé slots per user, one marked primary, real upload→extract→sync-to-profile flow. Sync is additive-only by design (never overwrites/removes existing profile content) — confirmed during later debugging that this has a real limitation: it never *upgrades* an existing entry even when a fresh extraction has much better content, which is why stale pre-fix profile data survived several sync cycles undetected (see the bug list below). `getResumeProfileDiff`/`syncResumeToProfile` show a real diff before writing, mapped to the profile's actual section granularity (Personal/Professional/Education/Certifications/Work Experience/Preferences — 6 sections, not JobRight's 4).

  **Résumé quality-analysis feature built**: grade (A-F + plain label), Urgent/Critical/Optional issue counts, a 10-dimension role-fit matrix (reuses `JobEvaluationDimension`'s existing `{dimension, grade, note}` shape — no new type needed), a Strategic Narrative Alignment insight (one holistic paragraph, not a per-dimension score), an Interviewer Skepticism vulnerabilities list (framed as "prepare an answer," not "fix this"), and per-bullet issue drill-down (original text → issue explanation → AI-suggested rewrite → editable save, scoped to that résumé's own `extracted_data`, not the live profile). One structured Gemini call produces all of it (`analyzeResume` in `actions/resumes.ts`) rather than three separate calls. New `resume_quality_analysis` usage key (3/day — matches JobRight's own observed cap for the equivalent feature, confirmed via the live scan) and feature flag, both distinct from the pre-existing `resume_analysis` key (that one is the older résumé-vs-one-job gap check, `agent/resumeGap.ts` — different feature, kept separate on purpose). New `app/resume/[id]/page.tsx` détail route + `ResumeAnalysisView.tsx`.

  **Three real bugs found via live debugging with the user, not assumed fixed:**
  1. Fixing a bullet and re-analyzing appeared to show the exact same issues — root cause was `revalidatePath("/resume")` never invalidating the `/resume/[id]` détail route specifically (Next.js only revalidates the exact path given), so the page kept serving a stale cached render even though the underlying data had genuinely changed. Confirmed via direct DB inspection (`analyzed_at` timestamps and `extracted_data` content were both already correct) before touching any code — the backend logic was right, only the cache invalidation was wrong. Fixed by revalidating both `/resume` and `/resume/${id}` (and the same for rename/setPrimary/delete), plus adding `export const dynamic = "force-dynamic"` to both résumé pages, matching `app/profile/page.tsx`'s existing pattern.
  2. A duplicate "Wizcraft" work-experience entry appeared after a second sync — Gemini's extraction isn't perfectly casing-consistent run to run (`"Wizcraft"` vs `"wizcraft"` for the same company across two different extractions of the same underlying PDF), and `syncResumeToProfile`'s dedup key was case-sensitive, so it treated them as different companies and appended instead of skipping. Fixed with a new `dedupeKey()` helper (lowercase + trim before comparing).
  3. The deeper issue behind "the PDF has many bullets but the profile shows one line": the profile's `work_experience` had **never actually been upgraded** since early in the session — sync's additive-only design means it only adds companies that don't already exist, so once Meridian/HCL/Sutherland/etc. were in the profile (from before today's extraction-quality fixes), later syncs with much better extractions silently skipped all of them. Fixed the live test profile's data directly via a one-off admin-client script (`createAdminClient` from `@insforge/sdk`, run with `node --env-file=.env` from inside the project so ESM module resolution works — the InsForge CLI's raw-SQL endpoint rejected a `$$`-quoted multi-row JSONB update as a security-filtered pattern, so the SDK's REST path was used instead). **Not yet fixed in code**: sync still won't upgrade existing entries going forward — flagged as explicit next-session work, not silently left ambiguous.

  `tsc`/`eslint` clean across the whole project at session end. Everything in this entry was verified against real live data (direct `db query` inspection, not just UI screenshots) before being reported as working.

- **2026-07-29 (same session, later still): Real SMTP configured (Resend) and full email/password auth shipped, alongside the existing Google/GitHub OAuth.** You gave a live Resend API key (`re_...`) directly in chat — saved straight to `.env`, never echoed back. Confirmed the exact SMTP connection details live (`smtp.resend.com`, username `resend`, password = the API key) before configuring anything, and found InsForge's actual SMTP config path is `PUT /api/auth/smtp-config` (not the `config apply`/TOML flow, which has no password field) — set live via the same admin key this project already uses elsewhere. **Real limitation, disclosed, not hidden:** without a verified custom domain in Resend, the default `onboarding@resend.dev` sender can only deliver to the email you signed up to Resend with — real public users won't get verification emails until a domain is added and verified there (your move — I can't buy/register a domain for you, but can walk through the DNS records once you have one).

Next step: try a real sign-up with your own email and confirm the code arrives.

Chose the simpler URL-query-param version over the research's suggested Next.js Intercepting Routes — same bookmarkable/back-button behavior, without that machinery. `/settings` (the real page, shipped earlier this session) stays as a no-JS/hard-refresh fallback.

  - **A real, non-obvious bug found and fixed, not assumed working:** a cold page load with `?settings=1` already in the URL left the modal permanently stuck on its loading spinner — confirmed live the DOM node had **zero React fiber attached** (`Object.keys(el).some(k => k.startsWith('__react'))` was false), i.e. genuinely unhydrated static HTML; its `useEffect` never fired, so the data fetch never ran. Opening the modal via a click while already on a hydrated page worked fine, which is exactly why this wasn't caught by interaction testing alone — only a true cold load with the query param already present reproduced it. Root cause: mounting a `useSearchParams()`-driven client component in the root layout (`app/layout.tsx`, which also wraps the fully static marketing homepage) inside a `<Suspense>` boundary. Fixed by making the modal load client-only via `next/dynamic(..., { ssr: false })` (new `components/settings/SettingsModalLoader.tsx` — `ssr:false` can't be called directly in a Server Component, hence the wrapper) — it now never attempts server rendering at all, sidestepping the SSR/static-page conflict entirely. Verified live in a genuinely fresh browser tab (not just HMR/Fast Refresh state, which was masking the bug during earlier same-tab testing): cold load with the query param now correctly hydrates and resolves. Also found and fixed along the way: an `[isOpen, data]` effect-dependency feedback loop (the effect's own `setData` call was retriggering itself) — simplified to `[isOpen]` alone, with reset-on-close moved into the effect's cleanup function (lint-endorsed) instead of a synchronous `setState` in the effect body (which this project's `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` rule correctly flagged).

- **2026-07-29: Phase 0 cleanup shipped, real account deletion built (GDPR/CCPA), real /settings page shipped, gemini-mcp-tool replaced by Antigravity's `agy` CLI for research.** Long session, several distinct threads:

  **Phase 0 (from `context/build-action-plan-2026-07-28.md`, itself synthesized from two Gemini 3.1 Pro deep-research passes on competitive strategy and UI/architecture review):** `/preview/*` gated behind `NODE_ENV !== 'production'` via a new `app/preview/layout.tsx` (was fully unguarded before — confirmed live, no dev regression). Match-score colors redesigned across `JobResultCard.tsx`/`EvaluationBreakdown.tsx`/`ResumeGapAnalysis.tsx` — replaced a literal green/blue/amber traffic light with a scheme that respects this file's own invariant (`--color-accent` is reserved for user actions, never AI content — every one of these scores IS AI-generated, so the strong tier uses `--color-agent` instead of a generic success green); documented in `ui-tokens.md`. `AutoResearchCompany` renamed to `CompanyResearchAutoLoader` (was a verb phrase, not a component noun); `GlassCursorGlow` moved `shared/` → `ui/` (it's a primitive). **Explicitly NOT touched**, per your instruction: the ComingSoon placeholder routes/nav (`/agent`, `/interview`, `/notifications`) stay as-is until you decide what goes there — only `/settings` was later pulled out of that group by a separate explicit ask (see below).

  **Real account deletion (GDPR/CCPA — was an outstanding Phase 0 legal requirement, now closed).** Confirmed live against InsForge's own OpenAPI spec: there is no documented endpoint to delete an `auth.users` row — only `GET /api/auth/users/{userId}` exists. You asked whether to switch backend vendors over this; recommended against it (disproportionate — InsForge is the entire backend) and instead found the real fix: `auth.users` is a normal Postgres table with no destructive triggers on DELETE (confirmed via `pg_trigger`) and clean `ON DELETE CASCADE` to `user_providers`/`applications`/`usage_daily`/`rate_limit` (confirmed via `pg_constraint`) — reverse-engineered the actual raw-SQL admin endpoint from `@insforge/cli`'s own published source (`POST /api/database/advance/rawsql/unrestricted`, same `INSFORGE_API_KEY` this project already uses). `profiles` and `jobs` have **no FK to `auth.users` at all** — confirmed live, meaning they'd silently survive an auth-only deletion if not deleted explicitly. `agent_runs.user_id -> profiles` has no delete action, so it must be deleted before `profiles` or the FK blocks it. Built `lib/insforge-admin-sql.ts` + `actions/account.ts`'s `deleteAccount()`: reads storage paths before deleting the rows that reference them, best-effort storage cleanup, then one multi-statement SQL call (no explicit `BEGIN`/`COMMIT` — the endpoint rejects those, confirmed live; Postgres's own simple-query protocol already wraps a multi-statement string with no explicit transaction control in an implicit transaction). **Verified live, not assumed**, against a real disposable test account (created via `signUp()` + the admin API, cleaned up after): confirmed the full delete actually removes every row including every cascade, AND confirmed a deliberately-broken statement appended mid-batch rolled back an already-executed `DELETE` too — genuinely atomic, not just "usually works."

Subscription/Credits/Job alerts tabs show an honest "not available yet" state — no monetization or notification-producing backend exists yet, so faking that data was rejected in favor of being honest about it.

- **2026-07-28: Nav restructure + 4 new pages, Gemini rate-limit/quota resilience, and a multi-round company-logo reliability fix.** Long session, several distinct threads:

  **Theme, continued from the day before.** Rebuilt to match Raycast specifically (not just "premium in general") — pulled real computed styles from raycast.com (colors, radius, no-blur nav) via the browser tool and matched them exactly (`#07080a`/`#101111`/`#18191a` neutral ramp, `--radius` 0.5rem, chrome classes converted from blurred to fully solid/flat — Raycast's own header has zero `backdrop-filter`). `Navbar` rebuilt as a true 3-column CSS grid (`grid-cols-[1fr_auto_1fr]`, not `flex justify-between` — the latter only equalizes gaps, doesn't truly center a middle item with 3 unevenly-sized children) so the nav links land exactly centered regardless of logo/actions width, then made into a floating inset rounded card (`sticky top-4 mt-4`, not edge-to-edge) matching a reference screenshot you shared — two real bugs found and fixed here: `top-N` alone doesn't create a resting-position gap (needed `mt-4` too), and `--color-overlay` had been set identical to `--color-background` so the "floating" card had zero color contrast against the page even with correct margin. `JobActionBar` was made non-sticky (was competing with the now-sticky Navbar for the same screen real estate) and `FloatingApplyButton.tsx` (new) added as a persistent Apply CTA — tried `position: fixed` (a real Apple-pattern need `sticky` structurally cannot satisfy: it only catches scrolling *up* past an element, not pinning through a *long* scroll-down), documented as a one-off exception in `ui-rules.md`, then explicitly reverted back to `sticky` at your request with the ban restored — the limitation is documented in the component's own comment instead.
  - Job-details list cards (`Qualification`/`Benefits`/`Responsibilities`/`HiringProcess`) redesigned from plain `list-disc`/`list-decimal` bullets (flagged as looking like "a wall of text") to icon-per-item rows: `CheckCircle2`/`Circle` for Required vs. Preferred (solid = must-have, outline = nice-to-have — the visual weight now matches what the words already meant), `Sparkle` for Benefits, numbered accent circles for Hiring Process, `ArrowRight` for Responsibilities.
  - `JobResultCard.tsx`: Save/restriction-menu icons moved off an absolute overlay (was competing visually with the score) into a real bottom toolbar strip with a `border-t` divider; added a Job Type/Salary/Seniority/Years-experience meta row (icons matching `JobInfo.tsx`'s existing convention) — deliberately NOT adding "25+ applicants"-style numbers the way JobRight does, no free data source for that and this app doesn't fabricate counts; the restriction-icon menu (Already Applied / Not Interested / Report Issue) was a direct ask matching a JobRight screenshot, wired to real actions (`markApplied`, `toggleHideJob`) plus a `mailto:` for Report Issue (no backend for that one yet); company logo bumped from 40px to 56px, and the same `CompanyLogo` component extracted for reuse on `JobInfo.tsx`'s header at 80px.
  - Unscored jobs (right after a search, before the background Inngest evaluation lands) now show a pulsing "Scoring…" placeholder instead of literally nothing — the polling mechanism (`FindJobsForm.tsx`'s `getJobsByIds` loop, already existed, already worked) had nothing to display while waiting, which read as broken even though it wasn't.

  **Full nav restructure, per your blueprint.** Dashboard / **Jobs** (dropdown: Recommended→`/find-jobs`, Liked→`/saved-jobs`, Applied→`/jobs/applied`, External→`/jobs/external`) / Resume / Agent / Interview / Profile, plus Settings/Notifications icons in the right cluster, plus a mobile slide-out drawer (none of this existed before — no dropdown/menu primitive, no mobile nav at all, confirmed via research agent). Custom-built (hand-rolled `useState` open/close, matching `Tabs.tsx`'s existing precedent) rather than pulling in a new dependency, even though `@base-ui/react` is installed for other primitives.
  - **Applied** — minimal real tracking, not the full future Kanban (your call): `jobs.application_status text DEFAULT 'draft'` (migration `20260727100000`, matches `build-plan.md`'s already-planned Phase 15 shape so nothing needs redoing later), `markApplied` action, a "Mark as applied" pill in `JobActionBar`, `/jobs/applied` page.
  - **External** — build now, for real (your call): `/jobs/external` form (URL optional + Title/Company/Description required, a "Fetch from URL" button using `agent/research.ts`'s `fetchViaJinaReader`, now exported), `addExternalJob` action inserts a `source: "url"` job row and fires the *exact same* `jobs/evaluate` Inngest event the real search flow uses — no new evaluation code path, confirmed `evaluateJobCompatibility` only needs id/title/company/description, everything else nullable.
  - **Resume list** — `/resume`, merges two separate storage mechanisms confirmed by research (the one base resume on `profiles.resume_pdf_url`, and per-job generated ones in `applications`, joined to `jobs` for title/company) into one list.
  - **Agent/Interview/Settings/Notifications** — placeholder pages (`ComingSoon.tsx`, new shared component), real routes so the nav doesn't link to 404s, no backend — explicitly deferred, not half-built.
  - `seniority_level`/`years_experience_required` (migration `20260727110000`) added the same zero-marginal-cost way as everything else in `lib/evaluator.ts` — riding the existing per-job Gemini call, empty string if the posting doesn't actually state it.

  **Gemini rate-limit crisis, root-caused and fixed in three layers.** Company research started 500ing (`429` from `lib/models.ts:113`). Root cause, confirmed against your own real Google AI Studio usage dashboard screenshot: `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` had hit **both** its RPM (16/15) and its **daily** RPD cap (502/500) — a fundamentally different problem from a momentary burst, since retrying the same model can't produce quota that doesn't exist until the next day.
  1. `withRateLimitRetry()` — 3 attempts, backoff, added to the ONE shared `complete()` chokepoint so every AI call site (evaluator, research, documents, chat) gets it, not just one script.
  2. A model fallback chain — first attempt guessed model IDs from the dashboard's display names and got it wrong (several read as having quota but are actually deprecated for this account, `404 "no longer available to new users"`); re-verified live against the *exact* OpenAI-compat endpoint `complete()` calls (not just Google's `models.list`, which doesn't guarantee compat-layer support) before finalizing `GEMINI_FALLBACK_MODELS = ["gemini-flash-lite-latest", "gemini-3-flash-preview", "gemini-3.5-flash-lite"]`.
  3. A process-lifetime cooldown cache (`modelCooldownUntil` in `lib/models.ts`) — company research fires several sequential `complete()` calls per request (homepage extraction, each sub-page, final synthesis), and without this, every single call independently re-paid the full ~12s discovery cost of "the primary is exhausted" before falling through, compounding into minutes of dead time across one request. Now only the first call in a chain pays that cost.

  **Company logo reliability — five real iterations, each one live-verified against an actual screenshot, not assumed fixed.** Root cause was never a single bug — it was two independent, previously-invisible problems stacked on each other, both only surfaced by you testing and reporting back:
  1. Broken-image glyphs (not even our own fallback icon) for many companies → added a 5s defensive timeout + `referrerPolicy="no-referrer"`.
  2. Tried Google's favicon service as a second real source → you reported blurry generic globe icons for RBC/Fidelity/BMO/Compass → confirmed live: Google's favicon service doesn't 404 for a domain it doesn't recognize the way Clearbit does, it silently serves its own generic placeholder with a 200 status, defeating any error-based fallback logic. Removed.
  3. You reported previously-working logos (Scotiabank, Moneris) now gone → those were only ever being rescued by the just-removed Google-favicon tier for THOSE specific companies. Re-added it with a CORS-dependent byte-size placeholder-detection guard as a compromise.
  4. You reported globes still appearing → the CORS-dependent detection was silently no-op'ing (this sandbox couldn't confirm Google's CORS headers either way). Root-caused the *actual* underlying issue instead of patching the symptom again: naive client-side domain-guessing (`"Bank of Montreal"` → `bankofmontreal.com`) is simply wrong for any company known by an abbreviation — the real domain is `bmo.com`. Fixed at the source: `lib/evaluator.ts`'s `companyDomain` field now asks Gemini for the company's real domain from its own world knowledge (same zero-marginal-cost pattern, empty string if not confident — a wrong domain risks surfacing a *different* real company's logo), `lib/inngest/functions.ts` writes a Clearbit URL built from it into `company_logo_url` (fallback only, never overwrites a real SerpApi thumbnail).
  5. You reported Scotiabank/Moneris gone *again* after simplifying the client down to `logoUrl`-only → the new server-side resolution only applies to jobs evaluated from 2026-07-28 onward, not retroactively — old jobs had no persisted URL and nothing left to reconstruct one client-side. Restored the client-side naive-guess as a permanent second tier (real, was genuinely carrying real companies the whole time) — final chain is `logoUrl → client-side Clearbit guess → icon`, permanently excluding Google favicons.
  6. Still seeing the exact original symptom (broken-image glyph, `onError` never firing) even after all of the above → new theory, more fundamental: an ad-blocker/tracking-protection extension likely silently blocking direct requests to `logo.clearbit.com` (a known third-party data company) in a way that doesn't cleanly fire a JS error event. Added `app/api/logo/route.ts` — a same-origin proxy with a small explicit host allowlist (not an open SSRF-able proxy) — so the browser never talks to Clearbit directly at all; a 404 from *our own* server fires `onError` reliably regardless of any browser extension.
  7. **2026-07-28, next session — theory 6 was WRONG. Root cause found and fixed for real.** You reported logos that used to render were now blank. Live `curl`/`nslookup` from this environment confirmed `logo.clearbit.com` doesn't resolve in DNS at all (`Could not resolve host`), while an unrelated external host (`google.com`) resolved and connected fine from the same environment in the same test — Clearbit's free Logo API is dead/decommissioned, not ad-blocker-blocked. The same-origin proxy built for theory 6 was solving a problem that didn't exist. Swapped the upstream everywhere it appears — `app/api/logo/route.ts`'s allowlist, `CompanyLogo.tsx`'s domain-guess candidate, and `lib/inngest/functions.ts`'s DB-write fallback — from `logo.clearbit.com` to `unavatar.io`, live-confirmed via `curl` to behave the way Clearbit used to (real domain → real image; unresolvable domain with `?fallback=false` → a real `403`, not Google-favicon-style silent placeholder). Backfilled the 17 existing `jobs` rows that already had a dead `logo.clearbit.com` URL persisted in `company_logo_url` (`UPDATE ... REPLACE(...)`, confirmed via `RETURNING`). **User-confirmed live: logos render now.**
  - `npx tsc --noEmit` and full-project `npx eslint .` clean after every step above.

- **2026-07-27: Liquid Glass narrowed to chrome-only — content cards reverted to flat, dark mode deepened to near-black, researched against real "premium" references.** You flagged the glass-everywhere theme as not looking great and asked for research into what modern/premium sites (Apple, macOS apps) actually do, not just a re-tune. Pulled live computed styles (not visual impressions) from Apple's own macOS Tahoe marketing page, Linear, Vercel, and Raycast via the browser tool — all four reserve `backdrop-filter` blur for floating chrome only (sticky nav/header), use flat cards + hairline borders for ordinary content, and run dark backgrounds near-black (`#07090a`-`#000`), not charcoal-gray. That's the opposite of what shipped 2026-07-24 (`.glass-panel` applied to every content card app-wide). You confirmed the direction via AskUserQuestion: keep both brand hues (amber accent + teal agent) but sparingly, go near-black in dark mode, redesign both themes.
  - **`app/globals.css` `.dark {}` block retuned**: `--color-background`/`-surface`/`-surface-secondary`/`-surface-tertiary`/`-surface-muted` deepened toward near-black (`#0a0b0d`/`#101215`/etc., was `#14171b`/`#1b1f24`/etc.), `--color-border*` lowered toward hairline contrast. `--color-overlay`/`-overlay-dark` (Navbar's always-dark chrome) were already correctly near-black and left unchanged — the gap was specifically in the ordinary card/page tokens.
  - **Ambient `body` backdrop simplified**: removed the animated 2-blob drift/breathe keyframes entirely (none of the 4 references have an ambient gradient wash at all) — replaced with one static, very-low-alpha `--color-accent` radial highlight near the top. This also resolves the one open item from the 2026-07-24 session (the Apple-Music-inspired backdrop was applied but never re-verified) by removing the thing that needed verifying.
  - **`.glass-panel` (plain, ~20 content-card call sites across dashboard/profile/job-details) retired entirely** — reverted to the exact pre-Liquid-Glass pattern the class's own code comment said it replaced: plain `border border-border bg-surface shadow-card` utilities, no custom CSS class. `--shadow-card` is intentionally near-invisible on the new near-black dark surfaces; elevation there comes from the background/surface/border gray-step difference, matching how Linear/Raycast do it, not a shadow.
  - **`.glass-panel-interactive` renamed to `.card-interactive-glow`** (`JobResultCard.tsx`, `GlassCursorGlow.tsx`'s selector) — kept the one genuinely "alive" hover touch (cursor-tracked highlight via `--mouse-x`/`--mouse-y`) but toned down the glow alpha/shadow stack now that it's layered on a flat card instead of a glass one.
  - **`.glass-panel-strong`/`.glass-panel-overlay`/`.glass-pill` (the legitimately floating/sticky chrome — `Navbar`, `JobActionBar`, `FindJobsForm`'s console) kept glass but retuned**: `saturate(200-210%) brightness(108-112%)` dropped to a light `saturate(150%)` touch with no brightness boost (references use plain blur, no vibrancy boost at all), the sheen `background-image` highlight layer removed, box-shadow stacks collapsed from 3-4 down to 1. The two documented Lightning CSS gotchas (`-webkit-` prefix ordering, isolating `animation`/`background-size` from `color-mix()`-sharing blocks) were preserved while doing this.
  - **`LoginCard.tsx`'s `landing-hero-glow` leak fixed** — that class is otherwise scoped to the marketing homepage only; swapped for `bg-surface-secondary` so `/login` is visually consistent with the rest of the app rather than the old marketing gradient.
  - **`context/ui-rules.md` reconciled** — several sections (Cards, Typography Hierarchy, Badges, Buttons, Form Inputs, Table, Empty States) had hardcoded hex left over from a pre-rebrand purple-primary draft (`#7C5CFC`, `#E7EAF3`, `#101828`, etc.) that contradicted `ui-tokens.md`'s actual amber/teal palette and had never been caught before. Rewritten to reference tokens.
  - Landing page (`app/page.tsx`, `components/homepage/*`) deliberately out of scope — it never used the glass system, it's a separate older light-only system from the pre-Liquid-Glass rebrand, and the complaint was specifically about glass.
  - **Not yet re-verified by the user** — this environment can't complete a real OAuth login, so the actual dashboard/job-details/profile card read (does near-black dark mode actually look premium, does the toned-down chrome still feel "alive") needs your own screenshot, same as every prior UI change in this project.

- **2026-07-24 → 2026-07-27: "Liquid Glass" (Apple-inspired) redesign across the whole app.** Started as "add a glass theme to Find Jobs + Job Details," expanded to the whole app on request. Built as a reusable material system in `app/globals.css` — full recipe, dark-mode reasoning, and the "chrome vs. content" split documented in `context/ui-tokens.md`'s new **Liquid Glass** section (read it before touching this system again). Summary of what shipped and what broke along the way, in order:
  - **The system**: `.glass-panel` (default content cards), `.glass-panel-interactive` (add-on for clickable cards — lift, accent border, cursor-following glow), `.glass-panel-strong` (chrome needing to stay legible over scroll, e.g. `JobActionBar`), `.glass-panel-overlay` (dark ink chrome — `Navbar`, `FindJobsForm`'s hero), `.glass-pill` (small controls sitting on an already-glass surface). Applied by replacing the old `rounded-2xl border border-border bg-surface ... shadow-card` pattern across 22+ files (job-details, profile, dashboard) plus `JobResultCard.tsx` and the Navbar/hero.
  - **`components/shared/GlassCursorGlow.tsx` (new)** — a single document-level, rAF-throttled `pointermove` listener (event delegation via `.closest('.glass-panel-interactive')`) that sets `--mouse-x`/`--mouse-y` CSS custom properties, consumed by a radial-gradient glow on hover. The one genuinely interactive part of the system — everything else is a static gradient, which alone reads as "glass-styled" rather than glass.
  - **Bug #1 — `backdrop-filter` silently dropped.** Tailwind v4's build (Lightning CSS) auto-generates an `@supports (color: color-mix(...))` fallback for any `color-mix()`-based declaration, splitting the rule into fragments. Writing standard `backdrop-filter` *before* `-webkit-backdrop-filter` in the same block caused the *standard* property to vanish entirely from compiled output (confirmed via `getComputedStyle` showing `backdropFilter: "none"` despite blur/border/shadow all rendering correctly) — every glass panel had zero blur, which read as "nothing changed" to the user. Fixed by always writing the `-webkit-` prefix first.
  - **Bug #2 — a real layout bug, not a rendering quirk (initially misdiagnosed as one).** `.glass-panel-strong` declared `position: relative`, which — being unlayered plain CSS — silently overrode `JobActionBar`'s `sticky` Tailwind utility class entirely (this file's own established cascade rule: unlayered always beats layered). Combined with that same element's `top-16` offset, the box rendered visually shifted down by 4rem while its original document-flow space stayed reserved, overlapping the job title below it. First suspected as a browser compositing bug (nested sticky+blur elements — a real, documented category of Chromium bug) and "fixed" by removing the Navbar's stickiness; the actual cause was unrelated and required a second real screenshot from the user to properly diagnose. Fixed by removing `position` from all three glass classes — they must never declare position, call sites own that.
  - **Bug #3 — `animation`/`background-size` silently dropped**, same root mechanism as bug #1 (shared a declaration block with a `color-mix()`-based `background-image`). Confirmed via `getComputedStyle(body).animationName === "none"` despite correct source. Fixed by isolating them into their own separate `body { }` rule.
  - **Design miss, not a bug — the first ambient-backdrop version read as muddy in dark mode.** 4 radial gradient blobs mixing warm (`--color-accent`) and cool (`--color-agent`) hues in overlapping regions, worsened by a drift animation sliding the blobs into each other's path over a 60-75s cycle — warm+cool alpha-blended together reads as a brown/olive haze, not a clean gradient, confirmed via a real user screenshot showing exactly this plus an odd extra glow blob on a card (the card's own secondary sheen gradient compounding with the ambient blob bleeding through blur). Redesigned Apple Music-inspired on explicit request: 2 blobs only, kept in opposite corners, dark mode alpha cut significantly (a "whisper of color on near-black," not a vivid wash), drift/breathe animation travel range tightened so the blobs can never cross paths, and the card's redundant secondary sheen glow removed entirely (one clean highlight per card, not two competing ones).
  - **Deliberately not implemented: true SVG-filter refraction** (`feDisplacementMap` as a `backdrop-filter` value — actual light-bending distortion, not just blur). Researched live before deciding against it: Chrome-only (Safari/Firefox silently show a flat unstyled card, not a graceful fallback), and expensive to recompute on any dynamic resize. Judged not worth staking a real product's cross-browser readability on for the visual upgrade it adds over the standard `blur()`/`saturate()`/`brightness()` trio already in use.
  - **Status at end of session: the Apple Music-inspired backdrop redesign was applied but not yet re-verified by the user** — they moved to wrapping up (docs + commit) before confirming it actually reads better. Check this first, next session.
  - `npx tsc --noEmit` and full-project `npx eslint .` clean throughout.

- **2026-07-24: Structural analysis of 25 real scraped postings found two genuine gaps in the extraction taxonomy, plus one thing to explicitly exclude.** Sampled real `jobs.description` text directly (not guessed from general job-posting knowledge) to decide what a "uniform" JD layout should actually cover:
  - **Compensation almost always appears as its own distinct block** ("CAN base pay range per year: $181,000-$241,000", "Pay Details: $50,000-$100,000 CAD") but `jobs.salary` was only populated for 134/771 real jobs — confirmed live via `db query`. Rather than a new card, this now feeds the *existing* `JobInfo.tsx` "Salary Est." slot that was already built but under-populated. `lib/evaluator.ts` now extracts it as a fallback only — never overwrites a real structured value the scraper already wrote.
  - **Hiring/application process is a real, recurring, differentiated category** (Acuity Insights' full "Application Review → Intro Chat → Technical Deep Dive → Team Conversations → Decision" pipeline; SPECTRAFORCE's "1 step, in person or virtual, 1 hour") that had no home anywhere. New `jobs.hiring_process text[]` column (migration `20260724010000_add-jobs-hiring-process.sql`, applied live via `db query` then written to match, per this project's established untracked-migration-history pattern) + new `components/job-details/HiringProcess.tsx` card (hidden if empty, same pattern as Responsibilities/Benefits) + wired into `app/find-jobs/[id]/page.tsx` after Benefits.
  - **Equal Opportunity/accommodation/legal boilerplate appears in nearly every single posting**, worded almost identically regardless of employer — zero per-job differentiation value. `lib/evaluator.ts`'s `aboutRole` prompt rule now explicitly names this pattern and excludes it, rather than relying on a generic "strip ATS boilerplate" instruction that didn't call it out specifically.
  - `lib/evaluator.ts`/`lib/inngest/functions.ts` extended with `salary`/`hiringProcess` (mirrors the `aboutRole`/`responsibilities`/etc. addition earlier this session) — same zero-marginal-cost pattern, rides along on the existing evaluation call.
  - `npx tsc --noEmit` and full-project `npx eslint .` both clean. Verified live: ran the extraction on 3 real jobs before trusting it, checked actual output (`"C$142,400 - C$190,100"`, correctly empty `hiring_process` where the posting had none) before continuing the backfill.

- **2026-07-24: Second real gap found in the same-day extraction fix — the production evaluator never actually produced the cleaned `about_role` summary, only the standalone backfill script did.** Caught live: a real job (Scribendi, evaluated via the actual Inngest pipeline, not the manual backfill) had `requirements` populated but `about_role`/`responsibilities`/`niceToHave`/`benefits` all still null — meaning `JobDescription.tsx`'s `about_role ?? description` fallback was still showing the raw scraped blob for every job evaluated through the real app, not just historical ones. Root cause: the `aboutRole` cleanup (strip ATS/job-board boilerplate, write a real 2-4 sentence summary) had only ever been added to the manual backfill script (later promoted to `scripts/backfill-job-structure.mjs`), never to `lib/evaluator.ts` itself.

Every future evaluation (any new search, from now on) populates it — not just the ones this session manually backfilled.

- **2026-07-24: Saved Jobs page added — the "Saved only" toggle inside `FindJobsForm.tsx` only ever filtered the current search's results, not the user's full save history.** A job saved today would vanish from view entirely after a new search tomorrow, even though `jobs.is_saved` stayed true in the DB — no page anywhere queried saves unscoped by `run_id`.
  - New `app/saved-jobs/page.tsx` (server component) — queries `jobs` by `user_id` + `is_saved = true` only, no `run_id` scoping, so a save persists in view across every future search.
  - Extracted the job-card JSX that `FindJobsForm.tsx` had inline (~65 lines) into `components/shared/JobResultCard.tsx`, reused by both this new page and the existing Find Jobs results list — kept using the real `Card` UI primitive with the exact original className rather than reinventing equivalent-looking markup, to avoid silently drifting from shadcn's actual base classes (`ring-1 ring-foreground/10`, `rounded-xl`, etc. — not this app's own tokens, so an approximate rebuild could easily have looked subtly different).
  - Added a "Saved Jobs" link to `Navbar.tsx`'s nav items, between Find Jobs and Profile.
  - `FindJobsForm.tsx`'s own "Saved only" toggle was left as-is (still useful for filtering within one search's results) — this is an addition, not a replacement.
  - `npx tsc --noEmit` and full-project `npx eslint .` both clean.

- **2026-07-24: A real, previously-latent bug fixed — `jobs.responsibilities`/`requirements`/`nice_to_have`/`benefits` were never populated by anything, ever.** Found while verifying the JobRight-parity layout below on two real jobs: both had `matched_skills`/`missing_skills`/`evaluation` (populated by the evaluator) but `NULL` on all four JD-structure columns, confirmed via direct `db query`. Traced with a full-codebase grep: `lib/evaluator.ts`'s `buildJobText()` *reads* these fields as input (expecting them to already exist) and the UI reads them, but no code path anywhere — not the scraper (`lib/actions/scraper.actions.ts`), not the evaluator, nothing — ever wrote to them. These columns had been dead since the table was designed.
  - Fixed at the cheapest point: the evaluator's existing Gemini call in `lib/evaluator.ts` already receives the job's full raw description text (via `buildJobText`) to grade fit — extended that same call to also extract `responsibilities`/`requirements`/`niceToHave`/`benefits` as four more fields on the existing per-job JSON response, rather than adding a second AI call. Zero marginal cost per evaluation going forward. `maxTokens` bumped 8000 → 10000 for the extra per-job output (a real headroom need, distinct from the earlier documented completeness-truncation investigation — see the code comment). Extraction instructions follow the same "spell out the exact JSON shape" and "never invent, only extract" pattern already established for `agent/research.ts`'s `extractStructured` helper (the systemic bug fixed in the 2026-07-22/23 session).
  - `lib/inngest/functions.ts`'s per-job DB `.update()` (the same call that already writes `match_score`/`matched_skills`/etc.) now also writes these four fields.
  - **Backfilled the two real jobs found with this gap**, verified live end-to-end: fetched their raw `description` text via `db query`, ran the exact extraction prompt against the app's own `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` model (same model `lib/models.ts` uses, free tier, $0 real cost — confirmed, not assumed), inspected the actual output for invented content before writing anything (none found — every item traces to real text in the posting), then wrote it back via `db query UPDATE`. Both jobs now have real, non-empty `responsibilities`/`requirements`/`nice_to_have`/`benefits`. Scratch files used for this (`.scratch_*`) deleted after, never committed.
  - Going forward this fills in automatically for every newly-evaluated job. Jobs evaluated *before* this fix (any job in the DB with a non-null `evaluation` other than the two backfilled above) still have empty JD-structure fields and won't self-heal — a real re-evaluation (or a broader backfill) is needed if you want the fix applied retroactively across the whole table, not attempted here since it's a real (if free-tier) API call per job and wasn't asked for.
  - `npx tsc --noEmit` and full-project `npx eslint .` clean.

- **2026-07-24: Job description layout split into three JobRight-parity cards (Responsibilities / Qualification / Benefits).** Working from a real JobRight screenshot. Previously, `JobDescription.tsx` bundled the "about role" paragraph plus Responsibilities and Benefits as two bullet sub-sections inside one card; JobRight instead shows three visually distinct cards.
  - New `components/job-details/Responsibilities.tsx` and `Benefits.tsx` (both follow the existing icon-header card pattern — `h-8 w-8 rounded-full bg-surface-secondary` icon circle + title, same as `JobDescription`). `Benefits` renders as a `sm:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-3` grid rather than a single column, matching the screenshot. Both return `null` on an empty array.
  - `JobDescription.tsx` simplified to just the about-role paragraph + full-post-link — its old `responsibilities`/`benefits` props and the `BulletList` sub-section logic were removed (fully superseded, not kept for back-compat).
  - `Qualification.tsx` reworked: skill tags were previously split into two labeled sections ("You have" / "Gap skills") stacked vertically — merged into a single `flex flex-wrap` row (matched tags first, then gap tags), matching JobRight's layout where a check/✕ icon on each tag is the only distinguishing signal. Added the icon-header pattern (`Award` icon, previously this card used a bare uppercase label with no icon) for visual consistency with its two new sibling cards, an intro sentence, and a small top-right legend ("Represents the skills you have"). Required/Preferred bullet columns unchanged. Reuses the existing `bg-success-lightest`/`bg-accent-muted` skill-badge token pair — no new colors introduced.
  - `app/find-jobs/[id]/page.tsx` Overview tab reordered: `JobDescription` → `Responsibilities` → `Qualification` → `Benefits` (was `Qualification` → `JobDescription`, with Responsibilities/Benefits nested inside the latter).
  - Verified live: `npx tsc --noEmit` and full-project `npx eslint .` both clean. Rendered via a temporary addition to `/preview` (removed after review, per that route's own "delete once approved" convention) with sample data mirroring the reference screenshot — checked both light and dark themes in the browser, confirmed correct token usage and grid/row layout before removing the scratch addition. Real authenticated click-through on a live job (not just `/preview`) is still yours to do, same standing limitation as other sessions.
