- **Large session (2026-07-22 → 2026-07-23): JobRight-parity job details page + full LinkedIn/Apify integration.** Full summary in `RESUME.md`'s "30-second state" — this entry is the detailed technical record underneath it. Roughly chronological:

  - **Finished résumé-fit analysis left uncommitted from a dropped session** (`agent/resumeGap.ts`, `/api/documents/analyze`, `AnalyzeResumeFitButton.tsx`, `ResumeFitSection.tsx`) — the route was missing its `checkRateLimit` call, added to match every other AI route's pattern.

  - **`profiles.email` never existed as a column**, despite being read in a dozen places (admin/provider resolution, usage metering, PDF contact info, the profile form). Found live when `app/api/agent/research/route.ts` — the one place that explicitly named the column — crashed with `column profiles.email does not exist`. Everywhere else it silently evaluated to `undefined`, and since `isAdminUser()` returns `false` for a falsy email, **the admin account had been silently forced onto Gemini-only/full rate limits since Phase 0 shipped**, with zero errors to notice it by. Fixed: added the column, extended the existing auto-provision trigger (from the 2026-07-21 profile-provisioning fix) to populate it on signup, backfilled both real accounts from `auth.users`. Migration `20260721070000_add-profiles-email.sql`.

  - **`jobs.external_id` had a global `UNIQUE` constraint, but RLS enforces per-user row ownership.** When two accounts' searches surfaced the same real job posting (same SerpApi `external_id`), the upsert's `ON CONFLICT` tried to `UPDATE` a row RLS wouldn't let it touch — and the *whole batch*, not just the colliding job, silently returned zero rows (`Insforge upsert did not return any saved jobs`). Verified live by simulating the exact collision (same `external_id`, two real account IDs) before and after. Fixed: constraint changed to `UNIQUE(user_id, external_id)`, `lib/actions/scraper.actions.ts`'s `onConflict` updated to match. Migration `20260722010000_scope-jobs-external-id-per-user.sql`.

  - **Job details page restructured into JobRight's Overview/Company tabs**, following a full JobRight teardown comparison already in `build-plan.md` §H1. New `components/ui/Tabs.tsx` (plain client component, local state, no new dependency — this codebase has no Radix, so no shadcn-style Tabs primitive existed). Every genuinely free gap from that comparison shipped in the same pass:
    - **Sticky action bar** (`JobActionBar.tsx`, replaces the old `JobActions.tsx` — deleted, fully superseded) with real Save/Hide, backed by new `jobs.is_saved`/`is_hidden` columns (migration `20260722020000_add-jobs-saved-hidden.sql`) and a new `actions/jobs.ts` Server Action file (first one scoped to jobs — `actions/profile.ts` was profile-only before). Find Jobs list query now filters `is_hidden = false`.
    - **Correctable skill tags + Required/Preferred split** (`Qualification.tsx`, new) — consolidates what used to be split across `MatchScore.tsx`'s skill badges and `JobDescription.tsx`'s requirement bullets. Clicking a tag calls a new `correctSkillTag` action (in `actions/jobs.ts`) that moves the skill between `matched_skills`/`missing_skills` — a data correction, not a re-run of the evaluator (`match_score` deliberately untouched).
    - **Posting freshness** — confirmed live via a real SerpApi call that `detected_extensions.posted_at` genuinely returns a relative string ("2 days ago") for free; wired end-to-end (`jobs.posted_at`, migration `20260722030000_add-jobs-posted-at.sql`, extracted in `lib/jobScraper.ts`). A structured work-mode field does **not** exist in SerpApi's response (only sometimes embedded in free-text titles) — deliberately did not add a `work_mode` column that would overclaim precision; used a lightweight client-side "Remote" text-match heuristic instead, no schema change.
    - **`NetworkSignals.tsx` rewritten from scratch, twice.** First pass: dropped JobRight's fabricated "3 former colleagues work here" headcount (no free people-search data source exists for it, confirmed via research) in favor of a real "you worked at {company} before" fact derived from the candidate's own `work_experience` (new `lib/networkSignals.ts` helpers) plus a LinkedIn search deep-link. Second pass, after you reported the link "not working": the deep-link was jamming company + past-employer + school names into one flat `keywords` string — LinkedIn's basic search can't do a real cross-referenced search that way, it just text-matches the jumbled phrase and returns nothing useful (and drops the whole query if the visitor isn't logged in, redirecting to a bare login page). Fixed to search on the hiring company alone (a real, reliably-working search) with past employers/school shown as separate "look for" hint chips instead. Also fixed a design-token misuse caught in the same pass: the "you worked here before" badge was using `bg-agent-light`/`text-agent-dark` — tokens `ui-tokens.md` explicitly reserves for AI-*generated* content, and this is a deterministic string-match fact, not something the AI produced. Switched to the success-token pair used elsewhere for positive facts.

  - **`agent/research.ts`'s entire browsing layer replaced — Browserbase was never actually configured** (`BROWSERBASE_API_KEY`/`BROWSERBASE_PROJECT_ID` both blank in `.env`, confirmed). Replaced Stagehand/Browserbase with **Jina Reader** (`r.jina.ai` — renders JS pages server-side on their infra, returns clean markdown, genuinely free at this app's volume, no browser to host on Vercel) + Gemini extraction, for both the main company-research dossier (`collectBrowserResearch`) and a new **Leadership Team** lookup. `@browserbasehq/sdk`/`@browserbasehq/stagehand` uninstalled, `lib/browserbase.ts` deleted (fully unused after the swap). `resolveHomepageUrl`'s redirect-following switched from Browserbase's fetch API to native `fetch` (follows redirects by default — simpler, one less dependency).
    - **Leadership Team lookup** (`researchLeadershipTeam`, new `LeadershipTeamButton.tsx`, wired into `CompanyResearch.tsx`) is a waterfall, cheapest first: **Wikipedia** (free, no key — verified live returning real CEO/CFO names for Scotiabank and Meridian Credit Union; a search-query precision bug was found and fixed along the way — short company names like "Affirm" matched unrelated Wikipedia articles, fixed by appending "company" to the search query) and **site-guessing** (free, tries common `/about`/`/team`/etc. paths) run in parallel; only if both find nothing does it fall back to **paid Apify** (`harvestapi~linkedin-company` + `harvestapi~linkedin-profile-search`, CXO-seniority-filtered, ~$0.10-0.11/lookup) — and only if `APIFY_API_TOKEN` is configured. A title-keyword safety filter (`chief|ceo|cfo|coo|cto|cmo|president|founder|chair...`) was added after live testing showed LinkedIn's own "CXO" seniority tag is noisier than the name implies at large companies (Scotiabank's CXO-tagged results were department-level Managing Directors, not real executives) — the Apify path now returns "not found" rather than showing misleading noise labeled as leadership.
    - **A real latency/UX bug in the site-guessing step:** 7 guessed page paths were tried sequentially, each with a 25s Jina timeout — worst case several minutes, which reads as "not working" even though it would eventually finish. Fixed to run all 7 concurrently (`Promise.all`) — verified live: a real company that previously could have taken minutes now resolves in ~4s.
    - **A general, non-Leadership-specific bug found via the Scotiabank case:** `cleanCompanyName()`'s legal-suffix-stripping regex (`\s*(Inc|LLC|Corp|Co...)`) was unanchored and matched the substring "co" *inside* "Scotia**bank**", truncating the name to "S" and resolving a guessed homepage of `https://www.s.com`. This is a **pre-existing bug**, not introduced this session — affects any company name containing "co"/"inc"/"ltd"/"corp" as a substring, not just Scotiabank (Coinbase, Discovery, Cisco, Costco, Comcast...). Fixed by requiring a real preceding space (`\s+` not `\s*`) before the suffix match, verified against 9 real company names including all the substring-collision cases.
    - **The big one — a systemic bug in the shared `extractStructured()` helper, found via the Wikipedia/Scotiabank leadership case but affecting every extraction call in the file.** The helper's prompt only said "return valid JSON" — it never told the model what JSON *keys* to use. Gemini would correctly find the real data (verified: it found Scotiabank's actual CEO/CFO from the Wikipedia page) but return it in a plausible-but-wrong shape (a bare array `[{...}]` instead of the required `{"leadershipTeam": [...]}` object), `schema.safeParse()` would silently fail, and the caller saw "nothing found" — indistinguishable from a genuine miss. This hit **all four** `extractStructured` call sites: the main homepage extraction, the sub-page extraction, and both leadership paths — meaning the main auto-populated Company Research dossier had likely been silently degrading to its text-only fallback far more often than it should, even when the Jina fetch itself succeeded, for the entire time this pattern existed. Fixed by adding an explicit `shapeDescription` parameter to `extractStructured` and passing the real expected shape at every call site. Verified live with the exact same Wikipedia data that failed before — now returns the correct shape.

  - **`insiderConnectionsLookedUp`/`leadershipLookedUp` feedback + cost-safety pattern.** Found via a real user report ("Find leadership team still not working, no error message and nothing came up") — root cause was that a successful search which found nothing was indistinguishable in the UI from "never searched" (`!leadershipTeam?.length` gated both the button visibility and the empty-state message), so the user kept re-clicking, and **each click re-ran the entire paid Apify fallback** since the server-side guard only checked for a non-empty result, not "was this already attempted." Confirmed via real `agent_logs` timestamps showing 3 full re-runs (each hitting "Free sources found nothing — searching LinkedIn (paid lookup)") for the same job before the fix existed. Fixed with a `leadershipLookedUp: boolean` field on the dossier, set on any completed attempt regardless of result, checked both for UI state (three-state: not-looked-up / looked-up-empty / looked-up-found) and as a server-side re-spend guard. Built into `insiderConnectionsLookedUp` from the start on the newer feature, not as a follow-up patch.

  - **Full "Insider Connections" + email-reveal feature**, matching a real JobRight screenshot: three real-people buckets (`InsiderConnections.tsx`, new) — Beyond Your Network (broad `currentCompanies` search), From Your Previous Company (`currentCompanies` + `pastCompanies`, using only the candidate's single most recent past employer — deliberately not more, since attributing *which* past employer a match shares isn't verifiable in "Short" profile mode with several employers searched at once, and this app doesn't display a fact it can't check), From Your School (`currentCompanies` + `schools`). Verified live that combining `currentCompanies` + `pastCompanies` in one call genuinely is an AND (69 real matches for a Scotiabank/Meridian Credit Union intersection, not a broad thousands-person "anyone at Scotiabank" pool) — this confirmed the cost model (2-3 separate search calls needed for 2-3 buckets, not one combined call) before writing the expensive part.
    - **Email reveal was reworked mid-build after two real blockers, both confirmed live, not assumed:** the original plan (`dev_fusion/Linkedin-Profile-Scraper`, $0.01/profile, URL-based) returned a real 403 — that actor's developer restricts free-Apify-plan users to Console-only use, no API access, regardless of account-level permission approval (a separate gate that was also hit and resolved first: `full-permission-actor-not-approved`, needing a one-time manual approval at a URL the error response provides). Fallback: HarvestAPI's own search actor (same vendor as Insider Connections) supports a `"Full + email search"` `profileScraperMode` and does return real emails (verified live: a real `emails: [{email, status: "risky", qualityScore}]` array) — but it's filter-based (search by name+company), **not URL-based**, so it can reveal email for someone already found via the connections search (their name/company are already known) but cannot look up an arbitrary pasted URL at all. The standalone "Find Any Email" paste-box from the JobRight screenshot was **dropped** — no working backend exists for it on this Apify tier. `email_lookup`'s real cost is ~$0.10/lookup (not the originally-planned $0.01), daily cap lowered from 10 to 4 to match.
    - New `ConnectionPerson`/`InsiderConnections` types (`types/index.ts`), `insider_connections` (3/day) and `email_lookup` (4/day) `UsageAction`s (`lib/usage.ts`), new routes `/api/agent/research/connections` and `/api/documents/email`, new components `InsiderConnectionsButton.tsx`, `EmailLookupButton.tsx`, shared `LinkedInGlyph.tsx` (extracted from `CompanyResearch.tsx` since two components now need it — `lucide-react`'s installed version dropped brand/logo icons entirely, no `Linkedin` export).
    - Placed in the **Company tab**, not Overview (where it was originally planned) — moved after realizing it shares Leadership's "company research must already exist" prerequisite; Overview would let a user hit a confusing "Run company research first" error before ever visiting the tab that triggers that research.
    - **Explicit, deliberate scope/legal decision, discussed at length before building:** email lookup only ever *displays* a found email for the candidate to use themselves — Sortie never sends anything on the candidate's behalf. This was the resolution to a real CASL/GDPR concern (Canada's Anti-Spam Legislation, since this app is Toronto-based) — automated bulk outreach is what those laws actually target; a candidate manually deciding to email one specific person they found is normal individual networking, a materially different risk profile. Not a legal opinion, just the reasoning basis for proceeding.
    - **Real Apify spend this session, disclosed as incurred:** roughly $0.55-0.65 total across all verification calls. Several tests deliberately reused already-known data (a previously-discovered profile URL, an already-resolved company URL) instead of fresh speculative lookups, at your explicit request to minimize cost during development.

  - `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx next build` clean after every step throughout. Every new DB field verified via real round-trip writes/reads (then cleaned up), the re-spend guards verified by pre-seeding state directly via `db query` (free) rather than spending real Apify calls to test them. `/preview` mockups added/updated for `NetworkSignals` and `InsiderConnections`, checked live in the browser — no console errors, correct rendering in both cases. **Standing limitation, unchanged:** no way to complete a real Google OAuth login in this environment, so the actual authenticated job-details page click-through for all of the above is still yours to do.

- **Multi-user bug fixed (2026-07-21) — profiles were never auto-created.** You logged in with a second Google account and found: resume extraction intermittently errored, "generate" errored, and **profile saving failed entirely**. Root-caused live (2 rows in `auth.users`, only 1 in `public.profiles`): **no code anywhere in the app ever inserted a `profiles` row.** The original account's row predates this or was made by hand, so the bug was invisible with one user. `saveProfile()` runs an `UPDATE`, matched zero rows, and dead-ended every new user at "Profile not found." Every reported symptom traced to this one cause.
  - Fix (migration `20260721040000_auto-provision-profiles.sql`, applied live): safe column defaults so no path yields NULL arrays (app code calls `profile.skills.join(...)` and would crash on NULL), an `on_auth_user_created` trigger on `auth.users` that auto-provisions the row, and a backfill for existing accounts. Verified live: `auth.users` and `profiles` both at 2, trigger registered + enabled, backfilled row has `[]`/`{}` not NULL.
  - `saveProfile()` also now **self-heals** — if the UPDATE ever matches no row it INSERTs instead, so a missing row can't dead-end a user again. RLS still enforces `id = auth.uid()`.
  - **Second, separate bug found while fixing this — same class as the evaluator batch bug:** `extractProfile` used `maxTokens: 800`, too small for a real resume's `work_experience` array. Longer resumes truncated mid-JSON, `JSON.parse` threw, and it surfaced as a generic failure — which is exactly why it failed *intermittently* (short resumes fit, long ones didn't). Raised to 4000, added a specific parse-failure message, and added rate-limit (429) detection so Gemini free-tier throttling reads as "wait a minute" instead of "broken." Same truncation risk fixed in `app/api/resume/generate/route.tsx` (1000→4000) and `agent/documents.ts` (resume gen/revision 1200/1300→4000, cover letter 700/900→1500/2000). **`maxTokens` is a ceiling, not a charge** — raising it costs nothing unless output is genuinely longer, so this is safe under the minimum-cost constraint.
  - `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. **Not yet re-tested in-browser by a real second account** — that's the remaining verification (task #34).

- **Competitor teardown + design previews (2026-07-21, same session).** You asked for a systematic feature scan of JobRight — the closest live analog to Sortie — via screen recordings, then live browsing once the Claude-in-Chrome extension was installed on the right profile (first attempt failed silently: extension was on a different Chrome profile with no session, cookies don't cross profiles).
  - **Full anatomy captured**, now in `build-plan.md`: onboarding (role picker → work-auth → résumé upload → seniority/attribution), the job list and detail page (match sub-scores, correctable skill tags with color-coding, Required/Preferred split, Insider Connection network panel, "Ask Orion" conversational per-job AI chat, Crunchbase-sourced company/funding/leadership/news), the résumé editor workspace (AI Rewrite with score-jump + changelog + refinement chips, Editor with drag-to-reorder sections, Style with template/column/spacing controls), interview question bank (community-contributed, gated), settings (login/security, account deletion), and auth (Google One Tap, no separate signup screen — OAuth lands straight in onboarding).
  - **One explicit scope decision:** their premium tier is 1-on-1 human recruiter coaching. Deliberately not copying the delivery model (doesn't scale, expensive) — but reusing the problem-framing ("I'm applying but hearing nothing" etc.) for our own AI-powered equivalents, which already cover the same three problems.
  - **Real gap found and corrected mid-session:** initially under-weighted the "Insider Connection" network/email panel in a chat summary even though it was already captured in the anatomy table — a communication miss, not a research gap. Re-verified by scrolling the live page directly (not just reading extracted text), which also surfaced a real visual detail text extraction had missed: skill tags are color-coded (green thumbs-up = matched, plain grey = unmatched), not just clickable.
  - **Five design-preview pages built** from this research, real components with dummy props, in Sortie's own tokens, verified live in both themes: `/preview` (résumé gap analysis in two states, job card with decision-support badges, teaching loading state, network signals, generation-progress, problem-framed help cards), `/preview/onboarding` (interactive 3-step flow), `/preview/resume` (multi-résumé manager + editor/style workspace), `/preview/more` (interview bank, notifications empty state, settings incl. account deletion, beta-launch modal).
  - **`components/shared/NetworkSignals.tsx` built as real, reusable code** (not just a preview mock) — deep-links to LinkedIn's people-search with company + past-employer/school keywords, no scraping, no paid API, no per-lookup cost. Real email lookup (Hunter/Apollo-style) deliberately deferred: paid ($39–49/mo) and carries CASL/GDPR exposure that needs a compliance pass before building.
  - **`build-plan.md` restructured** with a new "START HERE — Session Handoff" section (mission statement, current state, full JobRight differentiation strategy) and a Master Feature Inventory (sections A–L) consolidating every feature ever discussed — strategy artifacts, teardown findings, brainstorms — into one place, so a future session searches instead of re-deriving.
  - `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. **Not yet built:** real backend wiring for the résumé gap analysis or network signals — both are currently UI-only, next up.


**Phase:** Phase 0 (minimum-cost public launch) is still gating real public launch — same 3 items outstanding as before (Privacy Policy/Terms + account deletion, two-account isolation test in-browser, real Vercel deploy), untouched this session. Everything else below is feature work that shipped on top of Phase 0's 6 already-done items. Most recent: the 2026-07-22 → 2026-07-23 session (top entry above) — JobRight-parity tab restructure, several real bugs found via live testing, and a full LinkedIn/Apify integration (Leadership Team, Insider Connections, email reveal). Before that: JobRight competitor teardown + `/preview` design pages (2026-07-21), multi-user profile-provisioning fix (2026-07-21), Phase 7 (Model Router) and Phase 9 (10-Dimension Evaluator) (2026-07-19 → 2026-07-20). **`RESUME.md`'s "30-second state" is the fast summary of the most recent session** — read that first, this file is the detailed history underneath it.
**Last completed:** See the top entry in this section (2026-07-22 → 2026-07-23) — full JobRight-parity tab restructure, Leadership Team + Insider Connections + email reveal via a real Apify/LinkedIn integration, and multiple real bugs found and fixed via live testing (see `RESUME.md` for the fast summary).
  - **Found and fixed a real, unrelated bug in round 1:** `lib/evaluator.ts` already computes a holistic `overallGrade` per the Phase 9 spec, but `lib/inngest/functions.ts` silently dropped it — never saved to the DB. New `jobs.overall_grade` column (migration `20260720050000_add-jobs-overall-grade.sql`, applied live) + one-line fix in `functions.ts` to actually persist it. Existing jobs will show no overall-grade badge until re-evaluated.
  - **A first width-only implementation attempt in round 1 was wrong and was caught before shipping:** tried the classic `relative left-1/2 w-screen -translate-x-1/2` full-bleed CSS trick. Screenshot verification showed real horizontal+vertical scrollbars and an overflowing duplicate-looking sliver of content — the well-known `100vw`-includes-scrollbar-width bug. Replaced with a structural fix (separate component + a genuinely wider container), no viewport-unit tricks — this same structural fix is what made round 2's "widen everything" ask trivial (just delete the inner wrapper divs).
  - **Round 2, richer per-dimension notes:** `lib/evaluator.ts`'s system prompt changed from "one-line justification" to "substantive 2-3 sentence justification... cite specific concrete detail" per dimension.
  - **Round 2, a serious reliability bug found and fixed via live testing, not part of the original ask:** tested the richer-notes prompt against a real 10-job chunk (the production batch size) and found 8 of 10 jobs silently fell back to neutral C-grade placeholders. Root-caused by inspecting the raw completion directly (not just the parsed result): it was NOT token truncation — raising `maxTokens` from 6000 to 24000 made zero difference, because the actual response was only ~1500 tokens both times. The model (Gemini 3.1 flash-lite) was returning syntactically valid JSON that simply omitted most of the requested jobs — schema validation alone can't catch an incomplete batch, only a length check can. Fixed by reducing `lib/inngest/functions.ts`'s chunk size from 10 to 5 (verified 3/3 clean runs with zero fallbacks at size 5 and 2/2 at size 7; size 8 already failed the same way size 10 did — the cliff is sharp, between 7 and 8). `maxTokens` right-sized to 8000 for the smaller chunks. Also added a `console.error` in `lib/evaluator.ts` when the model returns fewer evaluations than requested, so a future recurrence is visible in logs instead of silently degrading again.
  - **Verified live**: temporary route + screenshots at multiple widths confirmed the grid renders cleanly with no overflow and all 5 grade tiers are visually distinct; the chunk-size fix was verified with real DB jobs and the real system prompt, not synthetic fixtures. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Not yet checked against a real logged-in session (same standing limitation) — and the chunk-size fix specifically hasn't been observed end-to-end through a real Inngest-triggered Find Jobs run yet, only through the underlying `evaluateJobCompatibility` function called directly.
**Next:** Same 3 Phase 0 items as before, still not started — Privacy Policy/Terms + account deletion, a real two-account isolation test in-browser, and the actual Vercel deploy. These gate any real public launch and should come before new features. Beyond that: a future paid-subscription tier is the stated plan for eventually gating Insider Connections/email lookup (both currently free/ungated since no billing system exists yet) — not started. **Everything through this session is committed and pushed to `origin/main`** — run `git status`/`git log` to confirm before assuming otherwise.

- **Phase 0, item 1 — RLS security gate (2026-07-20) — a real, live vulnerability found and fixed:**
  - **The hole:** `profiles`, `jobs`, and `agent_runs` had RLS **disabled** entirely (found via `pg_class.relrowsecurity`) — any authenticated user (or the anon key) could read/write any user's rows straight through the PostgREST API. Same class of gap as the earlier `applications` RLS incident, on three more tables.
  - **A dependent bug fixed first:** `lib/inngest/functions.ts` read `jobs`/`profiles` with the cookie-based `createInsforgeServer()` client — anonymous inside a background function (no request cookies there). It only worked because RLS was off; enabling RLS without this fix would have silently broken evaluation (empty reads, no error). Switched both reads to the service-key `createAdminClient`; cookie client fully removed from the Inngest function.
  - Migration `20260720120000_enable-rls-profiles-jobs-agent-runs.sql` — RLS + the same four-policy own-row pattern `applications`/`agent_logs` already use. Applied live via `db query`.
  - **Verified live:** `pg_policies` shows all 5 tables RLS-enabled with 4 policies each; direct anon-key API calls to all four user-data tables now return `[]`. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

- **Phase 0, item 2 — Gemini-only public cost policy (2026-07-20):** new `lib/access.ts` — `isAdminUser(email)` reads a comma-separated `ADMIN_EMAILS` env allowlist (zero new infra/schema, since there's no paid tier yet). `resolveProvider(preferredModel, email)` is the single point every AI call site now goes through — non-admins always resolve to `gemini` regardless of what's saved on their profile, closing a real gap a naive UI-only restriction would have missed (a stale non-Gemini `preferred_model` value, or a direct action call bypassing the selector). Enforced at both write-time (`setPreferredModel` rejects non-Gemini for non-admins) and read-time (all 6 generation/evaluation call sites use `resolveProvider`, not raw `?? "gemini"`). `ModelSelector.tsx` also hides GPT/Claude options from non-admins in the UI. **Verified live** with real admin/stranger emails against the actual functions — all four cases resolved correctly.

- **Phase 0, item 3 — Per-user daily metering (2026-07-20):** new `usage_daily` table (RLS-protected, own-row) + `lib/usage.ts`'s `checkAndConsumeUsage()`. Caps: 5 searches/day, 10 document generations/day, 3 company-research runs/day, 5 resume imports/day — admins unmetered. Wired into all 6 real spend points: `scrapeAndEvaluateJobs` (search — gates before the SerpApi call too, not just AI), `/api/documents/generate`, `/api/documents/chat`, `/api/resume/generate`, `/api/agent/research`, `actions/profile.ts`'s `extractProfile`. `FindJobsForm.tsx` previously swallowed all search errors silently (`console.error` only, no UI) — added a real `searchError` state so a limit-reached message (or any future error) actually reaches the user. **Verified live:** real DB round-trip test hit the exact cap (5 allowed, 6th blocked) and confirmed admin bypass; cleaned up test rows after.

- **Phase 0, item 4 — Signup cap / waitlist (2026-07-20):** InsForge auto-provisions `profiles` on OAuth with no app-code insert to intercept — confirmed via `pg_trigger` (no trigger exists either) and a repo-wide search. The only real gate point is post-login access. New `profiles.created_at` column (didn't exist) ranks signup order; `lib/access.ts`'s `isWithinSignupCap()` compares a user's rank against `MAX_SIGNUPS` (unset/0 = uncapped) so **existing users already in are never retroactively locked out** by later signups pushing the raw count over the cap — only genuinely new accounts past the limit see it. Wired into `requireUser()` in `lib/auth.ts`, the choke point every protected page already calls — redirects to new `/waitlist` page. **Verified live:** the count-ranking query against the real DB returned exactly the expected rank.

- **Phase 0, item 5 — Global feature kill switches (2026-07-20):** new `lib/features.ts` — 4 env-var flags (`FEATURE_SEARCH_ENABLED`, `FEATURE_DOCUMENT_GENERATION_ENABLED`, `FEATURE_COMPANY_RESEARCH_ENABLED`, `FEATURE_RESUME_EXTRACT_ENABLED`), default-on so omitting them changes nothing today. Flip one to `false` on Vercel and that feature stops spending within seconds — no redeploy. Checked first, before any DB/AI work, at all 6 spend points. **Verified** the enable/disable logic directly (unset/true → enabled, `"false"`/`"0"` → disabled).

- **Phase 0, item 6 — Rate limiting (2026-07-20):** new `rate_limit` table (RLS-protected) + `lib/rateLimit.ts`'s `checkRateLimit()` — a DB-backed fixed window (6 requests/60s per user per route), admins bypassed. This isn't just abuse protection — it closes a real race in item 3's metering: that daily counter is read-then-write, not atomic, so a burst of truly parallel requests could all read the same under-limit count before any write lands, each passing the check. A tight window here makes that burst impractical in practice. Wired into the same 6 spend points, checked right after the kill-switch gate. **Verified live:** a real DB test hit the exact cap (6 allowed, 7th+ blocked) and confirmed a backdated `window_start` correctly resets the window.

App name is now **Sortie** everywhere user-visible in code (wordmark, page title, login card). v2 roadmap (career-ops feature port + custom additions) lives in `build-plan.md` under "v2 — Career-Ops Roadmap". All 5 Phase 6 items (17-21) are done, all open decisions resolved, and the rebrand/GUI pass that was blocking Phase 7 is now also done. Nothing blocking Phase 8 onward.

- **Phase 9 — 10-Dimension AI Evaluator (2026-07-20)** — full detail in `build-plan.md`'s Phase 9 entry (now marked done). Summary:
  - `lib/evaluator.ts` rewritten: grades each job A-F across the 10 spec'd dimensions (skills, seniority, compensation, location, domain, growth, culture, visa, effort-to-value, legitimacy) with a one-line note per dimension, an overall letter grade, and a 1-5 recommendation score. Routes through `lib/models.ts`'s `getModel`/`complete` (Phase 7). `match_score = round(recommendationScore * 20)`, preserving the existing 0-100 sort/filter UI and `MATCH_THRESHOLD = 70` cutoff.
  - **Real bug fixed, not part of the original ask:** the old evaluator graded every job against a candidate bio hardcoded directly in the code — never the user's real saved profile. Several new dimensions (compensation, visa, location fit) are meaningless without real `salary_expectation`/`work_authorization`/`remote_preference`/`location` fields the fake bio never had. Fixed by having `lib/inngest/functions.ts`'s `evaluateJobsAsync` fetch the real `profiles` row for the run's `userId` and pass it through, using `profile.preferred_model` to pick the provider — same pattern as every other AI call site since Phase 7.
  - **Second real bug, caught live during verification, same pass:** the new candidate-context builder itself omitted `profile.location` — every job's Location/remote-fit note came back "candidate location is not specified" regardless of the real value, because the model genuinely wasn't given it. Not caught by code review, only by reading the model's actual output during testing. Fixed and re-verified: a job requiring Colombia residency correctly dropped to a hard F once the model could see the candidate is Toronto-based (previously it hedged with partial credit).
  - Zod-validated response schema with a per-job fallback (`fallbackEvaluation()` — neutral C-grade placeholder) so one malformed entry in a 10-job batch can't crash the whole chunk, matching this codebase's existing fallback pattern in `agent/research.ts`.
  - New migration `20260720030000_add-jobs-evaluation-columns.sql` — `jobs.evaluation` jsonb, `jobs.recommendation_score` numeric — applied live via `db query`, verified via `information_schema.columns`.
  - `MatchScore.tsx` gains the 10-dimension breakdown (grade badges: A/B success-green, C neutral, D/F error-red), a legitimacy warning callout when that dimension grades D/F, and a separate "below recommended threshold" callout when `recommendation_score < 4.0` — both additive, existing sections unchanged.
  - **Verified live, not just compiled:** ran the real `evaluateJobCompatibility()` against the real saved profile and two real scraped jobs, once per `gemini` and `anthropic` — both produced grounded, correctly differentiated grades (Anthropic additionally caught a React-vs-Angular mismatch Gemini's run missed). `MatchScore` UI verified separately via a temporary route with real captured evaluation output plus a synthetic D/F legitimacy case (no real test job happened to grade poorly there). `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean; all temp verification files removed after.
  - **Not yet verified live in-browser end-to-end** — same login limitation as everything else in this project. The evaluator function and the UI are each independently verified live; what's not yet confirmed is a real Find Jobs search triggering the full pipeline (`jobs/evaluate` event → Inngest → DB → page render). Ask to be tested: run a new search (needs the separate `inngest-cli dev` process — see the Known Environment Gotcha in `RESUME.md`), then open a job details page and confirm the 10-dimension breakdown renders with real grades.

- **Phase 7 — Model Router (2026-07-19)** — full detail in `build-plan.md`'s Phase 7 entry (now marked done). Summary:
  - **Architecture decision, asked before building (per the spec's explicit "don't silently pick one, ask"):** raw provider clients, not the Vercel AI SDK. `lib/models.ts`'s `getModel()` returns an `openai` npm client (baseURL-overridden for Gemini) for `gemini`/`openai`, and the official `@anthropic-ai/sdk` client for `anthropic` — the loaded Claude API skill explicitly requires the official SDK for Claude and forbids OpenAI-compatible shims, which ruled out routing all three providers through one raw-OpenAI-client shape. `complete(handle, args)` normalizes the two client shapes (OpenAI `chat.completions.create` vs. Anthropic `messages.create`) so call sites don't branch on provider.
  - Tier mapping: `gemini` fast=smart=`gemini-3.1-flash-lite` (the only Gemini model actually proven working here — no second tier guessed); `openai` fast=`gpt-4o-mini`/smart=`gpt-4o`; `anthropic` fast=`claude-haiku-4-5`/smart=`claude-sonnet-5`.
  - **Four call sites migrated**, not the three originally named: `agent/research.ts` (dossier synthesis), `agent/documents.ts` (all four resume/cover-letter functions), `actions/profile.ts` (`extractProfile`), and `app/api/resume/generate/route.tsx` (Feature 08's profile-level resume generator — same hardcoded pattern, found mid-migration, included for consistency so the selector actually covers it). Each API route now reads `profile.preferred_model` fresh per request, defaulting to `"gemini"` for existing users.
  - New migration `20260719060900_add-profiles-preferred-model.sql` adds `profiles.preferred_model` (nullable `text`, `CHECK` constrained to the three provider values) — **applied live via `db query`, not `migrations up`**, because this project's migration-tracking bookkeeping is untracked for the `profiles` table (same pre-existing "migration file exists, remote history doesn't know about it" pattern documented elsewhere in this file — `db migrations list` returned zero applied migrations despite the table having years of real schema).
  - New `components/shared/ModelSelector.tsx` + `actions/profile.ts`'s `setPreferredModel()` server action, wired into the job details page above Company Research/Document Generator (one selector per page, not one per AI card, since every AI route reads the same DB column fresh).
  - **Scope cut, flagged not silently assumed:** the selector is **not** on the Find Jobs search bar as Feature 23's original spec called for — that search bar's scoring goes through `lib/evaluator.ts`, which was deliberately left unmigrated (see below), so a selector there would control a preference with no real effect yet. Revisit once Phase 9 ships.

Additionally ran the actual migrated `generateCoverLetter()` against a real profile row for `gemini` and `anthropic`, both producing genuinely tailored letters end-to-end. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean.

Ask to be tested: switch the selector, then generate/research/revise and confirm a visibly different writing style between providers.

Investigated live against your real profile/DB data: the router itself is working correctly (`profiles.preferred_model` was genuinely `"anthropic"`, and re-running `generateCoverLetter()` against your real profile/job for both providers produced clearly different letters — different hook, structure, tone). The two PDFs you compared most likely just predated the provider switch (every row in `applications` still showed `ai_model_used: "gemini-3.1-flash-lite"` at the time). Also design will never differ between providers by design — `ResumePDF.tsx`/`CoverLetterPDF.tsx` are fixed templates; only the AI-written text content varies.
    - **Found and fixed a real, unrelated bug during this check:** `lib/documentPersistence.ts` hardcoded `ai_model_used: "gemini-3.1-flash-lite"` on every save regardless of which provider actually ran, making that tracking column meaningless. Now passes the real `getModel(provider, "smart").model` through from both `app/api/documents/generate/route.ts` and `app/api/documents/chat/route.ts`.

Both also opened with the same tired resume clichés ("results-oriented," "proven track record"). Root-caused to weak prompt guidance, not a provider limitation — tightened the system prompts in `agent/documents.ts`'s `generateTailoredResume` and `app/api/resume/generate/route.tsx` to ban clichéd openers, require varied action verbs, cap bullets to ~15-22 words, require real-data-only quantified impact, restrict to ATS-safe standard characters, and explicitly target one-page fit. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.
    - Initially misread the older `pdf-parse@1.1.1`'s parse failure on the Gemini PDF ("Illegal character: 41") as a real corruption bug — cross-checked with a modern `pdfjs-dist` parser and confirmed both PDFs extract cleanly; it was an artifact of the old bundled parser, not a defect in the generated PDF. Not a false alarm worth chasing further, but worth knowing `pdf-parse@1.1.1` (used in `actions/profile.ts`'s resume extraction) can misreport perfectly valid PDFs as broken.

- **Resume/cover-letter theme selection (2026-07-19, same session)** — you pushed back further: wanted each AI model to have real influence over the resume's visual design ("theme"), not just its text, and didn't want a rigid blueprint dictating that design. Investigated the tradeoff before building: true AI-generated visual design would mean the model outputs HTML/CSS rendered to PDF via a headless browser (technically feasible — Playwright/Browserbase is already in this stack), but that directly conflicts with the project's ATS-safety guarantee, since free-form AI layouts risk tables/columns/images that break ATS parsing. Flagged this tension and asked rather than guessing; you resolved it by choosing user-selected themes instead of AI-generated ones — sidesteps the conflict entirely since every theme is pre-vetted ATS-safe.
  - `app/api/resume/generate/ResumePDF.tsx` refactored from hardcoded style constants to a theme-token system: `RESUME_THEMES` (`modern`/`classic`/`minimal`) each define color/font tokens, consumed by a `createStyles(tokens)` function. All three stay strictly ATS-safe regardless of choice — single column, no tables/images, only the PDF standard-14 fonts (Helvetica/Times-Roman families, no embedding needed). `modern` is the existing amber/Helvetica design (now the default); `classic` is Times-Roman/navy with a plain skill list instead of chips (more traditional, for conservative industries); `minimal` is pure grayscale Helvetica with no header rule (zero visual risk).
  - `CoverLetterPDF.tsx` now imports the same `RESUME_THEMES` tokens so a chosen theme applies consistently across both documents in an application package, not just the resume.
  - New migration `20260719080000_add-profiles-preferred-resume-theme.sql` adds `profiles.preferred_resume_theme` (nullable `text`, `CHECK` constrained to the three theme values) — applied live via `db query`, verified via `information_schema.columns`, same pattern as `preferred_model`.
  - New `components/shared/ThemeSelector.tsx` + `actions/profile.ts`'s `setPreferredResumeTheme()`, rendered next to `ModelSelector` on the job details page. All three document routes (`/api/documents/generate`, `/api/documents/chat`, `/api/resume/generate`) now read `profile.preferred_resume_theme` fresh per request and thread it into both PDF components, defaulting to `"modern"` for existing users.
  - **Verified, not just compiled:** rendered all three themes for both `ResumePDF` and `CoverLetterPDF` via `renderToBuffer` with real sample content — all three succeeded with zero errors and produced distinct byte sizes (confirming genuinely different output, not silently falling back to one theme). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.
  - **Not yet verified live in-browser (the document-generation flow)** — same login limitation as the rest of this session. Ask to be tested: switch the theme selector, regenerate a resume, confirm the visual design actually changes (color, font, skill-list style) while staying single-column and plain-text.

- **Dropdown option text unreadable in dark mode (2026-07-19, same session)** — you shared a screenshot: opening either the new `ModelSelector` or `ThemeSelector` dropdown showed unselected options as washed-out, barely-legible light-gray text on a white popup background (only the highlighted/selected option was readable). Root cause: `app/globals.css`'s `:root` sets `color-scheme: light` but the `.dark` block never set `color-scheme: dark` — so even in dark mode, the browser rendered native `<select>` popups with light-scheme chrome while the app's own CSS still applied dark-mode (near-white) text color to the options, an unreadable combination.
  - Tried the standard fix first (`color-scheme: dark` added to `.dark` in `globals.css`) but verified live that Chromium in this environment doesn't fully dark-scheme the native option popup from that alone — text stayed near-white, popup background stayed light. Confirmed with a real before/after screenshot comparison (reproduced the exact bug, then tried the partial fix, both via a temporary test page).
  - Real fix: explicit `className="bg-popover text-popover-foreground"` (shadcn's dedicated overlay-surface token pair, already dark-mode aware) added directly to every `<option>` in `ModelSelector.tsx` and `ThemeSelector.tsx`. This guarantees contrast regardless of native popup quirks, since the color no longer depends on the browser correctly inferring scheme.
  - **Found the same bug in two more places while checking for it**: `ProfileForm.tsx`'s Experience Level dropdown and the Month/Year selects (work experience date pickers) had the identical unstyled-`<option>` issue — fixed with the app's own `bg-surface text-text-primary` token pair (matching what the `<select>` itself already uses, rather than mixing in shadcn's popover tokens for a component that otherwise uses the app's real design system). Confirmed via `grep` that these three files are the *only* places in the codebase using a native `<select>` — nothing else affected.
  - **Verified live, not just compiled:** rendered the real `ModelSelector`/`ThemeSelector` components (not a simulation) via a temporary Next.js route with `next dev`, forced dark mode, opened both dropdowns, and screenshotted — "OpenAI"/"Claude" and "Classic"/"Minimal" are now clearly legible white-on-dark. Temporary route and dev server removed after verification. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.

- **Dropdown selected/highlighted option was browser-default blue, not the app's amber accent (2026-07-19, same session, immediate follow-up)** — you flagged the native `<select>`'s blue highlight bar as inconsistent with the app's amber brand color. Tried the lightweight fix first (`accent-color: var(--color-accent)` on the `<select>`) and verified live it does nothing — confirmed via screenshot the highlight stayed native blue. Native `<select>` popups give essentially no CSS control over that highlight in Chromium, so a real popup component was the only fix.
  - New `components/ui/select.tsx` — a `Select`/`SelectTrigger`/`SelectValue`/`SelectContent`/`SelectItem` wrapper built on `@base-ui/react/select` (already an installed dependency, used elsewhere for `Button`/`Input` — no new package added). Highlighted/selected items use `data-[highlighted]:bg-accent data-[highlighted]:text-accent-foreground`, which resolves to the app's real amber tokens (`--color-accent`), not shadcn's separate grayscale `--accent`/`--accent-foreground` pair — confirmed by reading `globals.css` that Tailwind v4's `@theme` namespacing makes `bg-accent`/`text-accent-foreground` resolve only to the `--color-*`-prefixed (amber) tokens.
  - `ModelSelector.tsx` and `ThemeSelector.tsx` rewritten on top of this — native `<select>`/`<option>` markup fully replaced (the earlier dark-mode contrast fix on the native `<option>` elements is moot now, superseded by this rewrite). `ProfileForm.tsx`'s native selects were **not** touched — only these two selectors had the blue-highlight complaint, and converting form-field selects too would be unrelated scope creep.
  - **Verified live, not just compiled:** same temporary-route pattern as above — opened the Model dropdown and confirmed the selected item ("Gemini") renders with an amber background and a checkmark; hovered "OpenAI" and confirmed the highlight follows hover with the same amber, text staying legible in both states. Clicking an item triggered the real `setPreferredModel` server action end-to-end (confirmed indirectly: the unauthenticated test page correctly redirected to login, proving the real `requireUser()` call ran, not a mock). Temporary route, dev server, and stale `.next` type-cache all cleaned up afterward. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.

No hardcoded duplicate of the model strings — if `MODEL_IDS` changes, the label updates automatically.

- **`gemini-3.1-pro` requested, tested live, rejected (2026-07-19, same session, immediate follow-up)** — you asked to switch Gemini's `smart` tier to `gemini-3.1-pro`. All three are now fixed to match the mockup: `app/globals.css`'s `--font-sans`/`--font-mono` are system stacks again (no `next/font/google` import), a new `--font-display` token (`Avenir Next`/`Century Gothic`/system fallback) is used only for the wordmark and hero heading, `Logo.tsx` is uppercase with the tag and a new `variant="dark"|"light"` prop, and `Navbar.tsx` uses `bg-overlay` dark chrome globally (public and authenticated pages alike). `ui-tokens.md`, `ui-rules.md`, `ui-registry.md` updated to match — `ui-rules.md`'s Navbar section had also been carrying stale v1 purple values (`#7C5CFC`) since 2026-06-03 that were fixed in the same pass.

**Verified live in-browser** (2026-07-18) — and live verification caught two real bugs the diff alone wouldn't have shown: (1) Tailwind v4 doesn't auto-generate a `font-*` utility for a non-canonical theme key like `--font-display`, so the wordmark was silently falling back to Times New Roman until a hand-written `.font-display` class was added to `app/globals.css`; (2) the global `a { color: inherit }` rule in `globals.css` was unlayered CSS, which beats *any* layered Tailwind utility regardless of specificity under CSS cascade layers — so `text-surface` on the navbar's `Logo` link was losing to `color: inherit`, rendering the wordmark invisible (dark ink text on the dark ink navbar). Fixed by wrapping `a`/`button,input,textarea,select`/`::selection` in `@layer base` in `globals.css`. Both are now documented as invariants in `ui-tokens.md`. Confirmed via computed-style checks and a screenshot: navbar wordmark and tag are white and correctly faced, footer wordmark (light-surface variant) reads dark ink correctly, zero console errors.

- **Light/dark theme toggle (2026-07-18)** — after the rebrand correction above, you pointed out the app still looked "black and white" outside the navbar/hero chrome (Dashboard, Profile, Find Jobs cards all plain white). That was actually faithful to the approved mockup (which only ever specified dark *chrome*, not a full dark app) but wasn't what you wanted, so a real toggle was built instead of either option, sequenced ahead of Phase 7 at your request. `next-themes` added; every `--color-*` token got a `.dark { }` override in `app/globals.css`, sourced from the mockup's own dark palette where it maps 1:1; new `ThemeToggle.tsx` client component rendered in `Navbar.tsx`. Full design writeup and dark-value table: `context/ui-tokens.md`'s "Dark Mode" section.
  - **A second, more serious pre-existing bug was found and fixed during this work, unrelated to dark mode itself:** `--color-accent`, `--color-background`, `--color-border`, `--font-sans`, and `--radius-sm` through `--radius-xl` had been silently resolving to shadcn scaffold defaults (generic oklch grays, a fallback font) instead of this app's real design tokens, since the original rebrand — the diamond wordmark mark, "Start for free" button, and every `border-border` usage across the entire app were never actually amber/paper/the-real-border-color, just close enough in light mode (pale neutrals both ways) to go unnoticed. Root cause: a leftover shadcn `@theme inline { }` block in `globals.css` redeclared those same theme keys, and Tailwind v4 silently keeps only the `@theme inline` version when a key exists in both a plain `@theme` block and an `@theme inline` block — not a normal CSS cascade, no way to out-specificity it. Caught immediately once dark mode made the wrong colors visually obvious (accent went pale gray, background stayed white). Fixed by removing the colliding key redeclarations from `@theme inline`, verified live via direct `getComputedStyle` checks in both themes. This had been live and shipped since the rebrand pass, undetected — see `ui-tokens.md`'s new invariant on never re-declaring a key across both `@theme` blocks.
  - Also removed leftover shadcn `dark:*` utility classes from `components/ui/button.tsx` and `components/ui/input.tsx` (referencing shadcn's own raw tokens, not this app's system) — inert until dark mode shipped, would have leaked uncoordinated styling into `Input`/`Button` the moment `.dark` activated.

- **Company Research + Apply button fixes (2026-07-18)** — you reported "Failed to load job" on Company Research and a non-working Apply button.
  - Root cause of Company Research: the live `jobs` table was missing `company_research`, `responsibilities`, `requirements`, `nice_to_have`, `benefits` — all defined in the original `migrations/20260603114535_create-jobs.sql` but never actually applied (same "migration file exists, live DB diverged" pattern as the earlier `agent_logs` incident — the live table was clearly built through some other undocumented path). New migration `20260718190000_add-missing-job-columns.sql` adds exactly the columns current code reads; applied live and verified via `information_schema.columns`.
  - Second bug in the same flow, would still have failed after the schema fix: `agent/research.ts`'s dossier synthesis called OpenAI's real API with `OPENAI_API_KEY` — but that env var is actually the Gemini key under a misleading name (confirmed: identical value to `GEMINI_API_KEY`; the correct pattern already exists in `actions/profile.ts`, which routes the same key through Google's OpenAI-compatibility endpoint). Fixed both the dossier-synthesis OpenAI client and Stagehand's own model config (was `openai/gpt-4o` with the same bad key) to use `GEMINI_API_KEY` — synthesis via the Gemini compat endpoint, Stagehand's page-extraction model via its native `google/gemini-3.1-flash-lite` provider support. No Tavily integration exists anywhere in the codebase yet — Company Research uses Browserbase/Stagehand for site browsing, not search-API-based research; that's still a real open question for later (Tavily is separately planned for Phase 10 "Deep Research", a different, bigger feature).
  - Apply button (`JobActions.tsx`) was already correctly coded as an external link — it wasn't broken, but silently fell back to an internal `/find-jobs` link when a job had no `external_apply_url`/`source_url` saved, which looked identical to "nothing happens." Now shows a disabled "No application link available" state instead of a fake link in that case.
- **Resume/cover-letter generation (2026-07-18, same session)** — Phase 11 Feature 30, built ahead of its numbered place right after the Company Research fixes above, since it depends on that dossier as its "deep research" input. Full detail in `build-plan.md`'s Feature 30 entry. Summary: new `agent/documents.ts` (Gemini-via-OpenAI-compat, same fixed pattern as research.ts), new `POST /api/documents/generate` (auto-runs Company Research first if missing) and `GET /api/documents/download`, new `components/job-details/DocumentGenerator.tsx` card on the job details page. Reuses the existing `applications` table rather than a new `documents` table.
  - **Found and fixed the same day: `applications` had RLS disabled and zero policies**, despite existing since the original schema — a real gap that would have let any authenticated user read/write any other user's generated resumes/cover letters via the API once this feature started writing real data to it. Closed with the standard four-policy own-row pattern (`applications_select_own` etc.) before any writes happened. New migration `20260718191500_prepare-applications-for-document-generation.sql` also added `resume_pdf_url`/`cover_letter_pdf_url` columns; applied live, verified via `pg_class.relrowsecurity` and `pg_policies`.
  - Scope cuts from the original spec, both deliberate: no cover-letter angle-picker UI (model picks its own angle), no Phase 7 model router (hardcoded to the same Gemini pattern already fixed elsewhere this session — a one-place swap once Phase 7 exists).
  - **Not yet verified live** — job details pages require a real login, which I can't perform. Ask to be tested: both Generate buttons on a real job, confirm PDFs open and the content is genuinely job-tailored (not identical to the generic profile resume from Feature 08), and that a page reload still shows the "View" links.

- **AI chat refinement for generated documents (2026-07-18, same session)** — Phase 11 Feature 32, built as the natural follow-on to Feature 30 right after it shipped. Full detail in `build-plan.md`'s Feature 32 entry. Summary: a conversational feedback loop (not the original spec's highlight-to-edit streaming) — `agent/documents.ts` gained `reviseTailoredResume`/`reviseCoverLetter`, new `POST /api/documents/chat` (re-fetches current content server-side, never trusts the client), new `components/job-details/DocumentChatEditor.tsx` nested inside each `DocumentGenerator.tsx` panel. Chat history is in-memory only, not persisted. Persistence logic shared with Feature 30 via new `lib/documentPersistence.ts` (extracted during this work to avoid duplicating the render/upload/upsert block across both routes).
  - Two of the three scope calls (no highlight-to-edit, in-memory-only chat) were made on the recommended defaults from a clarifying question that went unanswered — flagged clearly, not assumed silently. Revisit if that's not what was wanted.
  - **Not yet verified live**, same reason as Feature 30. Ask to be tested: generate a resume, send a revision instruction, confirm the reply appears and the "View" PDF actually changed, then send a second instruction and confirm it builds on the first revision rather than reverting to the original.

- **Resume PDF visual redesign (2026-07-18, same session)** — you reported the generated resume looked "very basic," wanted professional and attractive. Redesigned `ResumePDF.tsx` (`app/api/resume/generate/ResumePDF.tsx`, shared by both the profile-level generator and the tailored Feature 30 one): accent-colored section headings with rule dividers, a name/header accent bar, bordered skill "chip" tags instead of a plain comma list, company name in accent color, right-aligned uppercase date ranges, tighter bullet/spacing rhythm. Colors are hardcoded hex (not Tailwind tokens — react-pdf styles are plain JS objects, can't read CSS variables) but deliberately echo the app's real `--color-accent`/`--color-accent-dark` values for brand consistency. Stayed within ATS-safe bounds throughout — still single-column, plain extractable text, no tables/images/multi-column layout, just `Helvetica`/`Helvetica-Bold`.
  - **Rendered and confirmed working** via a standalone `tsx` script with sample data (`renderToBuffer` directly, bypassing the app) — produced a valid PDF, `tsc`/`next build` both clean. **Could not visually screenshot it myself** — this session's PDF-viewer tool has no allowed local directories configured, and no system PDF-to-image tool (poppler/ImageMagick) is available in this environment to work around it. The design is verified structurally (renders without error) but not visually by me — please look at the actual output next time you generate one.

- **Apply link fallback bug (2026-07-18, same session)** — "No application link available" and a missing "View Job Post" link, reported live on a real job. Root cause: `external_apply_url`/`source_url` (the columns `app/find-jobs/[id]/page.tsx` and `JobInfo.tsx` read) are never actually written by the scraper — `lib/actions/scraper.actions.ts:67` writes the real link to a separate `url` column instead (itself not part of the original tracked migration, another undocumented-schema-path column, same pattern as `company_research` earlier this session). Verified live via direct DB query against a real saved job: `source_url`/`external_apply_url` were null, `url` had the real link. Fixed by adding `url` as a third fallback in both places, and added `url: string | null` to the `Job` type (`types/index.ts`) since it was missing entirely. `JobInfo.tsx`'s "View Job Post" link also had the same silent-fallback-to-`/find-jobs` anti-pattern as the old Apply button — replaced with the same honest disabled state (`JobActions.tsx` already used) for the genuine no-link-at-all case.
  - **Not fixed, and worth flagging:** the `Job` type (`types/index.ts`) still declares several fields as required that don't exist in the live table at all — `cover_letter`, `tailored_resume_url`, `tailored_match_score`, `is_tailored`, `about_company` — leftover from the same original, never-fully-applied migration. These appear unused by the actual document-generation feature (which uses the separate `applications` table instead — see Feature 30/31/32 entries), but the type itself is still misleading. Worth a full reconciliation pass against the real schema at some point; out of scope for this specific bug fix.

- **Real portal apply links, not Google Jobs redirects (2026-07-18, same session)** — the `url` fallback above was still a `google.com/search?...` Google Jobs deep link, not an actual employer application page, since `lib/jobScraper.ts` only ever captured SerpApi's `share_link` field. You wanted the real job portal link instead. Fixed at the source: `lib/jobScraper.ts` now also captures SerpApi's `apply_options` array (real destinations — the employer's own ATS posting when SerpApi provides one, plus aggregators like LinkedIn/Indeed) and picks the first non-aggregator link via a new `pickApplyUrl()` helper, falling back to the first option if every one is a known aggregator host. `lib/actions/scraper.actions.ts` now writes that into `external_apply_url` — the column that was always in the schema for exactly this purpose but the scraper never actually populated. No change needed to the read side (`page.tsx`/`JobInfo.tsx`) — the fallback chain already checks `external_apply_url` first, ahead of `url`.
  - **Only applies to newly-scraped jobs.** Existing saved jobs (like the Seequent one from the bug report above) were scraped before this fix and only have the `url` column populated — they'll keep showing the Google Jobs link via that fallback until re-scraped. No backfill was run since there's no way to recover `apply_options` for already-saved jobs (SerpApi doesn't return it outside a live search).

- **"Back to Jobs" reset the whole page instead of returning to your last search (2026-07-18, same session)** — three-round debugging exchange. First guess (loading.tsx for the route transition) and second guess (a real, separate bug: the match-score poll in `FindJobsForm.tsx` was replacing the entire `jobs` array with just the small unscored subset it polled, discarding every other already-scored job — fixed by merging instead of replacing, plus added a missing stop condition) both landed real fixes but didn't address what you meant. Root cause, confirmed once you described the actual symptom ("comes to default state, form clear, default jobs listing"): `app/find-jobs/page.tsx` always showed the search form blank and the user's entire saved-job history (up to 100 jobs) — by design, from Phase 6 item 21 ("server-loads existing jobs on mount so back-navigation doesn't lose your last search") — but that's not the same as showing *the last search specifically*, which is what you wanted.
  - Real fix needed one more layer: `jobs.run_id` (a column that's existed since the original schema, meant to link a job to the search that found it) was **never actually set anywhere in the pipeline** — `lib/actions/scraper.actions.ts`'s insert never wrote it. Fixed by adding `run_id: runId` to the insert. `app/find-jobs/page.tsx` now scopes `initialJobs` to `run_id = lastRun.id` (the most recent completed `agent_runs` row) instead of an unscoped `.limit(100)`, and seeds the search form from that run's saved `job_title_searched`/`location_searched` instead of blank. Falls back to the old unscoped-history behavior only if the scoped query returns nothing (a brand new user, or jobs saved before this fix).
  - **Transitional caveat, worth knowing before the next test:** every job saved before this fix has `run_id = null`, so the very next "Back to Jobs" will hit that fallback and show the full history again (same as before) — it only starts showing "exactly your last search" once you run a **new** search after this fix ships.

- **First live test of Feature 32 (chat revision) — stale PDF after revision (2026-07-18, same session)** — you tested the chat editor for the first time, asked for a change, got a reply describing it, but the "View" PDF still showed the old version. Root cause: `app/api/documents/download/route.ts` always fetches fresh bytes from storage server-side (confirmed, not a server caching bug) but set no `Cache-Control` header — and the "View" link's URL (`/api/documents/download?jobId=X&kind=Y`) is byte-identical before and after a revision, so the **browser** was serving its own cached copy of the first-opened PDF instead of re-fetching. Fixed by adding `Cache-Control: no-store, must-revalidate` to the response. `tsc` clean; could not fully verify live (same login limitation as everything else this session) — please re-test: generate, view, send a revision, view again, confirm the new open actually shows the change this time.
  - **Also added:** you wanted explicit confirmation that a revision actually produced a new file, not just the AI's conversational reply text. `DocumentChatEditor.tsx` now shows a distinct green "New PDF generated with these changes — click 'View' above to see it" banner (`CheckCircle2` icon, `bg-success-lightest`/`text-success-foreground`) after each successful revision, separate from the chat bubbles — clears when the next message is sent or on error. Uncommitted along with the cache-control fix as of this note; commit when you're back.

- **Find Jobs card redesign (2026-07-18)** — you shared a screenshot of the approved mockup's job-card treatment and said you liked it better than the shipped `FindJobsForm.tsx` cards. Rebuilt to match: single-column list (was a 3-column grid), two-column card grid (`grid-cols-[1fr_auto]`) with title/company/location/tag pills on the left and a `font-mono text-2xl` tiered match score + "Match" sub-label on the right, small mono "Active targets — N" list label (was a larger heading with a `Building2` icon), and a real "Last sortie · Xm ago" stamp below the list (green dot + `formatTimeAgo()`, new helper in `lib/utils.ts`) sourced from the most recent completed `agent_runs` row for the user, fetched server-side in `app/find-jobs/page.tsx` and passed down as `lastRunAt`. The mockup's "Run a scan"/"Open dossier" buttons were deliberately **not** built — you chose to keep only the real timestamp rather than ship buttons with no clean, non-contrived action at the list level. Tag pills (`job_type`, a `Remote` tag inferred from `location`, up to 2 `matched_skills`) come from real job fields only via a new `jobTags()` helper, never fabricated placeholder tags.
  - While matching the mockup's Agent Read box fill, found the app-wide Agent Content pattern (`MatchScore.tsx` and now `FindJobsForm.tsx`) had been using `bg-agent-muted`/`text-agent-foreground` — a paler, app-only derived pair — instead of `bg-agent-light`/`text-agent-dark`, which is what actually maps 1:1 to the mockup's `radar-tint`/`radar-ink`. Corrected in both components and in the canonical pattern spec in `ui-tokens.md`/`ui-rules.md`. `CompanyResearch.tsx` still hasn't gotten the Agent Content treatment at all (pre-existing known gap) — use the corrected `bg-agent-light`/`text-agent-dark` pair when it does.
  - Verified live: toggle flips all computed `--color-*` values to the dark table in both directions, persists across reload via localStorage, no hydration warnings, navbar/logo/Find-Jobs-hero-panel content stays legible in both themes (the specific failure mode this feature could have introduced, since `bg-overlay` stays fixed dark in both themes and previously reused `--color-surface` for its content color). Dashboard/Profile/Job Details need your own spot-check — they require a real login this session can't perform, but should work automatically since they're 100% token-driven with zero component changes needed.

- Item 17: charts were already querying the DB directly instead of PostHog's API (found, not introduced) — kept as-is since PostHog isn't even configured (`NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_KEY` blank) and the DB data is equivalent with less complexity; flagged as a deviation from the literal v1 spec. What was actually missing — empty states for zero-data charts — is now added.
- Item 18: deleted the whole orphaned pipeline, including two files not on the original list (`JobsTable.tsx`, `/api/jobs/route.ts`) that became newly-orphaned once their only caller was removed. Full project type-check passes clean.
- Item 20: `agent_logs` table didn't actually exist in the live database (migration file present, never applied) — created and applied via a new tracked migration. Real `agent_runs` schema also differs from `architecture.md` (no `completed_at`; has `updated_at`/`error_message`/`total_time_ms`/`is_successful` instead) — code now matches the real schema, verified via a full simulated lifecycle.
- Found an existing, undocumented `applications` table that closely matches Phase 11's planned `documents` table — reuse it when building Phase 11 instead of creating a new one.

## Decisions Made During Build

- Homepage built as reusable App Router components: `Navbar`, `Hero`, `HowItWorks`, `Features`, `SuccessStory`, `CTASection`, and `Footer`.
- Landing page visuals rely on shared token-driven helpers in `app/globals.css` (`landing-panel`, `landing-grid`, `landing-hero-glow`, `landing-divider`) instead of component-level hardcoded color values.
- Landing CTA styling now uses `text-[var(--color-accent-foreground)]` on dark CTAs to guarantee readable contrast on all link/button states.
- Primary homepage CTAs currently point to `/login` until auth flow is implemented in Feature 02.
- Auth uses InsForge `@insforge/sdk` v1.3.1 with the SSR helpers from `@insforge/sdk/ssr`.
- OAuth starts through local route handlers at `/api/auth/oauth/google` and `/api/auth/oauth/github`; these store the PKCE verifier in an app-owned httpOnly cookie before redirecting to the provider.
- `/callback` completes the OAuth exchange server-side, sets InsForge auth cookies with `setAuthCookies`, then redirects incomplete or missing profiles to `/profile` and complete profiles to `/dashboard`.
- Next.js 16 route protection is implemented with root `proxy.ts`, not deprecated `middleware.ts`.
- PostHog browser initialization now runs from root `instrumentation-client.ts`, backed by `lib/posthog-client.ts`, and accepts either `NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_KEY` or PostHog's setup-screen `NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN`. `NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST` defaults to US Cloud when omitted.
- PostHog server tracking is centralized in `lib/posthog-server.ts` with a typed event contract limited to the seven approved JobPilot event names.
- Authenticated placeholder pages call `posthog.identify()` through `PostHogIdentify`, and current sign-out links call `posthog.reset()` before hitting `/api/auth/logout`.

---

## Notes

- The homepage uses the provided assets from `public/logo.png` and `public/images/` to match the approved design.
- Production build verification passed after allowing `next/font` to fetch the required Inter font outside the sandbox.
- Feature 02 lint and production build verification passed. Build still requires network access for `next/font` to fetch Inter.
- Feature 03 lint and production build verification passed. The first build attempt still failed on the known sandboxed `next/font` Inter fetch; rerunning with network access passed. PostHog will stay inactive until `NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_KEY` or `NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN` is present in `.env.local`.
- Feature 04: four tables (`profiles`, `agent_runs`, `jobs`, `agent_logs`) created via InsForge CLI migrations with full RLS (16 policies). Two Postgres triggers on `profiles`: `on_profile_updated` auto-sets `updated_at`; `on_auth_user_created` on `auth.users` auto-inserts a minimal profile row on signup. `resumes` private storage bucket created with path-scoped RLS on `storage.objects`. `types/index.ts` created with `Profile`, `WorkExperience`, `Education`, `AgentRun`, `Job`, `AgentLog` interfaces.
- Feature 06: `actions/profile.ts` created with `saveProfile` (text fields → `profiles` table, `is_complete` calculated via `calculateCompletion`, `profile_completed` PostHog event on first completion) and `uploadResume` (PDF → InsForge Storage at `{userId}/resume.pdf`, URL saved to `profiles.resume_pdf_url`). `lib/profile-utils.ts` created with shared `calculateCompletion` (9 required fields). `MissingField` union type moved to `types/index.ts`. `ProfileForm` now accepts `profile` prop, initialises all state from DB, has `<form onSubmit>` with `useTransition`, loading/error/success feedback, and `coverLetterTone` field added to Job Preferences. `ResumeSection` uploads on file selection via `uploadResume`. `ProfilePage` fetches full profile from DB and passes real data to all components. Three bugs fixed post-build: (1) `requireUser()` moved outside `try/catch` in both actions so `NEXT_REDIRECT` is never swallowed; (2) `parseInt` NaN guard added for years of experience; (3) `.select("id").maybeSingle()` added after `.update()` so zero-rows-updated is caught as an error instead of silent success. Profile row backfilled for existing user via `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`. `ProfileAttentionBanner` now returns `null` at 100% completion.
- Feature 08: `@react-pdf/renderer` installed. `app/api/resume/generate/route.tsx` (POST handler: auth → profile fetch → GPT-4o content generation → `renderToBuffer` → storage remove+upload → DB update → `revalidatePath`) and `app/api/resume/generate/ResumePDF.tsx` (server-only PDF component + `GeneratedContent` type) created. `ResumeSection.tsx` wired with `handleGenerate` handler, loading/error/success state, and `window.open('/api/resume/download')` on success. Route file is `.tsx` (JSX required for `renderToBuffer`). Buffer cast to `ArrayBuffer` before `new Blob()` to satisfy strict TS.
- Feature 07: `extractProfile` Server Action added to `actions/profile.ts`. Installs `pdf-parse@1.1.1` (v1) and `openai`. Flow: download resume from InsForge Storage → `pdf-parse` extracts text → GPT-4o with `response_format: json_object`, `temperature: 0.3`, `max_tokens: 800` → `ExtractedProfile` typed return. Empty-text guard returns user-friendly error. `ProfileForm` gains `ProfileFormHandle` ref type with `applyExtracted()` method via `useImperativeHandle`. `ResumeSection` gains `onExtracted` callback prop and an `Extract Profile` button (only visible when a resume exists). Thin `ProfilePageClient` client wrapper holds the `useRef<ProfileFormHandle>` and wires `ResumeSection.onExtracted → ProfileForm.applyExtracted`. `app/profile/page.tsx` now renders `ProfilePageClient` instead of the two components separately. Critical notes: (1) Must use `pdf-parse@1.1.1` (v1), NOT v2 — v2 uses pdfjs-dist v5 with ESM-only web workers that webpack cannot bundle in Server Actions. (2) Must import from `pdf-parse/lib/pdf-parse.js`, NOT the package index — the index.js has a debug block that reads a test PDF file on every `require()` call when `module.parent` is null (always true under Next.js/Turbopack), causing an ENOENT crash on page load.
- Feature 11: `GET /api/jobs` route created — accepts `search`, `matchFilter`, `sortOption`, `page` query params; runs server-side InsForge DB query with `.ilike` text search, `.gte`/`.lt` match score filters, `.order` sort, and `.range` pagination (20 per page); returns `{ jobs, totalCount, page, pageSize }`. `FindJobsClient` refactored: removed `useMemo`, added `fetchJobs` callback (called via `useEffect` on filter/sort/page changes and after a successful search); `initialTotalCount` prop added. `JobsTable` gains optional `isLoading` prop — dims table to `opacity-60` during fetch and skips empty-state when loading. `app/find-jobs/page.tsx` updated to fetch first page with `match_score` sort and pass `count` as `initialTotalCount`.
- Feature 10: `lib/adzuna.ts` — `searchJobs` HTTP helper. `app/api/agent/find/route.ts` — POST handler: auth → profile fetch → agent_run insert → Adzuna search → single GPT-4o batch scoring call → DB insert → agent_run update. `FindJobsClient` wired to `/api/agent/find`; after search, re-fetches from DB via `fetchJobs`. `jobs.source` corrected from `"linkedin"|"url"` to `"search"|"url"`.
- Feature 09: Find Jobs page built with mock data matching the design. `lib/utils.ts` created with `MATCH_THRESHOLD = 70`, `getMatchScoreColor`, `getMatchScoreTextColor`, `formatDate`, `formatSalary`. Components: `SearchControls` (job title + location inputs, Find Jobs button, success banner), `JobFilters` (text search + All Matches/High Match/Low Match dropdown + Match Score/Newest/Oldest sort), `JobsTable` (company icon, role, match score progress bar with score-range colors, salary, source badge, date), `JobsPagination` (showing X to Y of Z, Previous/page numbers/Next). `FindJobsClient` client wrapper owns all filter/sort/pagination state with `useMemo` for derived list. Filter and sort reset page to 1 on change. `Jobs by Adzuna` credit shown when jobs exist. Mock data uses 6 jobs matching the design exactly.
- Feature 05: full profile page UI built with mock data. Important caveat: Adzuna `description` is often a source-side preview, so the app cannot display text that was never returned/saved; users get a direct full-post fallback in those cases.
